# أدواتي (Adawati) — adawati.space

Free multi-tool Arabic website (calculators, converters, games, daily-info tools). Owner: Ahmed Amawi. This file exists so a new Claude Code session can resume work with zero ramp-up if this session is lost.

## Deployment — READ THIS FIRST

**There is no local git repo.** Do not run `git init`, `git commit`, etc. — they will not work as expected and are not how this project deploys.

Deployment is via a custom script that talks to the GitHub REST API directly:

```
node push-to-github.js "commit message here"
```

- Reads `GITHUB_PAT` from a local `.env` file (`GITHUB_PAT=...`, gitignored/excluded from the pushed tree).
- Repo: `adawati2027/my-tools-site`, branch `main`, pushed via blobs → tree → commit → ref update (not a real git working directory).
- **Always pushes every file in the project** (no diffing) — pushing after any edit is normal and expected, don't wait to "batch" unless GitHub's rate limit forces it.
- Site is served via GitHub Pages, custom domain `adawati.space` (CNAME on GitHub Pages auto-redirects `adawati2027.github.io/my-tools-site/` → `adawati.space/` with a real 301 — already verified working, don't re-verify).
- Has retry logic built in for transient network errors (`ECONNRESET` etc.) — exponential backoff, 4 attempts.
- After pushing, changes take roughly 30–90 seconds to actually go live; poll with `curl` + `grep` for a known string rather than assuming instant.

### GitHub API rate limiting (hits this OFTEN with ~260 files/push)

Two different things can fail with a 403:
- A short secondary/abuse-detection throttle — usually clears in 1–3 minutes.
- The real hourly quota (5000 req/hr) — tied to a fixed reset epoch.

**`GET /rate_limit` is unreliable** — it has repeatedly shown "5000/5000 fresh" moments before/after real failures this project. Do not trust it.

The correct way to get the real reset time: make one direct failing call (e.g. `POST /repos/adawati2027/my-tools-site/git/blobs`) and read the `X-RateLimit-Reset` response header from that actual call. Compute wait seconds from `epoch - now`, then use `ScheduleWakeup` for exactly that delay (capped at 3600s). Do not guess, do not poll in a tight loop, do not retry more than once without getting a fresh real reset time.

## Verification policy — the load-bearing rule of this whole project

**Never publish a legal/financial/religious fact without verifying it via WebSearch/WebFetch first.** This site has real calculators (taxes, zakat, customs, social security) that Jordanians and others actually rely on. If a fact can't be verified, or sources conflict, either:
- Say so transparently in the UI (a disclaimer, not a fabricated number), or
- Don't build that feature at all.

Concrete precedent: an income-tax calculator bug was reported where "extra expense exemption" was a flat 5,000 JOD for everyone. Investigation found the real law (Article 9, Income Tax Law 34/2014, as amended 2020+) breaks it down as 1,000 self + 1,000 spouse + up to 1,000/child (max 3 kids) = up to 5,000. A different official government page ("الجديد في أحكام القانون" on istd.gov.jo) shows totally different numbers (28,000 cap, 7/14/20% brackets) — that page turned out to describe the law's *original 2014 enactment*, superseded by later amendments; it's just an outdated government page, not current law. This took 5+ rounds of WebFetch on conflicting sources to resolve. **Government pages are not automatically authoritative over other corroborated sources — cross-reference multiple sources and reason about which is actually current before trusting any single one, including official ones.**

**This got re-flagged by external QA reviewers three separate times** (each citing the same superseded 7/14/20%/28,000 numbers) before being put to rest 2026-09-07 with a dedicated fresh re-verification fork: (1) istd.gov.jo's own laws listing explicitly confirms the law *currently in force* is "رقم 34 لسنة 2014 المعدّل بالقانون رقم 38 لسنة 2018", effective 1/1/2019 — direct proof the "الجديد في أحكام القانون" page describes the superseded original text, not current law; (2) a source quoting Article 9 directly; (3) PwC's Worldwide Tax Summaries for Jordan (tax-rates and deductions pages, last reviewed July 2026 — 2 months before this check) match the site's numbers exactly on both brackets (5/10/15/20/25/30%) and exemptions (9,000 + 9,000 + up to 5,000 = 23,000 cap). Three independent angles, one of them the regulator itself. **Do not re-investigate this from scratch again** if it resurfaces — point to this note and the on-page disclaimer (`jo/calculators/income-tax/index.html`) instead, unless the user has specific knowledge of a *new* legislative change (a real amendment, not a re-read of the same old istd.gov.jo page). One genuine gap found during this re-check — **added 2026-09-14**: a 1% "national contribution tax" applies to income over 200,000 JOD/year (very high earners only), introduced by Law 38/2018, effective 1/1/2019. Re-verified independently before publishing (WebSearch + a fresh PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries fetch, still last-reviewed July 2026) rather than trusting this note alone. Added as a transparent disclosure — a new bullet in the "معلومات مهمة" card, a new FAQ `<details>` entry, and a matching JSON-LD `Question` — explicitly stating the calculator does **not** apply this surcharge automatically (not a correctness bug in the existing brackets, which only go up to 30% and were never meant to include this separate surcharge).

When genuinely unresolvable, say so in the page's own disclaimer text rather than picking a number with false confidence.

## Site structure

- Root-level tool pages (English-first, generic): `age-calculator.html`, `bmi-calculator.html`, etc. — ~29 tools.
- Language variants: `/ar/`, `/fr/`, `/es/`, `/de/`, `/ru/<tool>/`.
- Country verticals: `/om/` (Oman, deepest/original), `/ae/`, `/sa/`, `/us/`, `/uk/`, `/jo/` (Jordan — actively being built out, now the second-deepest vertical, ~20+ pages across `calculators/`, `students/`, `games/`, `tools/`, plus daily-info pages like `prayer-times/`, `weather/`, `fuel-prices/`, `holidays/`, `emergency-numbers/`, `gold-price/`).
- Shared `style.css`, shared `i18n.js` (site-wide translations, language switching, country auto-detection and the "📍 Recommended for X" / "✨ You might like" widgets on hub pages).
- `build-sitemap.js` — regenerate `sitemap.xml` after adding any new page (`node build-sitemap.js`), then add the new URL to the `countryTools` array inside it first.

### Standard template for a new `/jo/...` page
Copy `om/salary-calculator/index.html` as the reference. Must include: `<base href="https://adawati.space/">` in `<head>` (makes all relative links resolve from site root regardless of nesting depth), GA + AdSense script tags, JSON-LD (`BreadcrumbList` + `WebApplication` + `FAQPage`), shared CSS classes (`.card`, `.tool-page`, `.option-toggle`/`.option-btn`, `.result-box`, `.stat-grid`, `.row`), standard nav/footer.

## Firebase (quiz "friend challenge" feature)

Project: **`adawati-challenges`** (Firebase console, Spark/free plan), owned by the same Google account as Analytics/Search Console/AdSense (all already correctly configured for `adawati.space` — verified, don't redo that audit).

**Shared engine (built):** `jo/games/quiz-engine.js` holds all Firebase/challenge/leaderboard/UI logic. Each game's `index.html` just defines `const QUESTIONS = [...]` and `const QUIZ_CONFIG = {gameId, shareTitle, challengeText, resultText(score,total,pct), resultMessages:[{min,emoji,msg}]}` before loading the engine script — don't copy-paste engine logic into a page again. Firebase JS SDK loaded via compat build from `gstatic.com` CDN (not npm). Config object is inline — safe to be public (Firebase web API keys aren't secrets; access is controlled by Firestore security rules).

**Group-challenge model (current, changed from the original 1-on-1 design):** single-player mode has been removed entirely — visiting a game page with no `?challenge=` param shows only the "ابدأ تحدي" box, `#quizArea` stays `display:none` until a challenge is actually started. `MIN_PLAYERS = 2`, `MAX_PLAYERS = 20` (constants in quiz-engine.js) — the creator sees a live count of who has accepted (`acceptCountLabel`/`accepterNameSpan`/`creatorStartBtn` elements) and the "ابدأ اللعب" button stays disabled until at least 1 joiner has accepted; `joinChallenge()` and `showChallengeJoinIntro()` both block joining once `MAX_PLAYERS` is reached. Any number of joiners (not just one) can accept via the same link — the `accepts`/`players` collections already support N participants, the leaderboard renders all of them.

**Category selector (opt-in per game):** `QUIZ_CONFIG.categories` (array of `{key,label}`) turns on a category-picker UI (`#categorySelectArea`, rendered by `initCategorySelector()`) shown to the creator before they start a challenge; `pickQuestions()` filters `QUESTIONS` by `q.cat === selectedCategory` when set (special key `'mix'` = no filter). Currently only `songs-quiz` uses this (`ar` / `foreign` / `mix`, split at a hardcoded index boundary in the source questions since the original batch was written Arabic-then-foreign) — other games don't set `categories` and behave as before.

**Firestore collections** (`challenges/{challengeId}`):
- `challenges` — doc has `creatorName`, `questions` (locked array for this challenge, already category-filtered if applicable), `game`, `acceptedCount` (number, starts at 0, see atomic-join note below), `createdAt`.
- `challenges/{id}/players/{autoId}` — `name`, `score`, `total`, `timeMs`, `submittedAt`. Leaderboard sort: `orderBy('score','desc').orderBy('timeMs','asc')` — **requires a composite index**, already created (players: score desc, timeMs asc, scope Collection).
- `challenges/{id}/accepts/{autoId}` — `name`, `acceptedAt`. Written when a joiner clicks "قبول التحدي"; the creator listens on this via `onSnapshot` (shows a live count + names of everyone who's joined, not just the first).
- `challenges/{id}/starts/{autoId}` — `startedAt`. Written when the creator clicks "ابدأ اللعب"; every joiner listens on this via `onSnapshot` and starts playing at the same moment (full sync).

Security rules: public `read: true` everywhere, `create` allowed with field validation, `delete` always `false`. `update` on the `challenges` doc is allowed **only** for an atomic `acceptedCount` increment-by-exactly-1 (`request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys().hasOnly(['acceptedCount']) && request.resource.data.acceptedCount == resource.data.acceptedCount + 1`) — added to close a race condition where simultaneous joins past the old client-side-only `accepts.size()` check could theoretically exceed `MAX_PLAYERS`. `joinChallenge()` in quiz-engine.js now uses `db.runTransaction()` to read `acceptedCount`, abort with a `CHALLENGE_FULL` error if it would hit `MAX_PLAYERS`, and atomically increment it + create the `accepts` doc together. All other collections (`players`, `accepts`, `starts`) keep `update`/`delete: if false` (replay-prevention stays client-side via `localStorage`, see `getPlayedRecord`/`savePlayedRecord`). No server-side dedup of a single person playing twice from two browsers/devices — discussed with the user, accepted as a known limitation rather than building real auth for it. Rules edited via the Firebase console Monaco editor — see the gotcha note below; last edited 2026-09-07 to add the `acceptedCount` update rule.

### Editing Firestore Rules via the console (gotcha)
When typing into the Rules Monaco editor via browser automation, **it auto-inserts a matching closing brace for every `{` you type** — including path-param braces like `{challengeId}`, not just block braces. For a small single-block edit this usually produces exactly one extra stray `}` (fix: click Publish, read the line number from the "Unexpected '}'" error, delete that one line, Publish again). For a **full-file replace of a large multi-block ruleset** (done 2026-09-07 to add the `acceptedCount` rule), it produced 6-8 extra stray `}` at the end of the file, not just one — the "explode on Enter between an auto-closed pair" behavior duplicates against each manually-typed closing brace on its own line for every block-level `{`. What worked: select-all + delete + retype (a JS-clipboard-paste was tried first to bypass this entirely but `navigator.clipboard.writeText` hung/timed out in this environment), then compare the displayed line count against the expected line count of the source you meant to type, `Ctrl+End` + `Backspace` to trim the exact number of extra trailing lines (2 backspaces per extra line: one for the `}`, one for its newline), verify via reading the page text before publishing, then Publish and fix any single remaining stray via the line-number method as before. Don't try to fix this by eyeballing indentation in a screenshot — the visual indent can look cascading/wrong while the actual brace balance is fine (or vice versa); count `{` vs `}` instead.

### Adding a new game to the challenge system
The Firestore doc already has a `game` field for exactly this. To add a challenge mode to a different game (planned — see below), reuse the same `challenges`/`players`/`accepts`/`starts` collections with a different `game` value; don't create parallel collections per game.

### In-page English translation system (quiz-engine.js, built 2026-09-07)

The 14 game pages under `jo/games/` **do not load `i18n.js`** (confirmed via grep) — the site-wide translation system doesn't reach them. When asked to translate the game UI to English, loading `i18n.js` there was deliberately rejected: `i18n.js`'s `detectDefaultLang()` has a path-based rule that force-returns `'ar'` for anything under `/jo/`, which would override an English-preferring visitor's actual choice — architecturally wrong for this specific case. Instead `quiz-engine.js` has its own **self-contained** EN/AR system, independent of `i18n.js`:

- `quizLang` — detected once via `localStorage.getItem('lang') === 'en' ? 'en' : 'ar'`.
- `QT` object (~35 keys, ar+en, some as functions taking params) + `qt(key, ...args)` helper.
- `qGetHomeHref()` — local re-implementation of `i18n.js`'s country-hub lookup (`sessionStorage.getItem('adawati_country')` → `{OM:'om/',SA:'sa/',JO:'jo/',AE:'ae/',US:'us/',GB:'uk/'}`, default `'index.html'`) — this is what actually fixes the back-link (see the bug note above), not `data-i18n`.
- `applyQuizTranslations()` — called once at page init. Walks `[data-qt]` (textContent), `[data-qt-placeholder]` (placeholder attr), `[data-en]` (English-only override text, applied only when `quizLang==='en'`), and fixes `.page-header a`'s href/text directly.
- `qShareTitle()` / `qChallengeText()` / `qResultText(score,total,pct)` / `qResultMessages()` — read `QUIZ_CONFIG.shareTitleEn`/`challengeTextEn`/`resultTextEn`/`resultMessagesEn` when `quizLang==='en'` (added to all 14 pages' `QUIZ_CONFIG` 2026-09-07), else fall back to the original Arabic fields. Wired into `shareResult()`, `shareOrCopy()`, `showResult()`.
- Scope, deliberately: **English only**, not all 6 site languages (explicit user choice when asked). The **question text itself stays Arabic-only** — an `#quizArabicNote` element (empty div under `progressLabel` on all 14 pages) shows `qt('arabic_note')` ("(questions are in Arabic)") only in English mode, so this isn't mistaken for a bug. Page `dir` stays `rtl` always, even in English mode — text-only translation, not a bidi layout rebuild.
- Hub-card labels/descriptions for these 14 games on `ae/index.html`/`us/index.html`/`uk/index.html` (the English-language country pages) — this was the original complaint that started the whole translation project — were translated to English 2026-09-07 (13 game cards + the "🏆 تحديات" tab label → "🏆 Challenges", plus the arrow given `data-i18n="start"` matching every other card on those pages instead of hardcoded "ابدأ →"). `jordan-quiz` isn't on these pages at all (excluded from non-Jordan country hubs per explicit instruction), so it needed no hub-card translation. `om/index.html`/`sa/index.html` stay Arabic-only by design (those are Arabic-first pages, `lang="ar"`).

When adding a 15th game or editing an existing one, any new page-level string needs: an ar/en pair added to `QT` in `quiz-engine.js` (or a `*En` field on that page's `QUIZ_CONFIG` if it's per-game content like `shareTitle`), a `data-qt="key"` (or `data-qt-placeholder`) attribute on the HTML element, and — if it's the h1 — a `data-en="..."` attribute directly on the tag instead (h1 has no wrapper element to hang a generic key off cleanly, so it uses its own override mechanism).

Original spec was ≥300 questions/game, but in practice each category's real, WebSearch-verified fact pool runs out well before 300 — smaller well-verified batches (60-160 range) have been explicitly accepted as fine rather than padding with unverified filler. No difficulty tiers (tried and reverted — see that section), flat random pool of 12/round, growing toward ~400 with a bias toward car brands and (per later user steering) harder/more obscure logos.

**All 14 [now 15] games expanded in a second pass (2026-09-07)** — final counts: jordan-quiz 301, world-sports-quiz 180, world-football-quiz 99, songs-quiz 315 (70 ar / 245 foreign), cooking-quiz 116, makeup-quiz 161, math-quiz 204, general-quiz 120, geography-quiz 251, islamic-history-quiz 113, science-quiz 185, inventions-quiz 155, animals-quiz 234, culture-quiz 211 — **total ≈2,850 questions**. Most categories plateaued well short of the original 300 stretch target once genuinely well-verified, non-overlapping facts ran out — this is expected and documented, not a shortfall to chase further without loosening the verification bar., all cross-linked to each other via the "أدوات الأردن الأخرى" section, all listed on `jo/index.html`'s hub (both the legacy games section and the tabbed challenges grid) and in `sitemap.xml`.

**Architecture (done):** shared `jo/games/quiz-engine.js` — see the Firebase section above. New games only need a page shell + `QUESTIONS`/`QUIZ_CONFIG`.

**Per-game build checklist** (the repeatable pattern used for games 5-14, all done via Node scripts in the scratchpad rather than hand-editing):
1. `mkdir jo/games/<slug>-quiz` and copy the most recently built game's `index.html` as a template (Node script: read file, `.split(oldSlug).join(newSlug)` for all URL/breadcrumb occurrences, replace title/meta description/keywords/og:description/card-icon emoji+bg color/h1/JSON-LD WebApplication description/challengeText).
2. Inject the verified question batch: replace the `const QUESTIONS = [...]` block (serialize with a single-quote-escaping helper, matching existing quoting convention) and the `const QUIZ_CONFIG = {...}` block (gameId, shareTitle, challengeText, resultText fn, resultMessages tiers).
3. Sync cross-links across **every** game page (not just the new one) — there's a reusable script for this pattern: build a `games` array of `{slug,label}`, for each page find its "أدوات الأردن الأخرى" links block and insert any missing `<a href="jo/games/OTHER/">` entries. Keep this script around and just append to its array for each new game rather than rewriting it.
4. Add a card to `jo/index.html` in **two** places: the legacy un-tabbed "🎮 ألعاب" section and the tabbed `data-cat="challenges"` grid.
5. Add the URL to `build-sitemap.js`'s `countryTools` array, run `node build-sitemap.js`.
6. Validate before pushing: `node --check` the engine, parse every page's inline `<script>` via `new Function(body)`, parse the JSON-LD via `JSON.parse`, and check the QUESTIONS array for 0 duplicates / 0 malformed entries (opts.length===4, correct in 0-3).
7. `node push-to-github.js "..."` — always pushes the full tree, so batch several games into one push rather than pushing after every single file when doing several in a row (GitHub's rate limit gets hit fast otherwise, see below).

### Logo quiz (`jo/games/logo-quiz/`) — image-based questions, built 2026-09-07

User explicitly requested real brand/car/restaurant logo images despite trademark/copyright risk (confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` — chose "شعارات حقيقية برغم المخاطرة" over a safer illustrated-icon alternative). Architecturally different from games 1-14 in two ways:

**Engine support (quiz-engine.js):** questions can now carry an optional `img` field. `pickQuestions()` copies it through into `activeQuestions`; `loadQuestion()` calls `getQuestionImageEl()` (same lazy-DOM-injection pattern as `getTimerEl()` — creates a `<img id="questionImage">` right before `#questionText` on first use, hidden by `display:none` when a question has no `img`) and sets/clears its `src`. Every other game's questions are unaffected (no `img` field → element stays hidden). Options (`opts`) are still plain brand-name strings.

**Logo sourcing (not WebSearch fact-checking — a different verification problem):** the questions aren't checkable facts, they're "does this image URL actually show this brand's real logo." The method that worked: query **Wikidata** (not raw Commons filename-guessing, which fails constantly — brand names rarely match their exact Commons file naming) for each brand's Q-id via `wbsearchentities`, then read its **P154 ("logo image") claim**, then build the image URL as `https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Special:FilePath/<urlencoded filename>` (a stable redirect-to-current-file endpoint, doesn't need the actual `upload.wikimedia.org` hash path) and verify with an HTTP HEAD (following redirects) for `200` + `image/*` content-type before accepting it.

**Two real gotchas hit during sourcing, both worth knowing if this pattern is reused:**
- **Wikidata rate-limits fast** under rapid sequential requests even with a compliant `User-Agent` — a first pass with only a 150ms gap between brands and no retry logic silently returned `no_wikidata_match` for ~65 very famous brands (Toyota, BMW, Coca-Cola, Starbucks, IKEA, Walmart, etc. — all of which do exist and resolve fine individually). Fixed by adding exponential-backoff retry (4 attempts, 800ms base) on 429/403/5xx to both the Wikidata and Commons calls, plus slowing the per-brand delay to 600ms. Don't trust an empty/no-match result from a fast unthrottled batch — it's very likely rate-limiting, not missing data.
- **A Wikidata entity's P154 property can carry multiple claims (historical/alternate logos), and the array order is NOT rank order.** Naively taking `claims[0]` picked wrong images for ~30 brands including real bugs, not just stylistically-outdated ones: **Toyota** initially resolved to a trademark-registration filing scan (`Registered trademark img 06.png`) instead of the actual oval logo, because that claim happened to sit before the `rank:"preferred"` one in the array. Fixed by explicitly preferring the claim with `rank === 'preferred'`, falling back to the first claim only if none is preferred. Even after that fix, **Wikidata's own "preferred" claim was occasionally still wrong** — Peugeot's preferred P154 claim points to a photo of a parked Peugeot 308 (`2021 - Peugeot 308 III (C) - 42.jpg`), not a logo at all, a genuine Wikidata data-quality issue, not a script bug; caught by eyeballing every resolved filename for the whole batch and manually re-pointing that one entry at Peugeot's `normal`-rank claim (the real logo file) instead. **Always spot-check the final filename list by eye before trusting it** — a HEAD check only proves the URL returns *an* image, not that it's the *right* image.
- Also caught: **wrong-entity matches**, not just wrong-image-on-right-entity — "Baskin-Robbins" and "Dunkin'" both initially resolved to the Q-id for "Dunkin' Brands" (their shared former parent holding company) instead of their own separate Wikidata entities, because the keyword-filtered search fallback picked the first description-matching candidate rather than the exact-name match. Both produce a *valid* image (so the automated HEAD check passes silently) but the *wrong* one, and duplicate-QID/duplicate-image-URL detection is what actually caught it — **always dedupe the final resolved set by both Wikidata Q-id and by image URL**, not just by input brand name, since two different brand names quietly resolving to the same underlying entity/image is exactly this bug and won't show up any other way.

**Final set (first pass, 2026-09-07):** 109 questions (60 tech/global brand, 35 car brand, 14 restaurant/fast-food), 0 duplicates, 0 malformed, every image URL HEAD-verified and the full filename list manually eyeballed. Room to grow later the same way the other 14 games did (well-verified batches over padding) — the `logo_source.js`/`build_logo_questions.js` scratchpad scripts (Wikidata-search + preferred-rank-aware P154 lookup + HEAD verify + question-array serializer) are the reusable pattern if more brands are added.

**Expanded to 464 questions (2026-09-08)**, adding a 4th category (airlines) on top of tech/cars/food: tech 289 (60 original + 229 new), cars 81 (35 + 46), food 66 (14 + 52), airlines 28 (new category). Sourced via a background `fork` running the same `logo_source.js` Wikidata pipeline against a much larger brand candidate list (~508 names across the 4 categories), 366 resolved successfully (72% hit rate — the rest failed on `no_logo_property` or `no_wikidata_match`, expected attrition, not a bug). **The fork repeatedly self-reported "completed" while actually just waiting on a background bash process it had spawned** (see the fork-notification lesson elsewhere in this file) — always check the actual output file (`logo_results_500.json` in the scratchpad) for real progress/a `Done: N / M resolved` line before trusting a fork's own completion summary, and resume it via `SendMessage` if the underlying script is still running.

**New failure mode found at this larger scale, beyond the 3 already documented above: a Wikidata entity's "preferred" P154 claim can be a plain photograph, not a rendered logo at all** — automated regex scanning of resolved filenames for photo-like patterns (`(12345678).jpg`-style Commons photo IDs, `night`/`building`/`exterior`/`fleet`/model-year patterns, `.jpg`/`.jpeg` extensions worth double-checking since real logos are almost always `.svg`/`.png`) caught 5 more bad entries the HEAD-check alone couldn't: **Royal Enfield** → a 1921 trade-magazine scan page, **Lotus Cars** → a photo of a parked Lotus Eletre, **Hooters**/**Hooters of America** (duplicate Q-id, same bug as Baskin-Robbins/Dunkin' before) → an unrelated restaurant-interior photo captioned "Supper Friday night", **Steak 'n Shake** → a nighttime photo of a physical restaurant building. All 5 excluded from the final set (see `BAD_NAMES` in the scratchpad `build_logo_500.js`). **Lesson: when sourcing at scale, always run an automated suspicious-filename regex pass (photo-ID patterns, building/exterior/fleet/night keywords, unexpected `.jpg` extensions) in addition to eyeballing — some bad matches are photographs, not just wrong-but-plausible logos, and a HEAD check can never catch that class of error since a photo URL returns a perfectly valid `200 image/*` response.** Also re-confirmed the earlier "same brand, different Wikidata entity" bug still needs a qid+url dedupe pass every time (caught 3 more collisions this round: Sprite/Sprite (drink), Nest Labs/Google Nest — harmless renames, kept one of each).

### Logo quiz — 2026-09-08 curation rewrite (464 → 139, still growing)

User reported two real problems with the 464-question set: (1) **most images had the brand's name spelled out as plain text inside the image** — trivial "read the text" rather than "recognize the logo," and (2) **images took 2-3 seconds to load** every question. Root-caused and fixed both, then kept growing the pool under a much stricter quality bar per follow-up user requests (target moved from "fix it" → 200 in 4 difficulty tiers → 200 in 2 tiers → difficulty removed entirely, flat pool, target ~400, bias toward harder/obscure logos and car brands). As of this note: **139 verified questions live** (95 tech/app, 32 cars, 8 airlines, 4 food), still short of the ~400 target — see "Realistic ceiling" below for why.

**Speed root cause:** every image URL was `https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Special:FilePath/<file>`, a Wikimedia-side redirect endpoint — measured at 1.8–2.4s per image due to 3 chained redirects before reaching the real `upload.wikimedia.org` file. Fix: resolve each URL's final redirect target once at build time, strip Wikimedia's `?utm_source=...` tracking query params (they were breaking naive `.svg` extension detection — always `url.split('?')[0]` before matching an extension), download the file, and **self-host it** under `jo/games/logo-quiz/assets/<slug>.png` — cut load time to <0.1s (same-origin, served by GitHub Pages/the site's own CDN) and removed the Wikimedia dependency from the hot path entirely.

**Text-in-image root cause:** Wikidata's P154 "logo image" claim usually points at a company's *official registered* logo, which for most companies is the full wordmark/lockup (e.g. "GOOGLE" spelled out), not the standalone icon casually used as an app icon or car badge. This is systemic, not a sourcing bug — re-auditing the original 464 found only ~44 were genuinely icon-only after a full manual visual review (a ~9.5% yield). **The quality bar applied, and why it survived several rounds of user clarification:**
- **EXCLUDE**: any image where the brand's answer-option text is spelled out in plain readable typography (the vast majority of pulls). Also exclude on sight: **wrong-entity/rebrand mismatches** found this round — a "Twitter" question resolving to the new X-bird logo, "WordPress" resolving to the Yoast plugin's logo, "Etisalat" resolving to the post-rebrand "e&" mark with "etisalat and" text baked in, "GMC" resolving to a *Greater Manchester Council* logo (!) — these are real Wikidata/Commons tagging errors, not just text-count issues, and only show up by actually looking at the image.
- **KEEP**: pure pictorial symbols with zero text (Apple, Mastercard, Mercedes-Benz star, Toyota rings, Audi rings, Nike swoosh, Target bullseye, Chevrolet bowtie, Rivian/Rimac shield marks), plus short 2-4 letter **stylized monograms/app-icon tiles** that don't spell the full multi-word answer (Adobe's "Ps"/"Ai"/"Pr"/"Ae"/"Au"/"Id"/"Xd"/"Ch"/"Br"/"An" app-icon convention; Microsoft Office's "W"/"X"/"P"/"N" tiles; Rolls-Royce "RR"; Suzuki/Honda-style single-letter badges; Netflix/Facebook/Bing single-letter icons). One deliberate exception: BMW's roundel has small peripheral "BMW" letters baked into the ring design but was kept anyway since the circular badge shape/colors are the actual recognizable element, not the tiny text.
- **Script that doesn't spell the Latin answer text is not a giveaway**: Emirates' Arabic calligraphy mark and PIA's Urdu calligraphy mark were kept (cropped to remove the accompanying Latin wordmark) since they don't read as "Emirates"/"PIA" to someone reading the Latin-script answer options — genuinely harder to identify, not easier.
- **Abbreviations that don't exactly equal the answer string are allowed** even if not fully abstract: "ANA" (for "All Nippon Airways"), "AZAL" (for "Azerbaijan Airlines"), "MG" (for "MG Motor") were kept — contrast with "HP", "UPS", "KLM", "NIO", "AWS" which were excluded because those abbreviations *are* the literal expected answer, so showing them is a 100% giveaway with zero brand-recognition required.
- **Crop don't discard**: many rejects have a clean icon-on-top/text-below (or icon-left/text-right) layout — `build_assets.js`'s `cropToPng(srcFile, cropRule, aspect, outPng)` renders the source at its true aspect ratio (NOT a square contain-fit — an earlier version of this function did that and barely cropped anything, since a wide image auto-fills a square box's width, leaving the "cropped" region still showing ~97% of the original; the fixed version fixes one axis to a constant size and computes the other from the real aspect ratio, then clips a container to the wanted fraction with the image positioned flush against the crop edge) and clips a `top`/`bottom`/`left`/`right` fraction. **Always re-render and visually verify a crop before trusting it** — first-guess percentages were wrong most of the time (Baskin-Robbins/Pizza Hut/Rolex/Vodafone/7-Eleven all needed 2-3 iterations to fully exclude residual text; Target/Philips/The North Face had their icon-vs-text layout assumption backwards on the first guess — Philips turned out to be text-on-top/icon-on-bottom, inverted from what the thumbnail suggested, and The North Face's icon was bottom-right with the rest of the image blank, not cleanly croppable at all, so it was dropped entirely rather than force a bad crop). 7-Eleven was also dropped after 3 crop attempts still left the "7" digit unrecognizable or the "ELEVEN" text partly visible — not every crop-looking case is actually recoverable within reasonable effort.

**Sourcing method, changed from the original Wikidata-P154-pull approach:** blind Wikidata pulls have the ~9.5% yield described above. Direct Commons filename-guessing has a much higher yield when you already suspect an icon-only file exists — `curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" -L -A "<UA>" "https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Special:FilePath/<guessed filename>.svg"`, trying 2-3 common patterns per brand (`<Brand>_logo.svg`, `<Brand>.svg`, `<Brand>_icon.svg`) — found the icon-only Mercedes-Benz star, BMW roundel, and Toyota oval-icon files this way after Wikidata's own P154 pull had returned their wordmark versions. Yield varies a lot by category: **tech/software app-icons are the best category by far** (Adobe's CC app suite, Google Workspace, Microsoft Office — all ~50-70% found-and-clean, since "app icon" is a real, common asset type companies actually publish separately from their wordmark); **cars are moderate** (~15-25% once you've covered the ~20 most famous marques — niche/regional/coachbuilder brands like Zagato, TVR, Lada, UAZ mostly don't have any simple-filename Commons SVG at all, found or not); **niche regional airlines are the worst yield** (found 6/40 in one batch) since most simply don't have a logo file on Commons under a guessable name.

**Practical ceiling on the ~400 target, worth knowing if this is picked up again**: this isn't a "keep grinding, it'll get there" situation. Growth per round follows a clear curve — tech app-icons stay productive far longer than any other category since there are hundreds of real software products with genuine icon-only branding; car and airline growth slows sharply after the ~30-40 most globally-recognizable marques are covered, because smaller/regional/defunct brands frequently have **no logo image on Commons at all**, not just a wordmark-only one — no amount of better guessing recovers a file that doesn't exist. Getting materially closer to 400 while holding this quality bar means either (a) grinding through many more small batches of tech app-icons (highest remaining yield, but increasingly obscure software), (b) accepting a Wikidata-search-driven (not filename-guess-driven) pass across a much larger brand candidate list the way the original 464 was built, re-applying the full visual-review+crop pipeline to the results, or (c) both. Don't reintroduce a difficulty-tier system without explicit new instruction — it was built, then explicitly reverted twice in the same session (see below) in favor of a single flat pool.

**Difficulty tiers — built, then explicitly reverted, don't rebuild without asking:** mid-session the user asked for 4 tiers (easy/medium/hard/very-hard) via a `QUIZ_CONFIG.difficulties` array mirroring the existing `categories` selector pattern (`initDifficultySelector()`/`selectedDifficulty` in `quiz-engine.js`, generalizing `pickQuestions()` to filter by both dimensions when set) — built and shipped once. Immediately revised to 2 tiers (medium/very-hard) — every question's `difficulty` field was mechanically collapsed (`easy→medium`, `hard→very-hard`) via a regex pass over the source-of-truth files rather than manually re-judging each entry. Then, before that 2-tier version even finished a push cycle, the user asked to **remove the difficulty selector entirely** — flat pool, `shuffle(pool).slice(0, 12)`, no filtering. `QUIZ_CONFIG.difficulties` and the `#difficultySelectArea` div were removed from `jo/games/logo-quiz/index.html`; the `difficulty` field was dropped from newly-built question objects (older entries may still carry a harmless unused field depending on which scratchpad build script generated them — not worth cleaning up retroactively). `initDifficultySelector()`/`selectedDifficulty`/the difficulty-filter branch in `pickQuestions()` were deliberately left in `quiz-engine.js` as dead code (same no-op-when-unset pattern already established for `categories` on games that don't use it) rather than ripped out — cheap to leave, and the pattern is already proven safe.

**Self-hosted asset pipeline, reusable for future rounds:** `jo/games/logo-quiz/assets/<slug>.png` — every file produced by rendering the (possibly cropped) source SVG through headless Chrome (`chrome.exe --headless --screenshot=...`) at a fixed intrinsic size, never relying on live Wikimedia URLs in the shipped question data. The scratchpad's round-by-round scripts (`round<N>/candidates.js` → `probe.js` → `download_batch.js` → `build_grid.js` (contact-sheet visual review, 24-per-page, 6 columns, labeled) → `final_list_r<N>.js` → `build_assets_r<N>.js` → merged into `build_merged_questions.js` → injected into the live HTML) are copy-paste-and-rename templates of each other by this point — grab the most recent round's copies rather than writing fresh ones. **Pace Commons requests at 600-700ms with retry/backoff** (established in the original 464-question pass, re-confirmed here) — one batch at 150ms silently got ~68% of downloads replaced with a "Wikimedia Error" HTML page saved under the expected filename instead of real image bytes; always sanity-check a downloaded file doesn't start with `<!DOCTYPE html` before trusting it's real.

**Verification workload & rate limits** (lessons from actually running this, useful if this pattern gets reused for another content push): background `fork` agents doing WebSearch-verified question generation, one category per fork, run well in parallel (9 were launched at once for games 6-14 in one session). Real constraints hit in practice:
- **WebSearch has a per-session budget** (200 calls) — once exhausted, forks fall back to WebFetch against specific pages (Wikipedia etc.), which still works but is slower per fact and is why later categories in a batch return fewer questions than earlier ones.
- **The account has an API rate limit that can trigger mid-fork** — first a short session-scoped one (resets within hours, a fixed daily clock time in Asia/Muscat), then (if enough forks run) a **weekly** one (resets a fixed day/time in Asia/Muscat, hit once this session) — a failed fork just needs `SendMessage` to its agentId telling it to resume; it picks up from where it left off. Don't loop retrying immediately — check the error message for the actual reset time/day and wait for it.
- A fork that returns 0 questions because WebSearch was exhausted **before any query ran** isn't a dead end — resuming it after telling it to fall back to WebFetch (like other forks in the batch did) works (this happened to the ثقافي fork, which got 138 questions on retry after initially returning empty).
- `push-to-github.js` itself hits GitHub's own separate rate limit constantly when pushing several games in sequence within a short window (each push sends the full ~270+ file tree) — this is unrelated to the Claude API limits above, see the rate-limiting section near the top of this file for how to handle it.

### Logo quiz — 2026-09-14 overnight expansion (139 → 212) + new football-clubs category

Ran as an unsupervised overnight `fork` per explicit user request ("زيد اسئلة تحدي اللوجو وارفع العدد لـ400 وضيف شعارات الأندية العالمية"). Two goals: keep growing the existing flat pool, and add a brand-new football-club-crests category. **Final: 212 questions (160 base + 48 football + 4 tech), well short of 400** — see "Realistic ceiling" note above, which held again this round; forcing 400 would have meant loosening the visual-review bar, which was avoided per explicit standing instruction not to do that without being asked.

**Football clubs — real yield story, not what was expected going in.** The brief assumed crests would be much higher-yield than tech logos since they're mostly graphical (true), but the *sourcing method* had to change entirely first:
- **The existing Wikidata P154 pipeline (`logo_source.js`) almost completely fails for football clubs** — tested on ~20 top clubs (Real Madrid, Chelsea, Arsenal, etc.), got ~10% success, all `no_logo_property`. Root cause confirmed by direct entity inspection: **football crests are near-universally non-free/fair-use images, and Wikidata (like Commons) can only attach freely-licensed media via P154** — so the property is simply empty for almost every club, not a search/matching bug.
- **Fix: read each club's English Wikipedia infobox wikitext directly** (`action=query&prop=revisions&rvprop=content`, regex the `{{Infobox football club}}` block for its `image` parameter), then resolve that filename against **Commons first, falling back to en.wikipedia.org's own `Special:FilePath`** for the (very common) case where the crest is a local non-free file that only exists on English Wikipedia, not Commons. This got the yield up to 122/143 candidates (~85%) — a completely different, higher-yield method than the one documented for every prior round of this feature, worth reusing as the *first* approach (not P154) if more clubs are ever added. Using non-free Wikipedia-hosted crests here is consistent with the original explicit user decision (`AskUserQuestion`, 2026-09-07) to accept trademark/copyright risk for this whole feature — not a new risk category.
- **Visual review caught a ~40% false-positive rate even after the sourcing fix** — of the 122 downloaded crests, roughly half had to be excluded after actually looking at them, for the *same* reasons documented in the 2026-09-08 rewrite (team name spelled in plain text being the big one) plus this round's own fresh examples: **Arsenal, Tottenham Hotspur, Ajax Amsterdam, Vissel Kobe, and Guangzhou FC all looked clean at 120px contact-sheet thumbnail resolution and turned out to have readable team-name text once zoomed to full size** — Arsenal's cursive "Arsenal" wordmark arcing over the cannon, Tottenham's "TOTTENHAM HOTSPUR" wordmark below the cockerel, "AJAX" worked into the artwork at top-left of the profile-head crest, "VISSEL" spelled in full under what looked like an abstract "V" mark, "GUANGZHOU" on the bottom ribbon. **This is the concrete reason the established practice mandates zooming every individual image rather than trusting a thumbnail grid** — a 120px thumbnail is not sufficient resolution to catch fine peripheral text, even though it's plenty to judge overall composition/color. Also caught two more entries matching the exact "photo, not a logo" bug documented in the original 464-question pass (Genesis Motor and Peugeot, both pulled as `.jpg` files during the *separate* tech/car expansion batch below — both were actual photos of the badge mounted on a car hood, with the name legible on the badge in the photo; the `.jpg`-extension-is-a-red-flag heuristic caught both before they were even zoomed, then zooming confirmed it).
- **Raja Casablanca and Étoile du Sahel were initially assumed safe because they looked Arabic-script-only in the thumbnail** (matching the established "non-Latin script isn't a giveaway" exception for Emirates/PIA) — zooming in showed **Raja's crest actually has "RAJA CLUB ATHLETIC" spelled in Latin letters too**, not Arabic-only, and Étoile du Sahel has small ring-text spelling the French name in full. **Lesson: don't assume "looks like it's in Arabic" from a thumbnail — some crests mix scripts, and the Latin half is exactly what needs to be checked for.**
- Final football set (48, self-hosted under `jo/games/logo-quiz/assets/`, question text `'شو اسم هالنادي؟'`, distractors drawn from the ~140-name candidate pool regardless of whether that name got an image): mostly Europe's biggest clubs (Real Madrid, Barcelona, Atletico Madrid, both Milan clubs, Inter, Dortmund, Bayern-adjacent Bundesliga clubs, Porto, Benfica, several more), Cyrillic-script Eastern European clubs kept under the non-Latin-script exception (Zenit, Dynamo Kyiv, Shakhtar, Red Star Belgrade), South American giants (Flamengo, São Paulo, Santos, Peñarol, Nacional Montevideo, Universidad de Chile), and a handful from Mexico/Saudi/Egypt/USA/Oceania (Club América, Pumas, Al-Ahli Saudi, Zamalek, Al Ahly, Seattle Sounders, Auckland City). **Zero Asian clubs survived** — every East Asian candidate resolved (Kashima Antlers, FC Seoul, Kawasaki Frontale, Yokohama F. Marinos, Vissel Kobe, Guangzhou FC, Shanghai Port) had spelled-out text once reviewed; not a scope decision, just how the review landed.

**Tech/car/airline/food general expansion — confirms the "practical ceiling" note above, don't expect this to move much further.** Ran the *original* Wikidata P154 pipeline (correctly — this one still works fine for company logos, unlike football clubs) against ~140 fresh candidates skewed toward the highest-remaining-yield category (more Google/Adobe/Microsoft ecosystem apps, dev tools, a dozen more car marques, a dozen more airlines/food chains). Raw resolve rate was decent (~65%), but **post-visual-review yield was only 4 questions kept out of 144 downloaded (~3%)** — Google Chat, Linux (Tux), WordPress ("W"), TypeScript ("TS"). Everything else in this batch was a full wordmark (the large majority — Discord, Reddit, Tumblr, Uber, Amazon, every airline, every fast-food chain, all checked and excluded) or, for cars specifically, confirmed the documented pattern that once you're past the ~20-40 most iconic marques, most remaining logos are either wordmark-only or simply don't exist as a clean standalone badge on Wikidata/Commons at all (Ferrari, Lamborghini, Aston Martin, Tesla, Hyundai, Kia, Nissan, Honda, Mazda, Subaru — all wordmark pulls this round, none had a badge-only file resolve). **This confirms the general (non-football) pool is genuinely close to exhausted at this quality bar** — further rounds should expect single-digit yields per ~100-150 candidates unless a fundamentally different sourcing method (like the Wikipedia-infobox trick that unlocked football) is found for a specific sub-category.

**Process note**: unlike earlier rounds, this one deliberately zoomed *every* borderline crest individually (via headless Chrome screenshot of just that one file) rather than relying on contact-sheet thumbnails for the final call — thumbnails were used only for the first triage pass to identify obvious excludes (full wordmarks) and obvious keeps (pure geometric marks with zero visible text at any resolution, e.g. Real Madrid's monogram, Atletico's bear crest), with every remaining "looks plausible" case individually zoom-verified before being added to the keep list. Given the ~40% false-positive-at-thumbnail-resolution rate found this round, **future rounds should default to individual zoom verification for every shield/crest-shaped candidate**, reserving thumbnail-only triage for candidates that are already obviously pure wordmarks (easy exclude) or obviously simple geometric/single-letter marks with no crest-style ornamentation (lower risk of hidden text).

## Android/iOS app conversion (Capacitor) — started 2026-09-07

User wants Adawati wrapped as a native Android + iOS app via Capacitor, on top of the existing web/PWA, without duplicating content or rebuilding the site. Full 20-phase plan supplied by the user (audit → PWA → offline → mobile UX → Capacitor → Android → iOS → QA → stores → Jordan integration → multi-country config → analytics → monetization → push notifications), sequenced across ~6 weeks (flexible — "not literally 6 weeks", can move faster).

**Week 1 (Phase 0 — audit, executed as a scheduled overnight job):** Full architecture/dependency/mobile audit, scored **89/100** initially, **~90/100** after this week's fixes (a judgment-call estimate against the original report's category weights, not a re-run automated audit — the user explicitly said not to bother re-running it). Full report published as an Artifact + PDF. Key findings:
- Dependency footprint is unusually clean: only 4 unique external `<script src>` references site-wide (GA + AdSense, both intentionally unversioned by Google design; Firebase SDK pinned to `10.13.2`). Heavier libs (`mammoth`, `xlsx`, `pdf.js`, `html2canvas`, `jsPDF`) are version-pinned and lazy-loaded only on `ar/file-converter`. No jQuery, no cookies, no fixed-width CSS causing horizontal-scroll risk anywhere (every flagged `width:` was actually `max-width:`).
- **`<base href="https://adawati.space/">` is not a Capacitor blocker to "fix" — it's a signal for the right architecture.** Recommendation locked in with the user: **don't bundle web assets locally into the native app** — configure Capacitor's `server: { url: 'https://adawati.space' }` instead, so the app is a native shell around the live site. This reuses the existing (already solid) service worker for all offline/caching behavior as-is, keeps one single source of truth, and avoids re-verifying CORS for the 5 third-party APIs the site calls live (`api.open-meteo.com` weather, `open.er-api.com` currency, `xaus.com` gold price, `api.country.is` geo-detect, `api.aladhan.com` prayer times) under a different bundled origin. **Do not silently switch to asset-bundling later without revisiting this decision** — it would break relative-URL resolution site-wide given how consistently `<base href>` is used.
- **Recommended against the plan's proposed `tools/oman/`, `tools/jordan/` folder reorganization** — user agreed. The current per-country top-level layout (`om/ ae/ sa/ us/ uk/ jo/`) is already deployed, indexed, and linked; reorganizing would mean a full 301-redirect + sitemap + re-indexing effort for no concrete unblocked benefit (Capacitor doesn't need it, adding a country doesn't need it). Revisit only if a future concrete requirement actually strains the current shape.
- Fixed same night (safe, additive, all validated + pushed): favicon (`<link rel="icon">`) was missing on **every single page** — added site-wide (260 pages) using existing `icon-192.svg`. Game pages (`jo/games/*`) never registered the service worker or linked the manifest themselves (relied entirely on the visitor having loaded some other page first — a real gap since shared challenge links are this feature's main entry point) — fixed by adding the same registration directly in `quiz-engine.js`. Game pages also never respected the site's dark-mode preference (same root cause: no `i18n.js`) — fixed by reading the stored `dark` key on load (no toggle button there yet, just honoring the preference).
- **Generated the missing native-app icon assets** (flagged as a P0 blocker for Phase 6/7 — `icon-192.svg`/`icon-512.svg` are SVG-only, but Capacitor/Android Studio/Xcode icon-generation tooling needs a PNG master): used **headless Chrome** (`chrome.exe --headless --screenshot=...`, already installed on this machine at the standard Program Files path — no new dependency) to rasterize a full-bleed square (no pre-baked corner rounding — native platforms apply their own mask shape, so a source icon with rounded corners already baked in double-rounds/gaps under the OS's own mask) version of the existing lightning-bolt SVG design at **1024×1024** (`icon-1024-master.png`, project root — hand this to `@capacitor/assets` or Android Studio/Xcode's icon generators later) and, via .NET `System.Drawing` bicubic downscale in PowerShell (headless Chrome's own tiny-viewport screenshots were unreliable — a `--window-size=180,180` render just cropped the top-left corner of a page CSS-sized for 1024px instead of scaling; fixed by making the render page's own CSS `100vw`/`100vh`-based instead of a fixed pixel size, which fixed the 1024 render, but the 180px screenshot was *still* unreliable at that tiny window size, so the working method was: render one clean 1024 master via headless Chrome, then resize *that* file with .NET), an **`apple-touch-icon.png`** (180×180). Both wired into all 260 pages via the same batch-script pattern as the favicon fix, plus a new `image/png` entry added to `manifest.json`'s `icons` array. **If regenerating these, remember the corner-radius lesson** — check the actual SVG source (`icon-512.svg` has `rx="112"` baked in) and strip it for any native/PNG-master render.
- Still open / needs the user, not mechanical: `manifest.json`'s `screenshots` array is empty (minor, richer install-UI only); no dark-mode *toggle button* on game pages yet (they now just read the preference); everything past Phase 5 (actual Capacitor install/build, real Android Studio/Xcode work, store accounts, native device QA) needs tooling this Windows environment doesn't have (no Android Studio, no Xcode — iOS builds fundamentally need a Mac) and the user's own paid accounts (Google Play ~$25 one-time, Apple Developer ~$99/yr).

**Week 2 (PWA/Manifest/Service Worker/Offline/Mobile UX) — same night, user said to finish it too:** most of this phase turned out to already be satisfied by the existing `manifest.json`/`sw.js`/Week-1 icon work; the two genuinely new pieces built:
- **Mobile bottom navigation** (`initBottomNav()` in `i18n.js`, called from the main `DOMContentLoaded` handler; a near-identical `initGameBottomNav()` duplicated into `quiz-engine.js` for the 15 game pages, same reason as every other game-page duplication this session — they don't load `i18n.js`). 3 items — **Home / Tools / Favorites** (the plan's suggested 4th "⚙️ Settings" item was deliberately dropped: a dark-mode toggle already exists in the top navbar via `injectDarkToggle()`, and there's no other settings destination yet, so a 4th button would have no real target). Reuses existing i18n keys (`nav_home`, `section_tools`, `fav_title` stripped of its emoji prefix) so it's already translated in all 6 languages on non-game pages; game pages use their own small AR/EN-only inline strings matching `quiz-engine.js`'s established scope. Links: Home → `getHomeHref()`, Tools → `getHomeHref() + '#toolsGrid'`, Favorites → `getHomeHref() + '#favSection'` (both anchors already exist on hub pages via the existing favorites/tools-grid features). CSS in `style.css` under "Bottom Navigation" — `display:none` by default, shown only under `@media (max-width:768px)`, `padding-bottom` added to `body.has-bottom-nav` in the same media query so it never overlaps page content, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` padding for the iOS home-indicator area. **Tested pre-push** (a first for a change this visually broad this session): since every page's `<base href="https://adawati.space/">` would've made a local-server preview silently load the *live, unedited* `style.css`/`i18n.js` instead of the local changes, defeating the point — built an isolated preview page instead that pulls the real local `style.css` via a tiny Node static server and manually injects the exact nav markup, verified in the browser tool in both RTL/Arabic and LTR/English, and in both light and dark mode, before rolling out to all 260 pages.
- **Offline status banner** (`initOfflineBanner()` in `i18n.js`) — a fixed top banner, hidden by default, shown via `navigator.onLine` check plus `online`/`offline` event listeners, telling the visitor live features may be unavailable but core calculators still work. Not duplicated to game pages (lower priority there — the challenge flow already needs a live Firestore connection to function at all, so "you're offline" is closer to self-evident there than on a pure client-side calculator).

## Known content/UX bugs already fixed this project (don't reintroduce)

- **Oman-only tool cards (salary-calculator, end-of-service, and on us/uk also vat-calculator + hijri-converter) were visible on every country hub and the global homepage** — user-reported 2026-09-14 via a screenshot showing `end-of-service.html` (explicitly Omani-labor-law content, "سلطنة عُمان" in its own title) reachable from the Jordan hub. Root cause: `index.html`/`jo/ae/sa/us/uk/index.html` each carried leftover `data-cat="oman" style="display:none"` cards (copy-pasted from `om/index.html`'s template, which is where these tools legitimately belong un-hidden), intended to stay permanently hidden since none of these 6 pages has a selectable `"oman"` tab — but each page's own `filterTools()` has `catMatch = !!q || activeCat==='all' || card.dataset.cat===activeCat`, and `activeCat` defaults to `'all'`, so `catMatch` is `true` for every card regardless of `data-cat` the moment the page loads, and `card.style.display = show?'':'none'` overwrites the original inline `display:none` — unhiding them unconditionally, not just during search. Fixed by deleting the leftover cards entirely from all 6 pages (they can never be legitimately reached via any real tab there, so keeping them served no purpose and was just a latent bug) rather than patching the shared `filterTools()` logic — lower risk, and `om/index.html` itself was unaffected (it shows these tools normally, by design, no hidden-card hack needed there). **If a future country hub is built by copying another hub's template, strip any `data-cat="X" style="display:none"` card whose `X` isn't one of that new page's own real tabs** — this exact pattern will silently reintroduce the same bug, since `filterTools()`'s `activeCat==='all'` default always exposes every card in the DOM regardless of category on initial load.
- Country-recommendation widget (`i18n.js`) used to freeze in the wrong language after switching site language via the dropdown — fixed by making `initCountryDetect()` actually write `sessionStorage.adawati_country` on the path-based detection branch (it only did this on the IP-geolocation fallback before).
- Zakat calculator: the "your gold is above nisab by X grams" info line was worded in a way users mistook for the zakat amount owed (it's just a weight-threshold note, unrelated to the actual JOD zakat figure computed separately). Reworded to remove the misleading number entirely.
- Wordle-style daily word game (`jo/games/wordle/`): the on-screen keyboard was missing the letter **ذ**, silently making any word containing it (e.g. "نافذة") impossible to guess. Fixed by adding it to `KEY_ROWS`. If adding more words to `WORDS`, validate every letter exists in `KEY_ROWS` first (script pattern used: build a `Set` from `KEY_ROWS.flat()`, check every char of every word is in it) — the keyboard still has **no key for أ/إ/آ** (only bare `ا` and the hamza forms `ء ئ ؤ`), so words must avoid those combined glyphs.
- The word-of-the-day mechanism was already correctly implemented before I was asked to add it (`dayIndex()` — deterministic by UTC date, same word for all visitors each calendar day) — don't rebuild it, just keep `WORDS` long enough that the cycle-repeat period stays reasonable (currently 129 words ≈ 4+ months before repeating).
- Cross-country navigation bug: visiting a shared/generic tool page (e.g. `bmi-calculator.html`) from a country hub (`/jo/`, `/om/`, etc.) used to force English and make the "Back to Home" links (both the floating prompt and the static per-page link) always point to the generic `/index.html` instead of back to the country hub. Root cause: `detectDefaultLang()` in `i18n.js` discarded country context on root-level pages. Fixed by having it check `sessionStorage.getItem('adawati_country')` (already set by `initCountryDetect()`) before falling back to English, and by adding `getHomeHref()`/`fixBackLink()` helpers that make both back-link mechanisms country-aware. No changes needed to the hundreds of individual tool pages — the fix works by rewriting `href` via JS on load.
- Multiplayer challenge "قبول التحدي" button had no disabled-state guard — tapping it multiple times while the async join was still in flight created duplicate accept records (user-confirmed real bug, felt like the button was "stuck"). Also, the capacity-check read at the top of `joinChallenge()` wasn't wrapped in try/catch — a transient network error there left the button permanently disabled with no error shown and no way to retry short of a page refresh. Both fixed 2026-09-07: the button now disables immediately on click, and the whole function body is wrapped in try/catch with a "جرب تاني" alert on failure that re-enables the button. Separately, a late joiner accepting *after* the creator had already clicked "ابدأ اللعب" used to wait on a `listenForStart()` Firestore round-trip; `joinChallenge()` now checks the `starts` collection directly right after joining and jumps straight into the quiz if already started, no waiting screen.
- Sitemap `lastmod` dates were a hardcoded string in `build-sitemap.js` (`const TODAY = '2026-09-02'`) that silently went stale on every subsequent regeneration — flagged by an external QA pass 2026-09-07. Fixed by making `TODAY` compute `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` at build time instead, so it's always accurate for whenever `node build-sitemap.js` actually runs.
- Multiplayer join had a client-side-only capacity race condition (`accepts.size() + 1 >= MAX_PLAYERS` checked, then written, as two separate un-atomic steps — simultaneous joins right at the cap could theoretically exceed it). Fixed 2026-09-07 by adding an `acceptedCount` counter field to the challenge doc and a Firestore security rule that only allows updating it as an atomic +1; `joinChallenge()` now uses `db.runTransaction()` to check-and-increment it together with creating the `accepts` doc. See the Firebase/Firestore section above for the exact rule and the transaction code.
- Every `jo/games/*` page's static "→ العودة لأدوات الأردن" back-link and was hardcoded to `href="jo/"` — harmless while games were only linked from `/jo/`, but became a real bug once the challenges module was added to every other country hub (`om/`, `ae/`, `sa/`, `us/`, `uk/`): a user reaching a game from `/om/` would hit "back" and land on `/jo/` instead of `/om/` (user-reported). First fix attempt (2026-09-07) gave the link `data-i18n="back"` expecting `i18n.js`'s existing `fixBackLink()`/`getHomeHref()` to handle it automatically — **this was inert**, because `jo/games/*` pages don't load `i18n.js` at all (confirmed via `grep -c "i18n.js"` = 0). Actually fixed by building a self-contained replacement inside `quiz-engine.js` itself — see "In-page English translation system" below. `data-i18n="back"` is harmless leftover markup on these pages (does nothing, not wrong) since nothing processes it there; the real fix is `qGetHomeHref()` + `applyQuizTranslations()` setting the link's `href`/text directly. Applied to all 15 `jo/games/*` pages including `wordle/`; `wordle/` needed a separate minimal fix since it uses neither `i18n.js` nor `quiz-engine.js` — a tiny standalone inline `<script>` right after the back-link (2026-09-07) reads `sessionStorage.getItem('adawati_country')` and sets the `href` directly, same country-code map as `qGetHomeHref()`/`i18n.js` but not sharing code with either (wordle is small enough that duplicating the ~5-line lookup was simpler than wiring in a dependency).
- Global homepage (`index.html`) was missing the "🏆 Challenges" tab and all 13 trivia game cards entirely (user-reported 2026-09-07: "In global page the challenge button not exist") — the module had been added to every country hub (`jo/om/ae/sa/us/uk`) but never to the generic global page. Fixed by adding the tab + the same 13 English game cards used on `ae/us/uk`.
- Mixed-language bug on `jo/index.html` (user-reported via screenshot, 2026-09-07): switching the site language to English left the hero subtitle, hero badges, and 6 of the page's category tabs (`jo-education`/`challenges`/`taxes`/`decisions`/`cars`/`daily`) still in Arabic, because those elements had no `data-i18n` attribute at all (i18n.js's `applyTranslations()` only touches elements with `[data-i18n]` — an element without one, or with a key missing from `T[lang]`, is silently left unchanged, see the `if (t[k] !== undefined)` guard). Separately, the `<h1>` had `data-i18n="hero_title"` — the *generic global* tagline key — producing an off-topic English string instead of Jordan-specific copy. Fixed by adding dedicated `jo_hero_title`/`jo_hero_sub`/`jo_hero_label`/`jo_badge_loan`/`jo_badge_free` keys plus `cat_kids`/`cat_jo_education`/`cat_challenges`/`cat_taxes`/`cat_decisions`/`cat_cars`/`cat_daily` to `i18n.js` (ar+en only, matching the "English only for now" scope already established for the trivia project — fr/es/de/ru intentionally left unset, same graceful no-op fallback as before) and wiring `data-i18n` onto every previously-bare element. Also retrofitted the "🏆 Challenges" tab on `om/sa/ae/us/uk/index.html` and the global `index.html` to use the new shared `cat_challenges` key instead of hardcoded text (om/sa were missing the key entirely; ae/us/uk/global had hardcoded English that wouldn't have reverted correctly if a user switched those pages to another language). **If a new Jordan-only (or any country-only) section/tab is added to a hub page in the future, always give it a `data-i18n` key from the start** — this bug happened because several tabs were added over time without one.
- Service worker (`sw.js`) precached a hand-maintained `STATIC` list that didn't include new `/jo/` pages as they were added, so they worked online but weren't available offline until a viewer happened to load them once first. Fixed 2026-09-07: the `install` handler now also fetches `sitemap.xml` and precaches every `/jo/` URL listed in it, so new pages get offline support automatically the next time `build-sitemap.js` + a cache-version bump ship together — no manual list maintenance needed for that part. Bump `CACHE` in `sw.js` (currently `adawati-v58`) whenever `sw.js` itself changes.
- **`quiz-engine.js` was being served stale indefinitely to returning visitors** — `sw.js`'s fetch handler treats it as a generic "static asset" (cache-first: serve from cache if present, never re-check network, cache whatever comes back the one time it does fetch), same bucket as images/fonts. Every edit made to `quiz-engine.js` this session (the translation system, `restart()` behavior, the join-button fixes, the atomic-transaction fix, the logo-quiz image support — all of it) was invisible to anyone whose browser had already cached an older copy, with no way to self-correct short of a full cache-version bump. This is how the logo-quiz "images not showing" report turned out to be a red herring at first look: the reporting device was still running a *pre-image-support* cached `quiz-engine.js`, confirmed by reproducing it live — `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()` found an active registration and `caches.keys()` found 2 caches; unregistering + clearing them and reloading immediately showed the correct current-code behavior. Fixed two ways 2026-09-07: (1) bumped `CACHE` to force every existing cache to be dropped once (`activate` deletes all caches whose key ≠ current `CACHE`, and `skipWaiting()`/`clients.claim()` are already in place so this takes effect on next load without waiting for tabs to close) — a one-time catch-up for everyone already stale; (2) moved `quiz-engine.js` into the same **network-first** `isCore` bucket as `i18n.js`/`style.css` (`url.pathname.endsWith('/quiz-engine.js')`) so this class of bug can't recur — every future edit to it reaches visitors on their very next load, no manual cache-version bump needed for *this specific file* going forward. **Lesson: any shared JS file under active iteration must be network-first in `sw.js`, not cache-first — cache-first is only safe for files that don't change, or where you will remember to bump `CACHE` on literally every edit.** If a browser-tool check of a live page ever shows behavior that contradicts the code you just read/pushed, suspect this before assuming the push failed — check `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()` / `caches.keys()` in that tab.
- Logo-quiz images ultimately also had a real (separate, smaller) bug once the stale-cache issue above was ruled out: `getQuestionImageEl()` sized the `<img>` with CSS `max-width`/`max-height` only, no explicit `width`/`height` attributes on the element itself. Some Commons SVGs render at 0×0 in that setup — the element has nothing to establish an intrinsic box from before the max-constraints apply, so it can collapse to zero and take up no visible space at all (not a broken-image icon, no layout gap, genuinely invisible). Fixed by setting explicit `el.width = 220; el.height = 140` alongside the CSS. Verified live post-fix by creating an `<img>` with the exact same attributes/CSS via `javascript_tool` and confirming `naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight`/`offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` all resolved correctly and a screenshot showed the logo rendering. If any future `<img>`-based feature is added anywhere on the site, give it explicit `width`/`height` attributes as a matter of course, not just CSS sizing.
- **Challenge creation was broken for every game except logo-quiz** since the `img` field was added to questions (2026-09-07) — `pickQuestions()` in `quiz-engine.js` always set `img: q.img` on every question object it built, so for the 14 non-logo-quiz games (whose source questions have no `img` field at all) this became `img: undefined`. Firestore's JS SDK **rejects any field with an `undefined` value** (`db.collection('challenges').add(...)` throws), so `createChallenge()`'s catch block silently showed "صار خطأ بإنشاء التحدي" for every single challenge creation attempt on those 14 games — user-reported 2026-09-09 via a screenshot of the error on Animals & Nature Quiz. `loadQuestion()` already handled a missing `img` fine (`if (q.img) {...}`), so the fix was just making `pickQuestions()` omit the key entirely when absent instead of always setting it (`if (q.img) item.img = q.img;`) rather than adding `img: q.img || null` or similar — matches the existing no-op-when-absent pattern used elsewhere in this file.
- "🔄 جرّب مرة ثانية" (Try Again, shown on the result screen) used to replay the *same locked challenge* — same 12 questions, same `challengeId`, same shareable link — because `restart()` skipped `pickQuestions()` whenever `challengeMode` was true, which by the current group-challenge architecture is *always* true once you reach the result screen (single-player mode was removed entirely earlier this session, so there's no other path to `resultArea`). User-reported 2026-09-07 ("لما تكبس جرب مرة اخرى بلش صفحة جديدة من دون اسئلة وزي كأنو دخل من الاول ويعمل شير" — Try Again should start fresh, as if arriving for the first time, with a new share). Fixed by collapsing `restart()` to a single `location.href = location.origin + location.pathname` — a full reload of the bare game URL, landing back on the "ابدأ تحدي" creation screen (new name entry, new `createChallenge()` call, new `challengeId`, new shareable link, needs a friend to accept again before playing). This is a deliberate behavior change, not just a bug fix — replaying the identical challenge is no longer possible via this button; if that's ever wanted again, it would need a separate explicit control, not `restart()`.

## adawati-app (Capacitor native shell) — separate GitHub repo, 2026-09-09 findings

The actual Capacitor/Android/iOS project lives in a **separate repo**, `adawati2027/adawati-app` (not this one — `my-tools-site` is the web content only, `adawati-app` is just the native wrapper). Same account owns both; the `GITHUB_PAT` in this repo's `.env` also has full push access there, but there's no `push-to-github.js`-equivalent for it — pushes there were done ad hoc via a small one-off blob→tree→commit→ref script (same pattern, no local checkout of that repo either).

A user-relayed QA report (82/100, claiming a "real Full QA on the GitHub repo itself") was independently verified file-by-file rather than trusted outright — mostly accurate, but with one gap worse than reported and one gap the report completely missed:

- **iOS Associated Domains — confirmed missing, then fixed 2026-09-09.** No `App.entitlements` file existed anywhere in the iOS project (not misconfigured — genuinely absent). Added `ios/App/App/App.entitlements` (`com.apple.developer.associated-domains` = `applinks:adawati.space`) and wired `CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS = App/App.entitlements;` into both the Debug and Release build configs in `project.pbxproj` (hand-edited directly via the GitHub API — no local Xcode/Mac available to verify, so the only real check was watching the `ios-build.yml` GitHub Actions run go green afterward, which it did). Still **cannot be fully closed** until the user has an Apple Developer account: the actual `apple-app-site-association` file needs a real Apple Team ID and would need to be published at `adawati.space/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` (currently 404, confirmed via curl). Android's equivalent (`assetlinks.json`) already returns 200 — that half is fine.
- **P0 the original QA report missed entirely: App Links open the app but silently drop the destination URL.** Traced directly in Capacitor's Android source (`Bridge.java`/`BridgeActivity.java`, `ionic-team/capacitor` main branch): when an `https://adawati.space/...` link launches the app via the `autoVerify` intent-filter, Capacitor does **not** auto-navigate the WebView to that specific path — it just loads the configured `server.url` root and exposes the incoming URL only via `@capacitor/app`'s `appUrlOpen` event, which something has to explicitly listen for. Confirmed via grep that nothing in this repo (`my-tools-site`) referenced `Capacitor`/`appUrlOpen` at all — so a shared challenge link (`.../jo/games/animals-quiz/?challenge=abc123`) tapped from WhatsApp would open the app to the home page and silently lose the `?challenge=` param, defeating the entire feature. **Fixed 2026-09-09** by adding a guarded `window.Capacitor.Plugins.App.addListener('appUrlOpen', ...)` → `location.href = data.url` snippet to the top of both `i18n.js` and `quiz-engine.js` (needs both since no single script loads on every page — same duplication reason as everything else game-page-related in this file). No-ops completely outside the native app (`window.Capacitor` is undefined on the plain web), so zero risk to normal browser visitors. **Not end-to-end tested** — no real device/app install available here, only the JS logic itself and Capacitor's documented API were verified. If deep links still don't work after this, check that the app actually delivers `appUrlOpen` on cold start (not just warm-resume) as Capacitor's docs claim, since that's the one part not independently confirmed.
- Other things checked and found fine, no action needed: Gradle files all present (`android/build.gradle` compileSdk/targetSdk 36, minSdk 24 — current), no secrets in the repo or its commit history (`.gitignore` properly excludes the release keystore/passwords/`local.properties`), `ios-build.yml` is genuinely just an unsigned simulator compile-check as its own comments say (not App Store-ready, expected).
- **Known gap, not yet acted on:** no Android equivalent of `ios-build.yml` exists — Android compile is never verified in CI at all, only iOS is. `minifyEnabled false` in `android/app/build.gradle` means release builds ship unobfuscated (low priority for a WebView-shell app with little native logic to protect).
- The rest of the original 30-item checklist (real-device QA, performance, accessibility, crash/network-loss scenarios, store listing) is still untouched — needs actual device/store tooling this environment doesn't have.

## SEO indexing automation (IndexNow), set up 2026-09-09

Google's and Bing's old sitemap-ping endpoints (`google.com/ping?sitemap=`, `bing.com/ping?sitemap=`) are **both dead** (404 and 410 respectively, confirmed live) — Google retired theirs in mid-2023. Don't use them or build automation around them again.

What's actually live now: the **IndexNow protocol** (supported by Bing + Yandex; Google does not support IndexNow — there is no equivalent unauthenticated mechanism for Google, only the Search Console API with a service account, which needs one-time setup by the user in Google Cloud Console + adding the service account as a Search Console user — not done yet, offer this if the user wants real Google automation later).

- Key file: `a5ea7337c7887c9e2f59989180c48e33.txt` at the repo root, deployed to `https://adawati.space/a5ea7337c7887c9e2f59989180c48e33.txt` (must stay published — this is how IndexNow verifies ownership, it is not a secret).
- Submission script: `indexnow-submit.js` at repo root — fetches the **live** `https://adawati.space/sitemap.xml` (not the local copy) and POSTs every URL in it to `https://api.indexnow.org/indexnow`.
- **Daily cloud routine** (`RemoteTrigger`, not a local session cron — those don't persist): "Adawati Daily IndexNow Submission", id `trig_01HYCRr6rKB8GWBn3r6uM6Hc`, `cron_expression: "0 0 * * *"` = 4am Asia/Muscat (Muscat is UTC+4, no DST). Deliberately has **no git repo source** — creating one requires the user's claude.ai account to have GitHub connected first (hit this as a real error, `authentication_error`/`Connect your GitHub account`), and the task doesn't need repo access anyway since it's pure public HTTP calls (fetch the live sitemap, POST to IndexNow) — the whole logic is inlined directly in the routine's prompt instead of pointing it at `indexnow-submit.js`. If the routine ever needs repo access for something else, the user has to connect GitHub in claude.ai settings first.
- First manual run (2026-09-09) got `403 SiteVerificationNotCompleted` immediately after the key file went live — expected, IndexNow needs a short propagation window to see the key file. Retrying ~90s later succeeded (`200 OK`, 229 URLs). If the daily job ever reports this error, it's not a real failure mode for a key file that's been live for a while — investigate rather than assume it's transient.

## Currency converter bugs fixed 2026-09-09 + site-wide systemic scan

`currency-converter.html` (and all 6 language variants — ar/de/es/fr/ru + root, JS logic is byte-identical across all 6, verified) had its result never visible: `#resultBox` used the shared `.result-box` component (defaults `display:none` in `style.css`, needs a `.show` class added by JS) but `convert()` never added it, and the inner text used `.result-main`/`.result-rate` classes that have zero matching CSS anywhere (real classes are `.result-value`/`.result-label`). The computed conversion was always correct — confirmed via headless Chrome DOM inspection — it was purely invisible. Fixed by adding `show` to the initial class list and renaming to the real shared classes, across all 6 files.

Same session, also added (user-requested, all 6 files): default `From` currency is now the visitor's detected country currency (`COUNTRY_CURRENCY` map + `detectCountryCode()` — checks `sessionStorage.adawati_country` first, falls back to a live `api.country.is` fetch since this page has no `#toolsGrid` so `i18n.js`'s own `initCountryDetect()` never runs on it), default `To` is always `USD`; and a type-to-search currency picker (`initCombo()` — wraps each hidden `<select>` with a text input + filtered dropdown, search by code or name) replacing the old long native `<select>` list.
