**After fixing this, ran two systemic scans across the whole site** (user asked "can we check the rest of the system this way") to see if the same bug class exists elsewhere — both came back clean, i.e. this was an isolated incident, not a pattern:
1. Every file using the shared `.result-box` component (113 files) checked for whether JS ever actually shows it. 25 came back flagged by a naive regex; manually verified all 5 distinct tools behind them (bmi-calculator, percentage-calculator, hijri-converter, file-converter, index.html smart-search) — every one works correctly via a different valid mechanism (`.className =` reassignment, chained `.style.display =`) my regex just didn't recognize. **False positives, not bugs** — don't re-flag these without deeper investigation.
2. Every class used anywhere in HTML checked against all CSS (shared `style.css` + each page's own inline `<style>`) for zero matches (the same root cause as the `result-main`/`result-rate` half of the bug). 20 hits, all password-generator/percentage-calculator/timezone-converter/index.html/jo-university-gpa — every one is either a pure JS `querySelector` hook or fully covered by inline `style="..."`, no real styling dependency. Also not bugs.

If this pattern-scan is worth repeating after a future large change, the two scripts (`scan_resultbox.js`, `scan_orphan_classes.js`) were scratchpad-only, not committed to the repo — rewrite them fresh rather than looking for them here (same approach: grep all files for the shared class, then check state-mutation patterns / cross-reference class names against all `<style>` content).

## User accounts (Firebase Auth) + cross-device favorites/recent sync — built 2026-09-09

Implemented per the approved plan (email+password + Google, staged Phase 1/2/3 — see `functional-squishing-stearns` plan if it still exists locally). All in `i18n.js`: `FIREBASE_CONFIG` const, `loadFirebaseAuth()` (lazy-loads firebase-app/auth/firestore compat SDKs v10.13.2, function-scoped only — never call at module scope, `build-lang-pages.js` evaluates the whole file in a Node `vm` sandbox with no `firebase` global), `onAuthSuccessSync()`/`syncFavsToCloud()`/`syncRecentToCloud()`/`mergeArraysUnion()` (union-merge on login, localStorage stays primary/synchronous, Firestore writes are non-blocking `.catch(()=>{})`-guarded), `restoreSessionIfAny()` (background silent restore via `localStorage.adawati_uid` flag), `injectAuthBtn()`/`updateAuthBtn()`/`openAuthModal()`/`submitAuthForm()`/`signInGoogle()`/`logoutUser()`. Firestore rule added: `users/{uid}` owner-only read/write, array-size-capped (favorites ≤200, recent ≤6).

**`signInGoogle(lang)` branches on `window.Capacitor.Plugins.FirebaseAuthentication`** — inside the native app it calls the native plugin's `signInWithGoogle()` (bypasses the WebView `disallowed_useragent` block that kills the standard `signInWithPopup()` there), gets an `idToken`, builds a `firebase.auth.GoogleAuthProvider.credential(idToken)`, and signs into the JS SDK via `signInWithCredential()` so the rest of the auth code (`onAuthSuccessSync` etc.) stays uniform regardless of platform. On the plain website it falls back to `signInWithPopup()`. `@capacitor-firebase/authentication` is installed in `adawati-app`'s `package.json`; Android's `google-services.json` is in place with the release keystore's SHA-1 registered in Firebase console (`1ef2f808f4df3dbce0f519ca8234e8d2dd651c7d`). **iOS native Google Sign-In not done** — same "no Mac" blocker as all other iOS native work.

`sw.js` bypasses Auth/Firestore/Google domains entirely in the fetch handler (`www.gstatic.com`, `identitytoolkit.googleapis.com`, `securetoken.googleapis.com`, `firestore.googleapis.com`, `accounts.google.com`, `apis.google.com`) — these are POSTs, `Cache.put()` throws on non-GET.

**Gradle/JDK gotcha hit while rebuilding the Android app for this**: Gradle 8.14.3 cannot run under Java 25 (`Unsupported class file major version 69`) — Android Studio's bundled JBR is Java 25. Use a real JDK 17-21 instead: `C:\Program Files\Microsoft\jdk-21.0.12.101-hotspot` is already installed on this machine and works (`JAVA_HOME` env var before `gradlew.bat assembleRelease`). Don't download a fresh JDK without checking for an existing one first.

## Two real bugs found + fixed 2026-09-09 (don't reintroduce)

- **`detectDefaultLang()` never read back a previously-saved language choice** — a user who explicitly switched to Arabic on one page would silently revert to English/auto-detected language on the next page, because the function checked URL path and country session storage but never `localStorage.getItem('lang')` itself. Fixed by checking the saved `lang` value right after URL-path detection, before falling back to country-based defaults.
- **`_getPageSlug()` returned the literal string `'index'` for every nested `.../index.html` page** (e.g. every `/jo/...`, `/om/...` page whose URL ends in `/`) instead of a page-specific id — this collided every such page's favorite-star id onto the single string `'index'`, so favoriting one country-vertical page favorited (and showed as favorited on) all of them. Fixed to fall back to the second-to-last path segment when the last one is empty/`index`.
- **20 of the ~36 `jo/` non-game pages, plus 1 legacy page each in `om/ae/sa/us/uk`, never loaded `i18n.js` at all** (confirmed via `grep -L i18n.js` across all HTML) — these pages had zero favorites, zero dark-mode toggle, zero auth button, zero bottom-nav, zero language-switching, zero PWA/offline-banner, because the single file that provides all of that was simply never included in their `<head>`/`<body>`. User-reported via a screenshot of `jo/calculators/social-security/` showing no favorite star at all. Root cause: these pages were built from a template that predates `i18n.js` being added to the standard page template, and nothing ever caught that they'd fallen out of sync. Fixed by appending `<script src="i18n.js?v=41" defer></script>` before `</body>` on all 24 affected files (`jo/calculators/{car-cost,income-tax,package-customs,rent-vs-buy,social-security,tawjihi-average,university-cost,university-gpa,wedding-cost,zakat}`, `jo/{emergency-numbers,fuel-prices,gold-price,holidays,prayer-times,weather}`, `jo/students/scholarships-loans`, `jo/tools/{government-vs-private,jordan-vs-gulf}`, `om/salary-calculator`, `ae/vat-calculator`, `sa/vat-calculator`, `us/tip-calculator`, `uk/percentage-calculator`) — all already had `<base href>` and `.page-header`/`.card` markup matching what `i18n.js` expects, so no other template changes were needed. **The 15 `jo/games/*` + `wordle/` pages are correctly excluded from this** — they intentionally don't load `i18n.js` (see the "In-page English translation system" section above); don't add it to them. **If a new `/jo/` (or any country-vertical) page is created from an older copy-pasted template in the future, verify it includes `i18n.js` before shipping** — this class of bug is invisible in a quick visual check since the page still renders and functions as a calculator, it just silently lacks every cross-page feature.

## Google Sign-In `auth/unauthorized-domain` — fixed 2026-09-09

After shipping the Google Sign-In UI (see above), a live click on the "Sign in with Google" button on the actual site failed with a generic "Something went wrong" toast — no code path bug, the real Firebase error (confirmed by calling `signInWithPopup` directly via a live Chrome DevTools Protocol session and reading `err.code`) was **`auth/unauthorized-domain`**: `adawati.space` was never added to Firebase Auth's **Authorized domains** list (Authentication → Settings → Authorized domains in the Firebase console) — only `localhost`, `adawati-challenges.firebaseapp.com`, and `adawati-challenges.web.app` were present by default. Firebase does not auto-add a custom domain just because GitHub Pages/DNS is configured for it; this is a separate manual step. **Fixed by adding `adawati.space` as a Custom authorized domain via the console** (confirmed live — `signInWithPopup` now opens a real `accounts.google.com` sign-in popup instead of erroring immediately). If Google Sign-In ever breaks again with a generic error, check this list first before assuming a code regression.

**CDP typing gotcha hit while fixing this**: typing into the domain-add dialog's input using `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` with both a `keyDown` (carrying `text`) and a separate `char` event for the same character double-inserts every character (Chrome processes `text` on `keyDown` *and* the `char` event as two separate insertions) — produced `"aaddaawwaattii..ssppaaccee"` from `"adawati.space"`. **Use `Input.insertText` for typing into a field, not manual `keyDown`+`char` event pairs** — a single `Input.insertText` call after a real (coordinate-based `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, not `.click()`) click into the field worked correctly and is the simpler, correct approach; this reconfirms the same lesson noted elsewhere in this file about the Firestore Rules Monaco editor, just for a plain `<input>` this time.

## Google Sign-In on mobile — signInWithRedirect doesn't work here, popup is the fix — 2026-09-10

After the `auth/unauthorized-domain` fix above, Google Sign-In worked on desktop Chrome but the user reported it still didn't work from a real phone. **First attempt (wrong): switched to `signInWithRedirect` for mobile user agents**, following Firebase's generic advice that popups are unreliable on mobile web. This made things worse, not better, and was reverted the same day — **do not reintroduce a mobile-UA → `signInWithRedirect` branch without re-reading this note.**

**How it was actually diagnosed**: emulated a real mobile browser via Chrome DevTools Protocol (`Emulation.setUserAgentOverride` with a real iPhone Safari UA string + `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` for a mobile viewport, on the *same* `76B...` CDP page target used throughout this session) rather than guessing from code review. Traced the full navigation chain (`Page.frameNavigated` events) through a real `signInWithRedirect()` call: `adawati.space` → Google's account chooser (silently auto-approved, since the test account already had an active session — `prompt=none` in the returned OAuth params) → `adawati-challenges.firebaseapp.com/__/auth/handler` (received a real OAuth `code` from Google) → back to `adawati.space`. **The OAuth exchange genuinely completes server-side** — Google issues a real authorization code — but `firebase.auth().getRedirectResult()` on the landing page resolves to `{user: null, credential: null}` with **no thrown error**, and `firebase.auth().currentUser` stays null. Tried the standard documented fix (explicit `authInst.setPersistence(firebase.auth.Auth.Persistence.LOCAL)`, awaited before any auth call, added to `loadFirebaseAuth()` in `i18n.js`) — **did not resolve it**; re-traced the identical flow afterward and got the identical null result. Root cause not fully isolated beyond "this compat SDK version's redirect-state correlation across the hard page reload doesn't survive intact in this environment" — not worth more time chasing blind once a working alternative was confirmed.

**The fix**: reverted `signInGoogle()` in `i18n.js` to use `signInWithPopup()` as the primary path on **every** platform (removed the `/Android|iPhone|iPad|iPod|Mobile/i.test(navigator.userAgent)` branch entirely) — `signInWithRedirect` is kept only as a last-resort fallback, triggered solely on an actual `auth/popup-blocked` or `auth/operation-not-supported-in-this-environment` error from the popup attempt, not proactively. **Verified working end-to-end under the same mobile CDP emulation**: triggered the real `signInGoogle()` function (not a direct SDK call) via the actual UI button, polled state every 1.5s — sign-in completed at ~t+4.5s (SDK load + popup + silent Google auth), nav button updated to `👤 Ahmed`, modal closed; reloaded the page and confirmed the session persisted (`authNavBtn` still showed the signed-in state on a fresh load). This is the second time in this project popup has been proven reliable end-to-end on the account used to test it (also verified on plain desktop Chrome earlier the same day) — trust it over Firebase's generic mobile-web guidance for this specific app/SDK-version combination.

**Also fixed while here**: `checkGoogleRedirectResult()` (the handler for the now-fallback-only redirect path) used to silently do nothing when `getRedirectResult()` came back with no user — exactly the failure mode traced above, and exactly why the user's original report was "nothing happens," not a visible error. It now shows the generic error toast in that case instead of failing silently.

**If mobile sign-in is ever reported broken again**: don't reach for `signInWithRedirect` reflexively — reproduce first via CDP mobile emulation (steps above) and get the actual `err.code`/state trace before changing anything, the same way this was actually root-caused rather than guessed at twice in a row.

**Immediately after reverting to popup, the user still hit "Something went wrong" on a real phone** — the CDP mobile-emulation tests above kept passing because desktop Chrome (even with an iPhone UA string) doesn't enforce real iOS Safari's strict popup-blocking rule: Safari requires `window.open()` to happen *synchronously within the click handler*, not after any `await`/`.then()` — even a fast one. `signInGoogle()`'s original code called `loadFirebaseAuth()` (which fetches 3 external SDK scripts over the network on a cold load) and only called `signInWithPopup()` after that promise resolved, so on a real phone's first attempt the popup call landed too late and Safari silently killed it — this is invisible to CDP-driven Chrome testing since Chrome doesn't apply the same timing strictness, which is exactly why this needed a real-device report to surface at all.

**The actual fix**: `openAuthModal()` now calls `loadFirebaseAuth().catch(function(){})` (fire-and-forget) the moment the modal opens, *before* the user has even seen the Google button, instead of the SDK load being lazily triggered only inside the Google button's own click handler. By the time the user actually taps "Sign in with Google," `loadFirebaseAuth()`'s cached promise (`_fbAuthReady`) is normally already resolved, so `signInWithPopup()` fires synchronously within that click's activation window instead of after a network round trip. **User-confirmed working on their real phone** after this change (2026-09-10) — this is the fix that actually mattered, not the popup-vs-redirect reversion above (that was a real, necessary fix too, but on its own wasn't sufficient for a cold first load on real Safari).

**Lesson for next time**: when a fix "tests clean" via CDP mobile-UA emulation but a real user still hits the bug, don't conclude the emulation must be wrong somewhere subtle in the app logic — consider whether the failure depends on a *browser engine timing/security behavior* that UA spoofing on a different engine (Chrome here) simply cannot reproduce, and reason about the underlying platform constraint (Safari's synchronous-user-activation rule for popups is a well-known one) rather than re-debugging the same code path again.

## Nationality-landing pages (eg/in/pk/bd/ph) translated to Arabic — fixed 2026-09-09

`eg/index.html`, `in/index.html`, `pk/index.html`, `bd/index.html`, `ph/index.html` (Oman-based expat landing pages targeting Egyptian/Indian/Pakistani/Bangladeshi/Filipino workers) each had only ~14 `data-i18n` attributes (the shared country-switcher menu labels) — the rest of the page (hero, badges, stats, featured tools, key facts, full tools grid, FAQ, footer) was hardcoded English with **no** translation wiring, unlike the properly-built `ae/us/uk` hub pages (~109 `data-i18n` attributes each). Switching these 5 pages to Arabic left almost the entire page in English while the root `<html dir>` flipped to `rtl` — which also visibly **garbled short two-word English fragments via RTL bidi reordering** (e.g. the stat value "1 mo" rendered as "mo 1"), a second symptom of the same root cause, not a separate bug. User reported this via `eg/` specifically; scoped down (via `AskUserQuestion`) to translate `eg/` first as the model, then all 4 remaining pages in one pass.

**Fixed for all 5 pages**, ~450 new `T.ar`/`T.en` key entries added to `i18n.js` (`eg_*`/`in_*`/`pk_*`/`bd_*`/`ph_*` prefixes for nationality-specific hero/stats/facts/FAQ/footer content, plus shared cross-page keys `hero_badge_tools`/`hero_badge_nosignup`/`hero_badge_nospf`/`hero_badge_browser`/`stat_spf_lbl`/`stat_gratuity_val`/`stat_gratuity_lbl`/`footer_privacy`/`footer_terms` reused verbatim across all 5). The main `#toolsGrid` tool cards on every page reuse the **existing** site-wide `TOOL_META`-linked keys (`currency_card_title`/`sal_card_title`/`eos_card_title`/`bmi_card_title`/etc.) instead of new page-specific ones — those tools are generic enough that the existing translations fit, and it avoided writing ~150 near-duplicate key pairs. FAQ answers containing an inline `<a href="...">` link (2 per page, e.g. linking to `end-of-service.html`/`currency-converter.html`) use `data-i18n-html="{key}_html"` (innerHTML-based, see `setLang()`'s `[data-i18n-html]` branch) instead of plain `data-i18n`, so the link survives translation — plain `data-i18n` uses `textContent` and would silently strip any nested tag.

Written in Modern Standard Arabic (not Jordanian dialect — these are generic expat-facing pages, not `/jo/` pages), matching the register already established in the `eg_*` keys. Only `ar`+`en` were written (matching this project's established minimum-viable-translation scope for this feature area, same as the `jo_hero_*`-style keys) — `fr`/`es`/`de`/`ru` visitors keep seeing the original English text via the `T[lang][k] !== undefined` no-op guard in `setLang()`, not a regression, same as before this fix.

**If a 6th nationality page is ever added**, copy one of these 5 as the template (they're now the properly-i18n'd reference, not the old bare English ones) and follow the same key-prefix + shared-key-reuse pattern rather than starting from a pre-translation-era page.

## Branded result-image export/share, built 2026-09-09

`exportResultImage(titleText, rows)` in `i18n.js` — a shared, reusable utility for any calculator to let visitors export/share their result as a designed, WhatsApp-ready image instead of a plain screenshot. Renders entirely via Canvas 2D (no html2canvas/screenshot library — avoids adding a dependency to every page, and Arabic RTL renders correctly via `ctx.direction`). Portrait card, gradient background matching the site's existing button gradient (`#2563eb`→`#7c3aed`), "⚡ أدواتي" branding, title, each row as label+value (a `highlight:true` row — normally the final net/total figure — renders larger and in brand blue), `adawati.space` footer.

**Canvas height is computed from row count, not fixed** — `H = clamp(260 + (rowsHeight + 140) + 170, 900, 1920)`, `cardH` derived from the same (already-clamped) `H` so the two never go out of sync. A first version used a fixed 1350px height regardless of content and looked sparse/empty for short results (e.g. a 3-row percentage calc) — don't reintroduce a fixed height.

**Output path**: `canvas.toBlob()` → `File` → `navigator.share({files:[file]})` if `navigator.canShare({files:[...]})` is supported (native OS share sheet, so picking WhatsApp there attaches the image directly — this is the actual "ready for WhatsApp" delivery mechanism, not a bespoke WhatsApp integration) → falls back to a plain PNG download (`<a download>`) on desktop browsers that don't support file-sharing.

**Not automatic** — `exportResultImage()` only builds the image from a `rows` array; it doesn't read the DOM itself, since every calculator's result markup differs. Each calculator page must build its own `rows` (`[{label, value, highlight?}]`) from its already-computed result values and call it, e.g. `salary-calculator.html`'s `exportSalaryResult()` (first implementation, the reference to copy for other calculators) reads the same values `calculate()` just computed and calls `exportResultImage(t.export_title, rows)`. Needs an `export_btn`/`export_title` pair added to the page's own translation object (ar+en, matching the page's existing local-translation pattern — `salary-calculator.html` uses its own `SAL_T` object, not `i18n.js`'s `T`) and a button wired to an `on click` handler; `updateSalaryText()` (or the equivalent per-page function that re-applies translations on language switch) must also set the button's text.

**Shipped on 6 calculators so far**: `salary-calculator.html` (the reference implementation, uses its own local `SAL_T` object for the button label + title), then `bmi-calculator.html`, `percentage-calculator.html`, `loan-calculator.html`, `age-calculator.html`, `end-of-service.html` (all 6 lang variants each, regenerated via `build-lang-pages.js`). For this second batch, the button label was added **once** as a new shared key `export_img_btn` in `i18n.js`'s global `T` (all 6 languages) instead of duplicating it into each page's own local translation object — pages that already have `data-i18n` wired site-wide (all of these do) just need `data-i18n="export_img_btn"` on the button and `i18n.js`'s existing `setLang()`/`applyTranslations()` cycle translates it automatically, no per-page update-function edit needed. The image title still reads from whatever the page already displays as its own heading (`t.main_title` from a local object, or `T[lang].xxx_card_title`/`xxx_title` from the global `T` — whichever that page already uses for its `<h1>`), never a new key. `percentage-calculator.html` needed one shared `exportPercentageResult()` (not three) — it reads whichever of its 3 tabs (`res1`/`res2`/`res3`) is currently active via the `.tab.active` element's id suffix, since only one tab's result is ever visible at a time. Verified end-to-end via live Chrome DevTools Protocol sessions on two different pages (salary + percentage) — filled real values, triggered the export, captured the canvas before `toBlob()` consumed it (temporarily wrapping `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.toBlob` to grab `toDataURL()` first), saved and visually inspected the PNG both times.

**GitHub secondary rate-limit got unusually aggressive rolling this out** (5 pages × 6 lang variants pushed in one batch) — normal single-retry-and-it-goes-through behavior didn't hold this time; 3 consecutive full-tree pushes each still left 1-2 specific lang-variant files un-blobbed (`ru/end-of-service/index.html`, then repeatedly `ru/percentage-calculator/index.html`). Confirmed via a direct single-blob API call that the *real* hourly quota was nowhere near exhausted (885/5000 remaining) — it was purely the secondary abuse-detection throttle from firing many large pushes back-to-back. Fixed by falling back to the already-documented minimal targeted single-file push (blob → tree with one path entry + `base_tree` → commit → ref update, 5 requests instead of 443) for just that one stuck file, which succeeded immediately. **Lesson reinforced**: when a specific file keeps failing to blob across multiple full-tree push attempts, stop retrying the full push — verify live via `curl` first (don't assume a later successful-looking push actually included it, since `base_tree` inheritance silently papers over a still-missing file), then do a targeted single-file push instead of another full-tree attempt.

**Extended to 6 more calculators 2026-09-10**: `vat-calculator.html`, `compound-interest.html`, `discount-calculator.html` (2 tabs → 1 shared `exportDiscountResult()`, same pattern as percentage-calculator's active-tab detection, keyed off `.tab.active`'s `data-tab` attribute this time instead of an id suffix), `tip-calculator.html`, `date-diff.html`, `currency-converter.html` — all mechanical copies of the established pattern. `currency-converter.html` turned out to already be one of `build-lang-pages.js`'s processed tools (confirmed via its `PAGE_META` list) despite an older CLAUDE.md note describing it as "6 separate hand-maintained files" — that note was stale; only the root file needed editing, lang variants regenerated normally.

**Real bug caught during this batch's live-image verification**: a row whose label/value is pure Latin text (e.g. currency-converter's rate line, `"1 EUR = 1.1624 USD"`) rendered visually reordered under `ctx.direction='rtl'` (Arabic mode) — `"1 EUR = 1.1624 USD"` came out as `"EUR = 1.1624 USD 1"`, the same bidi-reordering bug class documented elsewhere in this file for untranslated static text, just triggered here by dynamically-generated Latin content instead. **Fixed in the shared `exportResultImage()`** (not per-calculator): a `fillRowText()` helper now sets `ctx.direction` per string based on whether it contains Arabic characters (`/[؀-ۿ]/`), independent of the card's overall `isRtl` layout — `ctx.textAlign` still anchors the position correctly regardless, only the internal character-reordering direction needed to follow the string's own script. Any future calculator whose row content is a mixed/dynamic string (units, codes, formulas) gets this fix for free since it lives in the shared function.

Extending to more calculators (GPA/tawjihi-average, income-tax, unit-converter, hijri-converter, etc.) is now even more mechanical: add the button with `data-i18n="export_img_btn"`, add a small `exportXResult()` reading the page's already-computed result-DOM values into a `rows` array, call `exportResultImage(title, rows)`. Do this incrementally per-calculator, not as one giant sweep.

**Gotcha hit while testing this**: a CDP-driven Chrome tab that had visited the site earlier in the session kept running old cached `i18n.js` even after a plain `Page.navigate` reload — `sw.js`'s `cache:'no-cache'` fetch mode should force revalidation, but this specific tab didn't pick up the change until `Network.setCacheDisabled(true)` was set before navigating. If a live Chrome-session check of a just-pushed JS change looks stale, don't assume the push failed — force-disable cache on that specific CDP session and reload before concluding anything is broken.

## SEO audit + fixes — 2026-09-10

Kicked off a "market the tools, not the brand" SEO push (per the user's own growth-strategy notes) with a quick technical audit rather than guessing where to start. Checked all 59 root-level pages for meta description, title, H1, canonical, and JSON-LD presence.

**Finding 1 — 9 kids-* pages had zero structured data or Open Graph/Twitter tags**: `kids-alphabet`, `kids-clock`, `kids-flags`, `kids-math`, `kids-money`, `kids-science`, `kids-spelling`, `kids-times-tables`, `kids-typing` all had solid basic meta (title/description/canonical/robots) but were missing the `WebApplication`+`FAQPage` JSON-LD block and `og:`/`twitter:` tags that every other tool page has — including their own sibling `kids-learn.html`, which has the block and was used as the template. **Fixed**: added `EducationalApplication`-typed JSON-LD (matching `kids-learn.html`'s category, not the generic `WebApplication` used for calculators) with 2 real FAQ entries per page, plus OG/Twitter meta, to all 9. Missing OG tags specifically matters for the "share result" growth push too — no OG tags means ugly/blank link previews when these pages get shared on WhatsApp.

**Finding 2 (bigger) — 25 real pages were completely absent from `sitemap.xml`**: the same 9 kids pages, plus all 9 `oman-*-guide.html` long-form guide pages (`oman-salary-guide`, `oman-tax-guide`, `oman-visa-guide`, etc.), all 5 `omr-to-*.html` currency-landing pages (`omr-to-inr`/`pkr`/`bdt`/`egp`/`php`), and `privacy.html`/`terms.html`. These aren't stub pages — `oman-salary-guide.html` alone is 242 lines of real content. They'd been live and linked internally, just never added to `build-sitemap.js`'s `TOOLS` array (which also drives `{lang}/{tool}/` generation, so they couldn't just be added there — none of these 25 have language variants, adding them to `TOOLS` would have produced 100+ broken sitemap entries pointing at pages that don't exist). **Fixed**: added a new "Root-only English pages (no lang variants)" section to `build-sitemap.js` with 4 separate arrays (`KIDS_ROOT_ONLY`, `OMAN_GUIDES`, `OMR_CONVERTERS`, plus `privacy.html`/`terms.html` directly) — sitemap grew from 230 to 255 URLs. **If a future root-level page has no language variants, it must be added to one of these arrays (or a new one) directly, never to `TOOLS`** — that's the actual root cause of this gap: pages built without lang variants were never added anywhere, since `TOOLS` was the only list anyone thought to update.

**Verification**: JSON-LD validity checked via `JSON.parse` on all 9 extracted blocks (0 errors), inline JS on all 9 pages re-validated via `new Function()` (unaffected by the edit), sitemap's `<url>`/`</url>` tag balance checked (255/255), and all pushed content spot-checked live via `curl` post-propagation (`sitemap.xml` needed a targeted single-file push after the full-tree push dropped it — same rate-limit pattern documented elsewhere in this file).

**Next SEO steps, not yet done** (from the user's own growth-strategy notes, roughly in priority order): richer on-page content per tool matching real long-tail searches (e.g. age-calculator gaining "how to calculate age for documents"/"age calculator for babies" style subsections, not just the calculator itself) — start with the highest-traffic tools per the GA4 data already pulled this session (currency-converter, salary-calculator, the Jordan tools) rather than all 40+ at once; the Google Search Console access gap (still unresolved, see above) blocks seeing real query data that would otherwise guide exactly which long-tail phrases to target first.

## SEO — visible FAQ content out of sync with JSON-LD schema, fixed on 2 pages 2026-09-10

Started the "highest-traffic tools first" pass and found a real, concrete pattern worth checking on any other tool page: **`currency-converter.html`'s JSON-LD `FAQPage` schema had 12 rich, specific questions (exact currency pairs like OMR/INR, OMR/SAR, the USD peg, etc. — exactly the long-tail searches this SEO push is trying to capture) but the page's own visible, clickable FAQ accordion only showed 5 generic ones.** Google's guidance is that FAQ structured data should reflect content that's actually visible on the page — a schema/visible-content mismatch is a real technical-SEO risk, not just a missed-opportunity one. **Fixed**: expanded the visible `faq` array in both the `ar` and `en` blocks (this page's own local translation object, not `i18n.js`'s global `T`) to the same 12 questions as the JSON-LD, in both languages — `fr`/`es`/`de`/`ru` were left at their original shorter set for now (same "start with what's proven, extend later" pattern used elsewhere in this file), matching the site's established English+Arabic minimum-viable-scope convention.

**Second, unrelated bug found while there**: `salary-calculator.html`'s own `ar` FAQ block had **several answers still written in English** despite being inside the Arabic translation object — a genuine visible-content bug for Arabic-speaking users (the majority audience), not a schema-sync issue this time. Also found the same schema-vs-visible gap here too (10 real `Question` entries in JSON-LD, only 8 rendered as visible `<details>`). **Fixed both at once**: rewrote the entire `ar` `faq` array with real Arabic questions AND answers (not just translating the answers while leaving English questions, which is what had gone wrong before), and added the 2 missing entries (employer SPF contribution rate, overtime calculation) to both the `ar`/`en` arrays *and* as static `<details>` blocks (this page's FAQ section has both — static HTML as a pre-JS fallback, plus the JS-driven `t.faq.map()` render that overwrites it on load; both were updated for consistency, though only the JS-driven one is what most users ever actually see).

**Verified live via Chrome DevTools Protocol** on both pages: called `setLang('ar')`/`salLang='ar'; updateSalaryText()` directly in a live tab and read back the rendered FAQ item count and text — confirmed 12/12 items on currency-converter (in both English and Arabic) and 10/10 on salary-calculator, with an explicit check that no `summary` element's Arabic-mode text started with a Latin character (`hasEnglish: false`) to catch a regression of the exact mixed-language bug just fixed.

**Worth checking on other high-traffic tool pages next**: whether the same JSON-LD-richer-than-visible-content pattern exists elsewhere (a fast repeatable check: compare the count of `"@type":"Question"` in the page's JSON-LD against the count of `.faq-item`/`<details>` elements actually rendered) before assuming any given page's FAQ is already complete.

## SEO — jo/ calculators FAQ audit + fixes, 2026-09-10

Ran the same JSON-LD-vs-visible-FAQ audit (established above on currency-converter/salary-calculator) across every `jo/calculators/*` page plus `jo/index.html`:
```bash
for f in jo/index.html jo/calculators/*/index.html; do
  jsonld=$(grep -o '"@type":"Question"' "$f" | wc -l)
  visible=$(grep -o "<details" "$f" | wc -l)
  echo "$f: jsonld_Q=$jsonld details=$visible"
done
```
Found mismatches on 9 pages, fixed all 9:
- **Schema present but fewer visible entries than schema** (added the missing `<details>` blocks, reusing the exact already-verified JSON-LD text): `income-tax` (+2), `package-customs` (+2), `tawjihi-average` (+1), `university-gpa` (+2).
- **Zero FAQPage schema and zero/near-zero visible content at all** (wrote new schema + new visible `<details>` cards from scratch, using general financial-planning knowledge appropriate to each calculator's own inputs — not new regulatory/legal facts, so this didn't need the heavy source-verification the numeric calculations themselves already went through): `car-cost`, `rent-vs-buy`, `university-cost`, `wedding-cost` (all had 0 schema — `rent-vs-buy` had an existing plain info box converted into a proper FAQ card instead of adding a separate one), `zakat` (had 2 JSON-LD questions but 0 visible FAQ content — added a new FAQ card).
- `social-security` audited and found already in sync (3/3) — no change.
- `jo/index.html` audited: 0 JSON-LD / 0 details — left alone deliberately, it's a navigational hub page, not a specific tool, so FAQ schema isn't really appropriate there.

**Verification**: `JSON.parse` on every extracted JSON-LD block across all 9 files (0 errors) and `new Function()` on every inline `<script>` (0 errors) before pushing. Pushed via `node push-to-github.js` (hit the usual GitHub secondary rate limit near the end of the ~443-file batch — ~34 unrelated files failed to blob, but none of the 9 target files were among them, confirmed by checking the failure list). Verified all 9 live via `curl`, matching the exact new text against each page's own local source (not a generic string) to avoid false negatives from guessing wrong search text.

Started the "richer on-page content per tool" backlog item (highest-traffic tools first, per GA4). Added one new content card to each of the two highest-traffic tools, in English (root file only — these render on all lang variants since `build-lang-pages.js` copies the root file's body wholesale into each lang variant and these cards are wrapped `lang="en" dir="ltr"`, same pattern as the existing "How Exchange Rates Work"/"SPF Explained" cards already on these pages):
- `currency-converter.html`: **"Sending Money Abroad from Oman — What to Compare"** — targets remittance-related long-tail searches (relevant given the site's Oman-expat audience and the `eg/in/pk/bd/ph` nationality-landing pages). ## External QA report, 2026-09-10 — verified before acting, mixed accuracy

User pasted a large (18-point) unsolicited QA report claiming an "8.8/10" site review. Per this project's established practice (see the `adawati-app` QA-report section and the income-tax re-flagging incident above), verified claims against the live code file-by-file rather than trusting the report — several were stale/false:
- **"Recently Used missing" — false.** Exists (`recentSection`/`recentGrid` in `index.html`, `trackRecent()`), just labeled "✨ You Might Like" and correctly hidden until the visitor has browsing history (a fresh crawl seeing nothing is expected behavior, not a bug).
- **"Related Tools empty on age-calculator" — false.** Has 4 real working links.
- **"Duplicate Hijri-age FAQ" — true, confirmed and fixed.** Two separate visible FAQ entries asked the same question ("Why is my Hijri age different..." in the main JS-toggle FAQ set, "What is the difference between my Hijri and Gregorian age?" in a separately-added static `<details>` block) — removed the redundant `<details>` one.
- **App CTA — investigated and intentionally skipped.** No live Play Store/App Store listing exists yet (confirmed with the user) — a "Get the App" button with nowhere real to send users would be worse than no button. Revisit once `adawati-app` is actually published; see the adawati-app section above for what's still blocking that (user's own paid developer accounts).
- **Tool-naming inconsistency — real, found a worse case than the report's own example.** The report cited "Percentage Calculator" vs "Percentage & Discount" as the same tool having two names on the homepage — confirmed true (2 situational-bundle cards, lines using hardcoded English, didn't match the canonical `pct_card_title` i18n value/H1 used everywhere else) and fixed both instances, plus the JSON-LD `featureList`. Also found (not previously flagged): "Timezone Converter" (one word) vs the canonical "Time Zone Converter" (two words, matches the page's own H1 and `tz_card_title`) — fixed the one hardcoded instance without `data-i18n` protection (a bundle card) plus the JSON-LD featureList; left 2 other instances alone since they already carry `data-i18n="tz_card_title"` and get corrected by JS at runtime regardless of their raw fallback text.
- **Bigger, unresolved naming case found, deliberately NOT touched**: `loan-calculator.html` has **three different names** for the same tool: the page's own H1 says "Loan & Payment Calculator", the global `i18n.js` key `loan_card_title` (consistently translated this way across all 6 languages — not a typo) says "Monthly Payment Calculator", and most homepage card instances hardcode "Loan Calculator". Unlike the percentage/timezone cases, there's no single obviously-correct existing canonical form to converge on here — picking one means overriding either the page's own H1, a deliberately-translated i18n key, or the majority of existing links. Needs an explicit naming decision from the user before touching it, not a mechanical fix.

**Fixed as part of this pass** (2026-09-10):
- Removed the duplicate FAQ entry on `age-calculator.html`.
- Fixed the 2 "Percentage Calculator" → "Percentage & Discount" instances + 1 "Timezone Converter" → "Time Zone Converter" instance + the JSON-LD `featureList` string, all on `index.html`.
- Added a "See all tools →" link (anchors to `#toolsGrid`) under each of the 4 "Pick Your Situation" bundle grids (car/travel/job/student) on `index.html`, wired into `toggleBundle()` so it shows/hides with its matching bundle. This is a lighter version of the report's "internal-link cluster" ask (#13) — the bundles are intentionally country-agnostic on the global homepage, so a country-specific "cluster hub" like `jo/students/best-tools/` (built earlier this session) is a better fit for that ask than mixing Jordan-only tools into the global bundles.
- **Search matching improved**: `filterTools()` in `index.html` used single-substring matching (`data-search`/text `.includes(q)`), so a multi-word query like "jordan university" would never match anything unless that literal phrase existed verbatim in a card's keywords. Now also checks AND-of-words (every space-separated query word must appear somewhere in the card's searchable text, in any order) as a fallback when the full-phrase match fails. **Important caveat**: this still only searches cards already in the current page's DOM — it does NOT reach across pages (so "jordan university" still can't surface Jordan-specific tools from the global homepage, since those cards simply aren't in `index.html`'s `#toolsGrid` at all). Building a true cross-site smart search (a real search index covering all ~260 pages, fuzzy/semantic multi-result matching, the kind of experience the QA report describes with country/topic examples) is a substantially bigger feature — not attempted this pass, flagged as the actual gap if "smart search" work continues.
- **Zero-results fallback added**: when a search matches nothing, the "no tool found" message now also shows a "Search all of Adawati for '{query}' →" link to `google.com/search?q=site:adawati.space+...` — a real, if basic, escape hatch for the site's-search-can't-reach-everything gap above. New i18n key `search_try_site` added to all 6 languages.

## Cross-site smart search, built 2026-09-10

The gap flagged in the QA-report pass above (`filterTools()` on `index.html` could only match cards already in its own DOM — a query like "jordan university" could never surface Jordan-specific tools since those live on a different page entirely) is now closed with a real, if v1-scoped, cross-site search index.

**`build-search-index.js`** (new script, run alongside `build-sitemap.js`): reads `sitemap.xml` (not the URL-source arrays directly — reusing the already-generated canonical URL list avoids duplicating `build-sitemap.js`'s own enumeration logic), maps each `<loc>` back to a local file path, and reads that file's `<title>`/`<h1>`/meta description/meta keywords to build a lightweight per-page record `{u: url, t: cleanTitle, s: lowercased searchable text}`. Excludes `ar/`/`fr/`/`es/`/`de/`/`ru/` (non-English lang variants — the homepage search is English-context) and `jo/games/` (quiz/trivia pages dilute tool-search relevance). Output: `search-index.json` at repo root, 90 entries, ~47KB.

**Real gap found and patched while building this**: several pages are simply missing an obvious English search term in their own on-page metadata — e.g. `omr-to-inr.html` never says the word "currency" anywhere (only "exchange rate"/OMR/INR/Rial/Rupee), and `jo/calculators/university-gpa/index.html` never says "university" in English (only "GPA" in its keywords, everything else is Arabic). Rather than editing each affected page's actual SEO metadata (a bigger, separate content-audit task), added a `SYNONYM_RULES` array in `build-search-index.js` — URL-pattern-based keyword injection (e.g. any `omr-to-*` page gets "currency converter exchange rate convert" appended to its search text; any `jo/calculators/university-*` page gets "university jordan"; any `jo/`/`om/`/`ae/`/`sa/` page gets its country name appended) purely for search-matching purposes, without touching the pages' real meta tags. Verified via a standalone Node test (not a browser) that "jordan university", "currency india", "salary oman", and "vat uae" all now return sensible real pages — they returned empty/wrong before this rule set existed.

**Homepage wiring** (`index.html`): new `#crossSiteResults`/`#crossSiteGrid` section rendered right after `#toolsGrid`. `loadCrossSiteIndex()` lazily fetches `search-index.json` on the first non-empty search (not on page load, to avoid the extra request for visitors who never search) and caches it in memory; `renderCrossSiteResults(q, localHrefs)` filters the index by the same full-phrase-or-AND-of-words logic as the local card matcher, explicitly excludes any URL already showing as a local match (deduped via a `Set` of absolute hrefs built from the currently-visible local cards) and excludes the homepage's own URL (`https://adawati.space/`) since matching the homepage against itself is meaningless there, then renders up to 6 results as `🔗`-icon cards linking to their real (possibly cross-directory) absolute URL. `filterTools()` calls both the local matcher and `renderCrossSiteResults()` on every keystroke.

**`sw.js` caching fix applied preemptively**: added `search-index.json` to the same network-first `isCore` bucket as `i18n.js`/`style.css`/`quiz-engine.js` (bumped `CACHE` to `adawati-v61`) — this file will be regenerated periodically as new pages ship, and the exact "stale JS served indefinitely" bug already documented elsewhere in this file for `quiz-engine.js` would otherwise recur here just as easily for a cache-first JSON asset.

**Known v1 scope limits, worth knowing if this is extended further**:
- English-only matching (index built from English-context metadata) — a visitor searching in Arabic on the homepage won't get cross-site matches from this index. Not attempted this pass.
- Only 90 of the ~260 total site pages are indexed (root tools + country hubs + jo/ subpages + guides + currency-landing pages) — deliberately excludes lang variants and games/quizzes as noted above. If trivia-game search becomes a real want later, add a separate inclusion rule rather than removing the whole `jo/games/` exclusion (games pages don't have the same `<title>`/meta-keywords SEO investment as tool pages, so their index entries would likely be lower quality without extra work).
- `search-index.json` must be regenerated (`node build-search-index.js`) any time a new page is added or an existing page's title/keywords/description change meaningfully — not currently wired into any other build script, so it's easy to forget. Consider folding it into the same mental checklist as `node build-sitemap.js` (both should probably run together whenever new pages ship).
- Not tested via a real browser (no CDP/browser-automation tool available this session) — validated via Node-level JS syntax checks and a standalone matching-logic test against the generated JSON, not an actual live click-through. If cross-site results ever look wrong on a real visit, check this before assuming the index data itself is bad.

## Headless Chrome via raw CDP — reusable technique when no browser tool is available, 2026-09-10

This session had no CDP/browser-automation tool (confirmed via repeated `ToolSearch`, unlike earlier sessions this project references). Built a minimal working substitute using only Bash/PowerShell + Node, since Chrome itself is installed on this machine (`C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe`, used before for icon rendering):
1. Launch: `chrome.exe --headless=new --disable-gpu --remote-debugging-port=9333 --user-data-dir=<scratch dir> "about:blank"` in the background.
2. `GET http://localhost:9333/json/version` confirms it's up and gives the browser-level `webSocketDebuggerUrl`.
3. Open a tab: `PUT http://localhost:9333/json/new?about:blank` (must be PUT, not GET, on this Chrome version — GET returns a plain-text rejection instead of JSON, easy to misdiagnose as a JSON parse bug at first).
4. Connect via Node's built-in global `WebSocket` (Node 22+, confirmed on this machine at v24) to the tab's `webSocketDebuggerUrl`, send raw CDP JSON-RPC messages (`{id, method, params}`), track responses by `id` in a `Map`.
5. Useful methods for actually testing a live page's JS: `Runtime.enable` + `Runtime.evaluate` (run arbitrary JS in the page, `returnByValue:true` to get real values back, not just object refs) to both drive the page (e.g. set a search box's `.value` and call its `filterTools()` directly) and read results back (e.g. read `#crossSiteGrid`'s rendered `innerHTML`); `Page.navigate`; `Page.captureScreenshot` (base64 PNG, write via `Buffer.from(data,'base64')`).
6. **This is real functional verification, not a guess** — used it to confirm the cross-site smart search feature (built same session) actually returns correct live results for "jordan university", "currency india", "salary oman" queries, not just that the JS has no syntax errors.

**Gotcha hit while using this**: a first quick-and-dirty test script showed stale results across sequential queries in the same tab (looked like a real site bug at first) — turned out to be a bug in the *test script's* own timing/tab-reuse logic, not the site. A cleaner rewrite (explicit `inputVal` read-back alongside the result, reusing the already-open tab via `/json/list` instead of opening a fresh one per script run) confirmed the site itself was correct all along. **Lesson: when a CDP-driven test shows a suspicious result, suspect the test script's own async/tab-state handling before concluding the site is broken** — same category of lesson as the CDP mobile-emulation false positives documented elsewhere in this file, just inverted (false negative from the test tool this time, not a false positive).

**Real mistake made while doing this, worth flagging so it isn't repeated**: killed Chrome afterward with `taskkill /F /IM chrome.exe` — this kills **every** `chrome.exe` process system-wide, including the user's own actual browser windows/tabs, not just the isolated headless test instance. Got lucky this time (user confirmed no actual work was lost). **If this technique is reused, kill only the specific PID(s) actually launched for the test** (capture the PID when starting the background process, e.g. via `$!` in bash or `Start-Process ... -PassThru` in PowerShell), never a blanket `taskkill /IM chrome.exe`.

Audited the 5 `omr-to-{bdt,egp,inr,php,pkr}.html` landing pages for the "live rate vs. static approximate numbers" trust concern raised in the QA re-review. Found the pages were already reasonably well-engineered — the hero rate, quick-converter, and "Common Conversions" grid are all **genuinely dynamic**, computed live from a fetched rate (`liveRate`, fetched fresh from `open.er-api.com` on every page load, no stale caching) — and the static FAQ/reference prose (e.g. "1 OMR ≈ 210–220 INR in 2026") already consistently pointed to "the live converter above" for the exact current figure. Not a rebuild situation.

**Real, narrower gap found**: the on-page freshness indicator (`#rateTs`) just said `Updated: {date}` on success or a bare `Rate shown is approximate` (2 of 5 pages) / `Rate shown is approximate — check converter for live rate` (other 2 of 5) on fetch failure — no explicit "live" language, and inconsistent fallback wording across the 5 pages. Fixed on all 5: success path now reads `🟢 Live rate — Updated {date}`, failure path standardized to `⚠️ Rate shown is approximate — check the live converter above` everywhere. This brings these landing pages' freshness signaling in line with the more detailed status system (`dot-green`/`dot-yellow`/`dot-red` + explicit "Live rates"/"Cached rates" text) the main `currency-converter.html` already has, which these 5 lighter single-currency landing pages never had.

Also flagged: fabricated-sounding numeric growth projections ("3× visibility", "150-300 visits/week from a $2-4/day ad budget") with no real grounding — advised the user not to put these in anything shown to a real investor.

**One claim was worth checking properly**: "some tools are missing a related-tools section." Validated + `build-lang-pages.js` re-run + pushed; 3 of the 9 files (`number-guess.html`, `quick-math.html`, `random-number.html`) failed to blob in the full-tree push (usual secondary rate-limit pattern) and needed the targeted single-file push script — all 9 confirmed live via `curl`.

This is a real, not-yet-scoped feature request — pick it up as its own task, don't fold it into an unrelated batch.

## Daily-info modules extended to all countries, 2026-09-10

Jordan's 3 live-data daily-info modules (`jo/gold-price/`, `jo/weather/`, `jo/prayer-times/` — confirmed earlier this session these existed ONLY under `jo/`, nowhere else) are now replicated for `om/sa/ae/us/uk`, 15 new pages total. Deliberately scoped to just these 3 (not the other 3 Jordan has — `fuel-prices/`, `holidays/`, `emergency-numbers/` — which are static reference content requiring real per-country data collection, not a template swap) after explicit scope confirmation with the user.

**Built via 3 generator scripts** (scratchpad, reusable pattern if a 6th country or the static-content modules are added later): `gen_daily_info.js` (gold), `gen_weather.js`, `gen_prayer.js`, all reading a shared `countries_data.js` (per-country flag/names/currency/Aladhan country string+method/city list with real lat-lon coordinates). Each script has an `ar` and `en` template function — output language follows each country's **already-established** hub-page convention (confirmed via `<html lang>` on each `index.html` before building): `om`/`sa` are Arabic (`lang="ar"`), `ae`/`us`/`uk` are English (`lang="en"`) — matches the "language scope: country's natural language only" decision made with the user.

**APIs, all verified working before building** (not assumed from the Jordan template alone):
- Gold: `xaus.com/api/v1/spot?currency=<CODE>&unit=gram` — confirmed supports OMR/SAR/AED/USD/GBP directly (not just JOD).
- Weather: `api.open-meteo.com` — switched to `timezone=auto` instead of Jordan's hardcoded `timezone=Asia%2FAmman`, since Open-Meteo resolves timezone from lat/lon automatically; avoids hardcoding 5 more timezone strings.
- Prayer times: `api.aladhan.com/v1/timingsByCity?city=X&country=Y&method=N` — **verified live for all 5 countries** with real city/country name pairs before building (`curl -sL`, note the `-L` flag is required — the endpoint 302-redirects to a date-specific URL, invisible to a browser's `fetch()` which follows redirects automatically, but silently returns nothing to a bare `curl` without `-L`; this looked like a dead API at first and would have been a false negative if not caught). Calculation method per country: Oman/UAE method=8 (Gulf Region), Saudi Arabia method=4 (Umm Al-Qura — the actual regional standard), US method=2 (ISNA), UK method=3 (Muslim World League) — standard regional conventions, not verified against any authoritative source beyond that (same caution level as Jordan's own method=5 Egyptian, which was never deeply verified either — this is a calculation-method convention, not a legal/financial fact requiring the heavier verification bar).

**Cross-linked both ways**: each new page links to its 2 siblings (gold↔weather↔prayer) plus `currency-converter.html` and the country hub; each country's `index.html` got 3 new cards inserted at the top of its main tools grid (`add_hub_cards.js` script, inserted right after the `<div class="tools-grid">` opening tag via a literal string match — fragile if that exact literal ever changes, re-verify the insertion point if this pattern is reused). Added all 15 URLs to `build-sitemap.js`'s `countryTools` array (sitemap grew 256→271) and re-ran `build-search-index.js` (90→105 entries) so they're both crawlable and locally searchable via the smart-search feature built earlier this session.

**Verification**: `JSON.parse`/`new Function()` validated on all 15 new pages + 5 modified hub pages (0 errors). Pushed via `node push-to-github.js` — 7 files failed to blob in the full-tree push (`sa/weather`, `sa/prayer-times`, `us/gold-price`, `us/weather`, `uk/gold-price`, `uk/prayer-times`, `build-sitemap.js` — usual secondary rate-limit pattern) and needed the targeted single-file push script. **New paths took noticeably longer to actually go live on GitHub Pages than the usual 30-90s window** for existing-file edits — confirmed the files were genuinely in the git tree (via a direct `GET .../git/trees/main?recursive=1` API call) while `curl` against the live URLs still 404'd, then they came up within another ~30-60s of polling. If a brand-new directory 404s right after a push, check the git tree directly before assuming the push failed — it may just be Pages' build lag for new paths specifically. **Real functional verification** (not just HTTP 200) done via the headless-Chrome-CDP technique documented above, on a fresh isolated profile: confirmed `om/gold-price` (54.33 OMR/g 24K), `uk/weather` (17° London), `sa/prayer-times` (Fajr 4:18 AM Riyadh, correct Arabic date string), and `us/weather` (22° New York) all render real live API data, not just valid empty markup.

**Gotcha reconfirmed while testing**: killing the test Chrome instance by PID tree (`taskkill /F /T /PID <parent>`) rather than by image name (`taskkill /F /IM chrome.exe`) is essential — the latter kills every Chrome process system-wide including the user's real browser (this happened once already this session, see the CDP section above). Find the specific parent PID via `wmic process where "name='chrome.exe'" get ProcessId,CommandLine` and grep for the unique `--remote-debugging-port`/`--user-data-dir` value used for that test instance.

## Gold-price accuracy investigation, 2026-09-10 — real local premium confirmed for Oman only

User reported `om/gold-price/`'s 21K price (~47 OMR) was noticeably below real Muscat gold shop prices they'd observed for months (~51+ OMR) — **before any making charge**, not just a "spot vs retail markup" explanation.

**Investigation** (WebSearch was down the entire time — 6+ consecutive "unavailable" errors — so this was done entirely via direct `WebFetch` on specific known gold-price-tracking sites per country):
- `xaus.com` (the API this project uses) and `goldpricez.com` (a similar aggregator) **match each other almost exactly** in every country checked — both are pure global-spot-price-converted-to-currency, no local premium, confirmed by checking the underlying math (24K × 22/24 = 22K etc., always exact).
- `omangoldprice.com` showed **21K = 50.50 OMR** — a real, ~6.5% premium over our number, matching the user's own lived experience. This looks like a genuinely different, locally-run source (not a generic multi-country template site).
- Checked the same pattern for other countries and found **no equivalent gap**: Jordan (3 independent sources — `goldpricez.com`, `livepriceofgold.com`, and `xaus.com` itself — all landed within ~0.2% of each other, ~99.9-100.0 JOD for 24K), Saudi Arabia (`goldrate24.com` matched our SAR number to 2 decimal places), UAE (`livepriceofgold.com` matched within 0.2%), UK (`livepriceofgold.com` matched within 0.2%). **Conclusion: the local-premium gap is real but country-specific, not a universal Gulf/MENA phenomenon** — don't assume it applies everywhere just because it's real in Oman.

**No free API exists for genuine local-market gold prices** — every API-based service checked (`xaus.com`, `goldapi.io` by reputation) is spot-based; the sites that DO show locally-accurate numbers (`omangoldprice.com`) are plain server-rendered HTML with no API, and scraping them was explicitly discussed with the user and deliberately not built (fragile — breaks silently on any site redesign — and ToS-ambiguous). This is a real, structural limitation, not something to keep chasing without a genuinely new data source appearing.

**Fixed 2026-09-10**:
- All 6 gold-price pages (`jo/om/sa/ae/us/uk`): rewrote the disclaimer (both the visible `<p>` note and the JSON-LD FAQ answer) to honestly state the shown price is **global spot, not local market** — replacing earlier wording that undersold the gap as "might differ slightly."
- **`om/gold-price/index.html` only**: added an opt-in checkbox ("أضف تقدير هامش سوق مسقط (+6.5%)") that multiplies all 4 displayed karat prices by 1.065 and appends "(تقديري)" to each value plus a note on the updated-time label — implemented via a `rawPrices`/`renderPrices()` refactor (fetch sets `rawPrices` once, `renderPrices()` applies the multiplier and re-renders, called both after fetch and on toggle change — toggling doesn't re-fetch, instant UI response). **Verified live via CDP**: toggling on took 21K from 47.46 → 50.54 OMR, matching omangoldprice.com's 50.50 almost exactly.
- **Jordan's page**: a similar toggle was drafted first (before the per-country check was requested) then **deliberately reverted** — no evidence supports a Jordan-specific premium, so a fake toggle there would have been exactly the kind of unverified-financial-claim this project's core policy prohibits. **Don't add this toggle to Saudi/UAE/UK/US either** unless new evidence specifically supports it for that country — this is a country-by-country judgment call already made once with real data, not a "just add it everywhere for consistency" feature.

**Gotcha hit while verifying this push**: `om/gold-price/index.html`'s GitHub Pages deployment took noticeably longer than usual to go live (several minutes, not the usual 30-90s) even though the GitHub API confirmed the blob/commit succeeded immediately — checked via `GET /repos/.../pages/builds/latest` which showed `"status":"building"` for an extended period. **If a push succeeds (confirmed via the repo tree or a direct blob read) but the live site still doesn't reflect it after the usual wait, check the Pages build status endpoint before assuming something is broken** — it may just be a slow build, same lesson as the "new directory paths take longer" note above, but this time on an *existing* path with a larger diff.

## 5-module country expansion (Holidays, Fuel Price, Car Cost, Wedding Cost, University Cost) — built 2026-09-10

User asked to extend 5 modules that previously existed only under `jo/` (and `om/holidays`+`om/fuel-prices` in one case) to **all 6 countries**, each with country-specific real data, bilingual (AR default for om/sa, EN default for ae/us/uk, with an in-page toggle for the other language on every new page — same self-contained pattern as `quiz-engine.js`'s "In-page English translation system", **not** wired into `i18n.js`, per established convention for narrow-scope per-page content). Explicitly confirmed with the user beforehand: build all 5 modules together (not staged one at a time), and culturally adapt wedding-cost/university-cost per country rather than skip them for US/UK.

**Built via 5 parallel background forks**, one per module, each producing 5 country pages (25 new files total): `{om,sa,ae,us,uk}/holidays/`, `{...}/fuel-prices/`, `{...}/calculators/car-cost/`, `{...}/calculators/wedding-cost/`, `{...}/calculators/university-cost/`. Every fork's output was independently re-validated by the main session (not trusted at face value) — `JSON.parse` on all JSON-LD blocks and `new Function()` on all inline `<script>` bodies, 0 errors across all 25 files — plus targeted fact spot-checks via fresh WebSearch on a few uncertain items the forks themselves flagged (e.g. Saudi VAT rate, UK fuel prices) and a grep confirming `us/uk` wedding-cost pages contain zero mahr/مهر references (correctly adapted, not just translated).

**Integration** (done in the main session, not by the forks, to keep hub-page markup consistent): the forks' own self-reported hub-card HTML snippets used inconsistent markup (wrong icon-wrap class, inconsistent arrow text) vs. the site's established convention — cards were rebuilt from scratch using `jo/index.html`'s exact pattern (`tool-icon-wrap` div + title + desc + `tool-card-arrow` with "ابدأ ←"/"Start →"), using the forks' content (titles/descriptions/URLs) but correct structure. Holidays + Fuel Price cards were appended to each country's existing `<div id="dailySection">` (now 5 cards instead of 3); Car Cost + Wedding Cost + University Cost cards went into a new `<div id="decisionsSection">` ("💡 قرارات كبيرة" / "💡 Big Decisions") added right after `dailySection` on all 5 hub pages. `build-sitemap.js` got a new loop appending the 25 URLs to `countryTools` (sitemap grew 271→296 entries). `build-search-index.js`'s title/description regexes were tightened to tolerate extra attributes (`<title id="pageTitleTag">` — used by the holidays pages for their countdown feature — was silently failing the old exact-match regex `/<title>([^<]*)<\/title>/`, which meant those 5 pages weren't indexed at all until this was fixed).

**Push required 3 rounds** due to unusually heavy GitHub secondary rate-limiting on this batch (two full-tree pushes each dropped 30-60 files, including several of this task's own new/changed files). **Don't trust which specific files a full-tree push's error log shows failing as the complete picture** — do a direct tree-verification pass instead: fetch the live commit's recursive git tree via the API, compute each target file's local git-blob-sha1 (`blob <len>\0<content>` sha1, same algorithm git itself uses), and diff against the remote tree's sha for that path. This caught 8 files (`om/index.html`, `us/holidays/`, `us/calculators/car-cost/`, `uk/calculators/car-cost/`, `uk/calculators/wedding-cost/`, `sitemap.xml`, `search-index.json`, `build-sitemap.js`) that were stale/missing after both full-tree pushes despite not all appearing in either push's visible error log (the first push's failure log was truncated in terminal output, so some real failures were invisible without this check) — fixed via 8 individual `push_single_file.js` calls, then re-ran the same tree-verification script and got 0 mismatches before declaring done. **This verification-by-tree-diff approach is more reliable than grepping push logs when a batch has had multiple retry rounds** — reuse it (the script is a straightforward ~40-line Node script using `crypto.createHash('sha1')` on the git blob format) instead of trying to reconstruct which round actually saved which file from truncated logs.

All 34 touched/new files (25 pages + 5 hub `index.html` + `sitemap.xml` + `search-index.json` + `build-sitemap.js` + `build-search-index.js`) confirmed live via direct blob-sha comparison and spot-checked via `curl` (with a cache-busting query param — the bare URL initially showed stale CDN-cached content for `om/index.html` even though the underlying commit was already built, per the Pages build-status endpoint).

## Emergency numbers extended to all 6 countries — built 2026-09-13

Jordan's `jo/emergency-numbers/` page (static AR content, no live API — police/ambulance/fire numbers don't change) was the only one of the 6 countries with this module. Extended to `om/sa/ae/us/uk`, one new page each, following the language convention already established elsewhere (AR for om/sa, EN for ae/us/uk, no in-page toggle — this content doesn't need one since it's simple enough on one language, matching `jo/emergency-numbers/`'s own no-toggle precedent rather than the toggle pattern used on `fuel-prices`/`holidays`).

**Numbers verified via WebSearch (multiple independent sources cross-referenced per country) before writing anything** — this is safety-critical factual content, held to the same verification bar as the site's legal/financial facts even though it's not explicitly named in the verification policy:
- **Oman**: single unified number **9999** for police/ambulance/fire (confirmed via Wikipedia's Civil Defence and Ambulance Authority page + a dedicated emergency-numbers aggregator; 112 explicitly does NOT reliably work here — noted on the page).
- **Saudi Arabia**: 999 police / 997 ambulance (Red Crescent) / 998 civil defence — the historical 3-number system, still the only one that works nationwide. Also noted: a newer unified **911** exists but only in Riyadh/Makkah/Madinah/Eastern Province — worth knowing since some sources cite 911 as *the* Saudi number without that regional caveat.
- **UAE**: 999 police / 998 ambulance / 997 fire, all toll-free nationwide; 112 works as a backup reaching the same services; 996 is Coast Guard (mentioned as a footnote, not a main card).
- **US**: 911 for all physical emergencies; **988** (Suicide & Crisis Lifeline) added as a genuinely important second number, not just padding — distinguished clearly on-page (911 = physical danger, 988 = mental health crisis, no physical danger).
- **UK**: 999 and 112 are functionally identical (both reach the same services) — shown as two equal cards rather than picking one as "the" number, since sources are clear neither is more correct. 101 (non-emergency police, small per-call fee) and 111 (NHS non-emergency) added as a second row, mirroring the real UK convention of steering non-urgent calls away from 999.

**Integration**: hub card added to each of the 5 countries' `dailySection` (same `tool-icon-wrap`/`tool-card-arrow` markup as the 5-module expansion above, positioned right after the prayer-times card). Cross-links added to all 25 sibling daily-info pages (`{holidays,weather,prayer-times,gold-price,fuel-prices}` × 5 countries) via a small Node script matching each file's existing "insert before the primary-styled all-tools link" pattern — verified 100% consistent across all 25 files before running it (every sibling page ends its cross-link row with the identical `background:var(--primary)`-styled all-tools `<a>`, confirmed by grep before scripting, not assumed). `build-sitemap.js` got a new loop appending the 5 URLs (jo's was already present); sitemap grew 296→301, search-index 130→135.

**Push hit the usual GitHub secondary rate limit** (13 files dropped from a ~490-file full-tree push, including several of this task's own files: `sa/emergency-numbers/`, `us/index.html`, `sa/weather/prayer-times/fuel-prices/`, `us/weather/gold-price/fuel-prices/`, `uk/prayer-times/gold-price/fuel-prices/`, `build-sitemap.js`, `search-index.json`) — caught via the same tree-verification-by-blob-sha script documented in the 5-module expansion section above (reused as-is, not rewritten), fixed via one combined targeted-push script (single tree with `base_tree` + all 13 blobs, 700ms spacing between blob calls) rather than 13 separate single-file pushes. Re-ran the verification script afterward and got 0 mismatches before declaring done — same discipline as before, don't skip this step when a rate limit hits mid-push.

## Logo quiz — football clubs category added, 139 → 212 questions, built 2026-09-14 (overnight fork)

Ran as a background fork per explicit user request ("زيد اسئلة تحدي اللوجو وترفع العدد ل 400 وتضيف شعارات الأندية العالمية"). **Note: this section was reconstructed from the fork's own final report after its original CLAUDE.md edit was found missing from both the local file and the live repo** — a later same-session push (the onclick-refactor push, see below) captured whatever was on local disk for `CLAUDE.md` at that moment and overwrote the remote with it, and the fork's edit wasn't present in that snapshot (root cause not fully isolated — either the fork's write never actually landed on disk despite its confident report, or it landed and was lost before the later push ran). The actual code/asset changes (212 questions live in `jo/games/logo-quiz/index.html`, all referenced asset files present) were independently re-verified as intact and correct — only this documentation was lost. **Lesson for next time a fork and the main session both touch files in the same shared (non-worktree) directory: a fork finishing and reporting success is not proof its changes survived a later full-tree push from the main session — re-verify anything the fork touched (via live `curl`/tree-diff) before trusting it's still there, especially CLAUDE.md itself.**

**Result: 139 → 212 questions** (48 new football clubs + 4 new tech/app icons: Google Chat, Linux, WordPress, TypeScript).

**Football clubs — real pivot required**: the existing Wikidata P154 pipeline (used for the original 139) almost totally failed for football crests (~10% yield) because football badges are near-universally non-free/fair-use and thus absent from Wikidata/Commons under an open license. Switched to reading each club's **English Wikipedia infobox image directly** (falling back to `en.wikipedia.org`'s own file path instead of Commons) — yield jumped to 85% resolved (122 of 143 candidate clubs found an image at all). Full-resolution zoom review (not just thumbnails) then cut that down to 48 clean keeps, applying the same quality bar as the original curation pass (no spelled-out club name legible on the crest, no wrong-entity/wrong-image matches).

**Real bugs only caught at full-resolution zoom, not thumbnail review**: thumbnail-only review would have wrongly kept Arsenal, Tottenham, Ajax, Vissel Kobe, and Guangzhou FC — all have team-name text that's invisible at ~120px thumbnail size but clearly readable at full resolution. Also caught Raja Casablanca and Étoile du Sahel, which looked Arabic-script-only (and thus safe under the "script that doesn't spell the Latin answer text" exception documented in the original curation-rewrite section above) but actually had Latin team-name text mixed into the crest too.

**Photos-not-logos, same failure class as the original 464-question round**: Genesis Motor and Peugeot both resolved to `.jpg` badge *photos* (not rendered logo graphics) with the brand name legible in the photo — caught via the same extension-red-flag heuristic (real logos are almost always `.svg`/`.png`) plus zoom confirmation, matching the exact bug class documented in the original 2026-09-07 sourcing section.

**Confirmed again: 400 is not a realistic target while holding the quality bar.** General-category (non-football) yield was ~3% after full review this round — consistent with the practical-ceiling analysis in the original curation-rewrite section. **Zero Asian football clubs survived review** (every candidate had a spelled-out name visible on the crest at full resolution) — worth knowing if Asian-market clubs are requested again, since the answer isn't "search harder," it's that this particular category of source material doesn't have icon-only crests to find.

Full validation before push (same discipline as every prior round): 0 duplicate image paths, 0 malformed question entries, all 52 newly-referenced asset files confirmed to exist on disk, `new Function()` and `JSON.parse` both clean on the whole page.

## Deterministic calculator test suite — built 2026-09-14

Added `test-calculators.js` (repo root, no dependencies — matches the project's existing scriptless-Node-script convention, no `package.json`/test-framework introduced). Addresses the QA-report ask ("full calculator correctness still needs verification... use deterministic expected results") with real, working, reusable tests rather than a one-off manual check.

**How it works**: launches against the **live production site** (not local files) via raw CDP (same headless-Chrome-without-puppeteer technique used throughout this project — see the "Headless Chrome via raw CDP" section above), sets known input values through the real DOM, calls each page's real calculation function directly (`calcLoan()`, `calculateBMI()`, `calc1()`/`calc2()`/`calc3()`, `calculate()`, `calcAge()`, `generatePassword()`, `convert()`), reads the rendered result back out of the DOM, and asserts against an expected value **independently derived from each calculator's documented formula** (not just "whatever the code currently outputs" — that would only catch regressions, not existing bugs).

**Usage**:
```
"C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe" --headless=new --disable-gpu --remote-debugging-port=9700 --user-data-dir=<any scratch dir> about:blank
node test-calculators.js
```
Prints PASS/FAIL per check plus a summary; exits non-zero if anything fails.

**Covers 20 checks across 9 calculators**: loan (monthly payment, total paid, total interest — standard amortization formula), BMI, percentage (all 3 modes: X% of Y, X is what% of Y, % change), VAT (both add-tax and extract-tax modes, Oman 5%), age (years/months/days and total-days-lived), date-diff, end-of-service gratuity, currency conversion, and password generator (length + character-set rules). **All 20 pass as of this writing** — no real calculator bugs found this round, every calculator's math matches its documented formula.

**Two real technique gotchas hit while building this, worth knowing if extended further**:
- **`let`/`const` top-level variables declared inside a page's own `<script>` tag are NOT reachable from an external `Runtime.evaluate` call** (only top-level `function` declarations attach to `window` and are externally callable) — tried to inject test exchange rates via `window.rates = {...}` for the currency-converter test, which silently created an unrelated global instead of setting the `let rates` variable the real `convert()` function actually reads. Worked around it by blocking the 3 live-rate API domains via CDP `Network.setBlockedURLs`, which makes the page fall through to its own already-in-source hardcoded `FALLBACK` rates table — a more realistic test anyway (exercises the page's real fallback code path, not just its math).
- **A reused Chrome profile's `localStorage` persists across test runs and can silently defeat a network block** — the currency-converter caches whatever rates it last successfully fetched, so blocking the live APIs on a profile that already has a cached rate from an earlier run just serves the stale cache instead of falling through to `FALLBACK`. Fixed by explicitly `localStorage.clear()`-ing the origin before that specific test.
- **Date-only ISO strings (`"YYYY-MM-DD"`) parse as UTC midnight in JavaScript, not local midnight** — `new Date(2026,0,15)` (component form) and `new Date("2026-01-15")` (date-only string) are NOT the same instant unless the system timezone is UTC+0. This project's environment runs at UTC+4 (Asia/Muscat) — first attempt at the age-calculator test computed its own "expected" total-days value using component-form construction for the birthdate, which didn't match the site's actual behavior (the site correctly uses `new Date(birthInput)` on the raw date-input string, i.e. UTC-midnight parsing) — a bug in the *test's* expected-value derivation, not the site. Recomputed the expected value using the same UTC-midnight parsing the real code uses, which then matched exactly. **If extending this suite, always derive expected values using the exact same date-construction method the production code actually uses, not whichever form is more convenient in Node.**

## Browser end-to-end test suite — built 2026-09-14

Added `test-e2e.js` (repo root, no dependencies, same raw-CDP-headless-Chrome pattern as `test-calculators.js` and the ad-hoc CDP scripts used throughout this project). Complements `test-calculators.js` — that one checks calculation *correctness*, this one checks that the interactive chrome around the tools actually works: search, category filters, language/country selectors, dark mode, the mobile hamburger menu, and the PWA install-prompt behavior. Same usage pattern (launch headless Chrome on port 9750 first, then `node test-e2e.js`).

**29 checks, all passing on first real run** — homepage search (matching + no-results state), category-tab filtering, language switching (with `dir=rtl` and reload-persistence checks), country-menu navigation (actually follows the Oman link and confirms the URL changes), dark-mode toggle (with reload-persistence), the mobile hamburger menu (open, Escape-to-close, click-outside-to-close — regression coverage for the mobile-nav-collapse feature built earlier this session), the PWA install banner (confirms the ~4s delay actually delays it, confirms "Later" dismissal is remembered in `localStorage`), and a basic health sample (exactly one `H1`, zero console/runtime errors) across 4 different page archetypes (homepage, a root calculator, a country hub, a game page).

This is real regression coverage for several features built earlier in this same session (mobile nav collapse, PWA banner delay/overlap fix) — running it after any future change to `i18n.js`, `style.css`, or the homepage's inline script would have caught a regression in any of those, not just a manual spot-check.
