**Scope note**: doesn't include a full sitemap crawl (all 301 URLs returning 200, every page having exactly one H1/title/description/canonical) — that class of check has already been done repeatedly by external QA scans this session and isn't meaningfully different from re-running `curl` in a loop; the value-add here is specifically the *interactive* journeys a plain HTTP check can't exercise. If a true full-site crawl regression check is wanted later, it's a separate, simpler script (no browser needed, just HTTP + HTML parsing).

## Lighthouse audit + real performance/a11y fixes — 2026-09-14

Ran Lighthouse via `npx --yes lighthouse@latest` against the live homepage (no npm dependency added to the repo — `npx` runs it ad hoc; a harmless `EPERM` temp-folder cleanup error appears at the end of every run on this Windows machine but the JSON report is still written correctly, don't treat that error as a failed run). This was the first real Core Web Vitals measurement done for this project.

**First run**: performance 45/100, LCP 6.4s, CLS 0.333, FCP 4.9s — all badly failing. Investigated rather than assuming the site itself was slow:

1. **Real CSP gap found**: `csi.gstatic.com` (an AdSense instrumentation beacon) was hitting the `connect-src` directive and getting blocked, a genuine gap in the CSP shipped earlier the same session — missing `https://*.gstatic.com` from `connect-src` (it was already in `script-src` for the Firebase SDK, but not `connect-src`). Fixed by adding it.
2. **Real render-blocking issue found and fixed**: `style.css` loaded Cairo via `@import url('https://fonts.googleapis.com/...')` — an `@import` inside a stylesheet can only be discovered *after* the browser fetches and parses that stylesheet, forcing a fully serial round-trip before text can render. **Self-hosted the font instead**: Google serves Cairo as a single variable-font file per Unicode subset (identical URL for every declared weight 300-900, confirmed by inspecting the returned `@font-face` CSS) — only 3 files needed (arabic/latin-ext/latin, ~81KB total), downloaded once into `/fonts/` and referenced from `style.css` with `font-weight: 300 900` ranges. This is a single-file change (style.css lives at repo root, so its own relative URLs resolve against its own location regardless of which nested page includes it — no per-page changes needed across any of the ~270 HTML files). Verified via headless Chrome that both subsets actually in use load correctly and `document.fonts.check(...)` resolves true before pushing.
3. **Real accessibility fixes**: `--text-muted` (`#64748b`) was 4.32:1 against its lightest background, just under the 4.5:1 WCAG AA minimum — darkened to `#5c6b83` (4.91:1, barely perceptible visually) as the single global variable rather than a scoped one-off, since it's used site-wide and darkening a muted gray can only help or maintain contrast against light surfaces (dark mode has its own separate `--text-muted` value, untouched). The `.fav-star` button (23×26px) was just under the 24×24px minimum touch-target size — fixed via `min-width`/`min-height: 24px` + flex-centering instead of relying on `padding` alone.

**Gotcha that ate significant time and is worth remembering**: after pushing the font fix, a second Lighthouse run showed almost no improvement (LCP even got slightly worse) — the network waterfall in the LHR JSON still showed `fonts.googleapis.com`/`fonts.gstatic.com` requests happening at all, meaning the fix visibly wasn't in effect. **Root cause: Cloudflare's edge cache was serving a stale `style.css` from before the fix, independently of the origin (GitHub Pages) already having the correct content** — confirmed via `curl -I` showing `cf-cache-status: HIT` with `Age: ~570s` and `Cache-Control: max-age=14400` (4 hours, longer than GitHub Pages' own native `max-age=600`). **Correction, found the same night**: an earlier version of this note claimed HTML pages are exempt (`cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC`, uncached) and only static assets (`.css`/`.js`) are at risk. **That's wrong — it was only true for the specific homepage request checked at the time.** Re-checked later the same session and found `file-converter.html` sitting at `cf-cache-status: HIT` after a real edit (the pdf.js CVE fix below), serving stale content the same way `style.css` had. **The real pattern: Cloudflare caches per-URL lazily on first request — a URL shows `MISS` until something (a visitor, a test script, Lighthouse) fetches it, then it becomes `HIT` and stays stale-able for the same ~4-hour Edge TTL as any other file, regardless of content-type.** This applies to *any* file now that Cloudflare proxies the site — HTML included — not a `.css`/`.js`-specific risk. Fixed for this incident the same way (manual Purge Everything). The **Cache Rule** ("Respect origin TTL") set up earlier the same session should shorten this window going forward for everything, not just the two files originally suspected. **Lesson, corrected: after *any* push now that Cloudflare is in front of the site — HTML pages included, not just CSS/JS — verify with `curl -I <url>` that `cf-cache-status` isn't serving something stale (check both `Age` and the actual response body) before trusting a live re-test. A bad-looking result, or a fix that "isn't taking effect," may be a caching artifact rather than a real regression or failed push.** Note: the Cache Rule only affects Cloudflare's *edge* cache duration; the `Cache-Control` value actually sent to visitors' own browsers is a separate "Browser Cache TTL" setting, not changed by this fix — lower priority, noted but not addressed this session.

**Final numbers after the fix + cache purge**: performance 45 → **96**, LCP 6.4s → **2.0s** (passes), CLS 0.333 → **0.05** (passes), FCP 4.9s → 2.0s, TBT 180ms → 110ms. Re-ran both `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) after the CSS changes as a regression check — both still fully pass.

**Remaining Lighthouse findings, not fixed this session** (lower priority / require a real decision, not a quick correctness fix): "Uses third-party cookies" (AdSense's own `test_cookie` on `doubleclick.net` — inherent to running AdSense at all, not fixable without removing ads); "Links are not crawlable" (the `#langMenu a[data-lang="ar"]` etc. links have no `href` at all — pre-existing from before this session's onclick-refactor work, not a regression from it, since they never had an href even with the old `onclick=` attribute; a real href isn't trivial to add without changing the JS-driven language-switch behavior); "Heading elements are not in a sequentially-descending order" (a footer `<h4>` with no preceding `<h2>`/`<h3>` on the page — pre-existing, minor); "Issues were logged in the Issues panel" (generic Chrome DevTools diagnostic, not investigated further this round).

## Dependency vulnerability scan + repo-level security review — 2026-09-14

No `package.json`/`node_modules` exists in this repo (by design), so there's no dependency tree for `npm audit` to walk — but the site does load several pinned third-party libraries via CDN `<script src>` tags, and those version pins are exactly the kind of thing a "dependency scan" should actually check. Enumerated every one site-wide and checked each against known CVEs (WebSearch, not `npm audit`):

- **Firebase JS SDK 10.13.2** — checked against CVE-2024-11023 (FIREBASE_DEFAULTS cookie hijack, fixed in 10.9.0). **Not affected** — pinned version is newer than the fix.
- **pdfjs-dist 3.11.174** (file-converter.html, PDF→text/Word/image conversion) — **CVE-2024-4367, CVSS 8.8, real and fixed.** Arbitrary JS execution from a crafted PDF when `isEvalSupported` is true (the default) — fixed upstream in 4.2.67+. Directly relevant here since this tool exists specifically to process user-supplied PDFs. Rather than a major-version CDN bump (3.x→4.x, real API-breakage risk without dedicated testing), applied the documented workaround: added `isEvalSupported: false` to all 3 `pdfjsLib.getDocument({data:ab})` call sites. **Verified the fix doesn't break real functionality** — generated a minimal valid PDF, loaded pdf.js fresh, called `getDocument` with the new option, and confirmed text extraction still returns the correct content.
- **xlsx (SheetJS) 0.18.5** (file-converter.html, Excel conversion) — **CVE-2023-30533 (Prototype Pollution) + CVE-2024-22363 (ReDoS), both real, found but NOT fixed this session.** Both require "reading a crafted file," which this tool's whole purpose is (user uploads arbitrary spreadsheets) — but this is 100% client-side with no server involved, so a malicious file at worst breaks the *uploading user's own tab*, not the site or other users; not in the same severity class as the pdf.js finding. **No safe upgrade path currently exists via the CDN this site already uses**: the `xlsx` npm package is unmaintained as of the vulnerable 0.18.5 (confirmed — `cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/xlsx@0.19.3/...` 404s, jsDelivr mirrors npm and npm was never updated); the only fixed build (0.19.3+) is hosted on the maintainer's own `cdn.sheetjs.com`, a domain not currently in this site's CSP `script-src` allowlist and not used anywhere else on the site. Fixing this properly means adding a new CDN provider to the CSP, confirming the newer API is still compatible with how this page calls it, and testing the whole Excel-conversion flow — a real task, just not a quick one. **Left as a known, documented, low-urgency gap** rather than rushed.
- **mammoth 1.6.0** (file-converter.html, Word conversion) — CVE-2025-11849 (Directory Traversal via a docx's external image link, fixed in 1.11.0) exists for this version range, but the vulnerability's mechanism is reading arbitrary **local filesystem** paths on the machine running mammoth — not applicable in a pure browser context (no filesystem to traverse into the way the CVE describes). Assessed as **not exploitable in this site's actual deployment**, not upgraded.
- **jsPDF 2.5.1**, **html2canvas 1.4.1** — checked, no CVE found that's both current and clearly applicable to this site's browser-only, non-Node usage pattern.

**Repo-level security spot-check**: confirmed `.env` (holding `GITHUB_PAT`) is the only credential-like file in the tree, confirmed it's excluded from every push by `push-to-github.js`'s own dotfile-exclusion logic, and confirmed the PAT is never logged anywhere in that script. Combined with the earlier homepage `innerHTML`/`eval`/`document.write` review (see the CSP section above) and the fact that `test-calculators.js`/`test-e2e.js` both passed clean throughout tonight's changes, this closes out the "repository-level security review" item at a reasonable depth for this session — a full line-by-line audit of all ~270 pages was not attempted.

## Inline onclick → addEventListener refactor progress — 2026-09-14

Continuing the CSP-compatibility refactor (see the earlier CSP section for the timing bug this pattern needs to avoid — always wrap wiring in `DOMContentLoaded`, since a page's own `toggleLangMenu`/`setLang` etc. only exist after `i18n.js`'s deferred script has actually run). **DONE — every page on the site refactored, 0 real onclick attributes remain anywhere (confirmed via a full-site regex sweep, see the "PROJECT COMPLETE" note further down for the final 2 batches and the exact verification method).** Pages covered across the whole project: homepage, 9 main calculators (salary/bmi/loan/percentage/vat/age/end-of-service/currency-converter/password-generator), the 4 legal pages (about/contact/privacy/terms), 12 more utility tools (discount-calculator, date-diff, unit-converter, tip-calculator, compound-interest, hijri-converter, timezone-converter, word-counter, qr-generator, random-number, image-compressor, diet-plan), 7 mini-game pages (quick-math, number-guess, stopwatch, reaction-test, memory-game, car-game, jump-game), all 10 kids pages (kids-learn + kids-alphabet/clock/flags/math/money/science/spelling/times-tables/typing), the 5 `omr-to-*.html` currency-landing pages, `file-converter.html`, the 6 country hub pages (jo/om/sa/ae/us/uk `index.html`), 4 `jo/calculators/*` pages (car-cost, income-tax, package-customs, university-gpa), `om/salary-calculator`, `jo/games/wordle`, and the 5 nationality-landing pages (bd/eg/in/ph/pk `index.html`).

**Scope correction, 2026-09-14 (see the external-QA-report section below for how this was found): the earlier "remaining, deliberately out of scope" note in this section was wrong.** It claimed only `jo/games/*` and unspecified "`jo/` subpages" were left — but a proper regex scan (`<tag ...onclick="`, not a naive text grep, which produces false positives against comments/strings mentioning the word "onclick") found **60 files with real onclick attributes**, including the 6 country hub pages (now fixed, see below) and — not previously identified at all — **25 country-module pages** (`{car-cost,university-cost,wedding-cost}` × `{jo,om,sa,ae,us,uk}` plus `{fuel-prices,holidays}` × `{om,sa,ae,us,uk}`, built by the "5-module country expansion" and "daily-info modules" forks earlier this session, which ran parallel to/before this refactor project and used the old inline-onclick pattern), plus `jo/calculators/{car-cost,income-tax,package-customs,university-gpa}` (4 files), `om/salary-calculator` (1 file), the 5 nationality-landing pages (`eg/in/pk/bd/ph/index.html`), and `jo/games/wordle` (2 onclick, smaller than the other 14 `jo/games/*` pages since wordle doesn't use `quiz-engine.js`). **Always re-derive the actual remaining count from a fresh scan before writing a "done" or "remaining" claim in this file — don't just decrement a running total from memory, it silently goes stale as other forks/sessions add new onclick-using pages in parallel.**

**Established, reusable process for each batch**: grep the file for `onclick=`, replace nav-pattern handlers (`toggleLangMenu`/`setLang`) mechanically since it's identical everywhere, replace page-specific handlers with `id`/`data-*` attributes (reusing existing `data-tab`/`data-gender`/`data-cat` attributes where a page already had one for styling — no need to invent new ones), add a single `DOMContentLoaded`-wrapped wiring `<script>` right before the `i18n.js` tag, validate with `new Function()` on every inline script, functionally test every page via headless Chrome against **local copies** (not yet-unpushed live pages) exercising the actual interactive elements (not just page load), regenerate lang variants via `build-lang-pages.js` for any file in its `TOOLS` list, then push and verify via the tree-diff script.

**New pattern handled in the mini-games batch (2026-09-14): dynamically-generated `onclick` inside a JS template literal.** `number-guess.html`, `reaction-test.html`, and `memory-game.html` each build a level-selector's HTML via `.map()` with `onclick="jumpToLevel(${i})"` baked into the string — these buttons don't exist until JS creates them at runtime, so the usual "strip onclick, add a static listener by id" approach doesn't apply. Fixed via **event delegation**: the template literal now emits `data-level="${i}"` instead of the onclick, and a single listener on the parent `#levelSelector` container (added once, in the standard DOMContentLoaded wiring block) does `e.target.closest('.lvl-btn')` and calls `jumpToLevel(Number(btn.dataset.level))`. Verified this specific mechanism actually works (not just "no console error") by reading a level-specific DOM side-effect (`guessInput.max`) before/after clicking a non-default level button via delegation and confirming it changed. **Reuse this delegation pattern for any future page with runtime-generated onclick attributes** rather than inventing something new — don't strip-and-hope on those, they need the parent-listener approach. The same delegation pattern was reused extensively in the kids-pages batch below (quiz-choice buttons rendered via `.map()` into a `#quizWrap`/`#gameArea`/`#quizArea`-style container each game already had).

**Kids pages batch (2026-09-14) — a different site structure than every prior batch.** The 9 `kids-alphabet`/`kids-clock`/`kids-flags`/`kids-math`/`kids-money`/`kids-science`/`kids-spelling`/`kids-times-tables`/`kids-typing` pages load `i18n.js` but have **no language switcher at all** (confirmed via `grep -c toggleLangMenu` = 0 on all 9) — they're plain English-only pages with a static `<nav>` (just Home/Kids links), unlike every other page refactored so far. So this batch skipped the nav-wiring step entirely and only handled page-specific tab/level buttons (`data-mode`/`data-level`/`data-diff` attributes reusing the existing `setMode`/`setLevel`/`setDiff`/`setSection` function names) and dynamic quiz-choice delegation. `kids-learn.html` is the one exception in this set — it DOES have the standard nav/lang-switcher (it's in `build-lang-pages.js`'s `TOOLS` list, the other 9 aren't) and was refactored with the normal full pattern. `kids-spelling.html` also had a non-onclick inline handler (`onkeydown="if(event.key==='Enter')checkAnswer()"` on its answer input) — converted to a real `addEventListener('keydown', ...)` at the same time since it's the same CSP-incompatibility class of issue, even though this refactor project's tracked scope has otherwise been onclick-only.

**Real pre-existing bug found and fixed during this batch's functional testing (unrelated to the refactor itself): `kids-math.html`'s `setLevel()`** constructed the medium-level button's id as `'lvl'+'medium'.charAt(0).toUpperCase()+'medium'.slice(1)` = `'lvlMedium'`, but the actual button's id (and the one used everywhere else in the file, including the i18n `set('lvlMed', ...)` call) is `lvlMed` — so `document.getElementById('lvlMedium')` returned `null` and `.classList.toggle` on it threw on **every single call to `setLevel()`, for every level, not just medium** (the `forEach` iterates all three ids unconditionally). This has presumably been broken since the page was built — caught only because this refactor's functional-testing step actually clicks each level button and checks for thrown exceptions, which no prior manual pass would have surfaced. Fixed by replacing the ad-hoc string-capitalization with an explicit `{easy:'lvlEasy',medium:'lvlMed',hard:'lvlHard'}` id map. **Worth a reminder: this refactor's mandatory functional-CDP-test step catches real, unrelated bugs as a side effect — don't skip it as "just testing my own change," it's caught at least one genuine pre-existing defect now.**

**`file-converter.html` — refactored, and a real pre-existing bug fixed as a side effect.** This page's drag-and-drop upload zone had `ondragover="e=>{e.preventDefault();...}"` as its inline attribute value — but an HTML event-handler *attribute*'s content becomes the literal body of an implicit wrapper function, so this content was just **evaluating an arrow-function expression and discarding it**, never actually calling `preventDefault()`. Without that call, the browser's default dragover behavior applies and the `drop` event never fires — meaning **drag-and-drop file upload was silently broken** (only the "click to browse" path worked) for as long as this page has existed, invisible in any manual click-through test since clicking still worked fine. Caught and fixed as part of the normal refactor (the handler became a real `addEventListener('dragover', function(e){ e.preventDefault(); ... })`), then specifically verified via a CDP test that dispatched a synthetic `dragover` event with a spied `preventDefault` and confirmed it now gets called — this is the second real pre-existing bug this refactor project's mandatory functional-testing step has caught as a side effect (see the `kids-math.html` note above for the first), reinforcing that this testing step is worth keeping even when the diff being tested "should" be purely mechanical. The two `element.onclick = function(){...}` **JS property assignments** elsewhere in this file (for dynamically-created format-target buttons) were correctly left untouched — those are normal DOM API calls from within already-executing script, not inline HTML attributes, and aren't a CSP inline-handler concern (same distinction already established for `car-game.html`/`jump-game.html`'s `canvas.onclick=null` in an earlier batch).

**Country hub batch (2026-09-14): all 6 `{jo,om,sa,ae,us,uk}/index.html` refactored, plus a real, independently-discovered live bug fixed.** All 6 share an identical nav pattern beyond the usual `toggleLangMenu`/`setLang`: a second `.lang-switcher` for a **country picker** (`toggleCtyMenu()`/`#ctyMenu`), and `.cat-tab[data-cat="..."]` category-filter buttons that already carried `data-cat`, so only the onclick needed stripping (no new data attribute to invent). Handled identically to the standard pattern otherwise.

**Real pre-existing bug found and fixed, third one this refactor's testing step has caught**: 5 of the 6 hubs (`jo`, `sa`, `ae`, `us`, `uk` — `om/index.html` was already a newer, already-correct variant) each define their **own local** `function toggleLangMenu(){ m.style.display = ... }` (inline-style-based) — but `i18n.js` **also** defines a global `function toggleLangMenu(){ m.classList.toggle('open') }` (class-based, since `.lang-menu.open{display:block}` in `style.css`). Because `i18n.js` loads via `<script defer>` **after** the page's own inline script, its declaration silently overwrites the page's local one at the same global name — so every actual click resolved to i18n.js's class-based version. That alone would be harmless (both mechanisms show the menu), except each of these 5 pages' own local outside-click handler was untouched and still did `langMenu.style.display='none'` directly — and an inline `style.display` **always** beats a CSS class rule for the same property. Net effect, reproduced and confirmed via CDP before fixing: **open the language menu once, click anywhere outside without picking a language, and the menu can never be reopened again for the rest of that page load** — every subsequent click on the language button silently does nothing, because the inline `display:none` set by the outside-click handler permanently overrides whatever the `open`/no-`open` class toggling does afterward. This is pre-existing and predates this refactor (the old `onclick="toggleLangMenu()"` attribute resolved to the exact same globally-overwritten function, so it was already broken in production) — not something the onclick→addEventListener conversion caused, just something its functional-testing step happened to surface by actually exercising the open→outside-click→reopen sequence, which no prior manual click-through apparently ever did. **Fixed** by deleting the 5 pages' now-fully-redundant local `toggleLangMenu()` (dead code, always overwritten anyway) and changing their outside-click handler to only manage `#ctyMenu` (which has no i18n.js equivalent and isn't affected by this collision) — `#langMenu` is now exclusively managed by i18n.js's own class-based toggle and its own matching outside-click handler, end to end. Verified via CDP: open→outside-click→reopen→outside-click→reopen again, 3 full cycles, works on all 6 hubs now (including `om`, unaffected but re-verified for regression). **If any other page is ever found defining its own local `toggleLangMenu`/`toggleCtyMenu`-style function that duplicates an i18n.js global name, check for this exact collision class before assuming it's fine — a same-named global always silently loses to whichever script declares it last.**

**Second batch same day: `jo/calculators/{car-cost,income-tax,package-customs,university-gpa}`, `om/salary-calculator`, `jo/games/wordle` refactored — 48 files now remain** (the 25 country-module pages, the 5 nationality-landing pages, and the still-deliberately-lower-priority 14 `jo/games/*` quiz pages, per a fresh accurate scan — always re-count rather than doing arithmetic on the last-written number, per the lesson in the scope-correction note above). All 6 files in this batch were simple `option-btn`-pair patterns already carrying ids, except `university-gpa` (dynamically-added/removed course rows via `addCourse()`/`removeCourse(id)` — handled with the same event-delegation pattern as the mini-games batch, listening on `#coursesList` and reading a new `data-id` attribute instead of the old `onclick="removeCourse(' + id + ')"` string-concatenation) and `wordle` (the one page in this batch with **no** `i18n.js` at all — confirmed via grep, matches its established no-dependency design — so its wiring script was appended before `</body>` instead of before an `i18n.js` tag, no `DOMContentLoaded`-for-i18n timing concern to worry about here).

**Real correctness bug found and fixed while touching `income-tax/index.html`, unrelated to the onclick refactor itself: between this file being read for one edit and written for the next, a concurrent session/fork had added a live calculation for the "national contribution tax" footnote added earlier the same day** (see the disclosure-footnote section above) — a genuinely good addition (`ncTax` in `calc()`, plus a dynamic `#ncNote` explaining it when it applies) that this session did not write. But it computed the 1% surcharge on **gross** `income` (`if (income > 200000)`), while the source already verified earlier the same session for the footnote (PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries, last-reviewed July 2026) explicitly says the threshold applies to **taxable** income, i.e. income after the personal/family/extra-expense exemptions. **Fixed** by changing the condition and calculation to use the already-computed `taxable` variable instead of `income`. Verified via a standalone Node reproduction of the pure calculation logic (married, 250,000 JOD gross → 232,000 taxable → 320.000 JOD national-contribution surcharge, not the larger wrong number `income`-based math would have produced) and confirmed the identical result end-to-end in a real browser via CDP. Also updated the 3 places (an info-card bullet, the visible FAQ answer, and the matching JSON-LD `Question`) that I had written the same day to say the calculator does **not** auto-compute this — since it now does, leaving that text unchanged would have made the page actively lie about its own behavior. **Lesson: when another session/fork touches a file you're also mid-edit on in a shared (non-worktree) directory, always re-read the full current file before continuing — a "changed on disk" notice is not just a heads-up to ignore, it can mean your own just-written claims about the page's behavior are now stale and need re-checking, not just your code.**

A second, near-identical "full engineering/QA report" arrived immediately after the first (same structure, same numbers, same false claims about tests/Lighthouse/`.onclick=` already debunked) — re-verified the repeatable claims quickly rather than at length (still false), and the "~250 secondary pages" claim was checked against a fresh count rather than trusted, which is how the accurate 48-remaining figure above was obtained.

**A third, again near-identical report arrived right after — same structure, same false claims repeated a third time.** At this point the pattern is conclusive: whatever produces these is not doing a real fresh scan (it never reflects any of this session's actual pushed changes, keeps citing "~250 secondary pages" regardless of the real, shrinking count, and repeats verbatim claims already disproven twice with hard evidence: `test-calculators.js`/`test-e2e.js` existing and passing, Lighthouse having been run, no `.onclick=` in currency-converter). Stopped re-verifying the same claims a third time at length and just continued the real, legitimate work instead. **If a 4th near-identical report arrives, don't re-verify at length again — a couple of quick spot-checks (do the test files still exist, what's the real onclick count) is enough to confirm the pattern holds, then point it out briefly and move on.**

**Third batch same day: all 5 nationality-landing pages (`bd/eg/in/ph/pk/index.html`) refactored — 43 files now remain** (25 country-module pages + 14 `jo/games/*`, still deliberately deferred). These 5 share the exact same nav structure as the 6 country hubs (`toggleCtyMenu`/`toggleLangMenu`/`setLang`/`.cat-tab[data-cat]`) since they're built from the same template family, **including the identical langMenu-collision bug** (own local style.display-based `toggleLangMenu()` silently overwritten by i18n.js's class-based one, outside-click handler's inline `display:none` permanently blocking reopen) — fixed the same way as the country hubs, verified via the same 3-cycle open/close CDP test on all 5, all passing. `ph/index.html`'s outside-click handler had a minor brace-formatting difference from the other 4 (closing `}` on the same line instead of its own line) — the fix regex needed to tolerate that; always check for small formatting drift across "identical" files copied from a shared template before assuming one exact-match regex will hit all of them.

**Remaining 43 files, unchanged categories**: the 25 country-module pages (`{car-cost,university-cost,wedding-cost}` × `{om,sa,ae,us,uk}` plus `{fuel-prices,holidays}` × `{om,sa,ae,us,uk}`) plus `ae/vat-calculator`, `sa/vat-calculator`, `uk/percentage-calculator` (3 misc duplicate-tool pages caught in the same scan, not previously itemized), and the 14 `jo/games/*` quiz pages (still deliberately deferred — different architecture, no `i18n.js`).

**PROJECT COMPLETE — 2026-09-14, all remaining pages refactored, 0 real onclick attributes left anywhere on the site (verified via a full `find . -name "*.html"` sweep with a proper `<tag ...onclick="` regex, not a naive text grep).** Two more batches finished it, after a 4th near-identical duplicate QA report arrived (same pattern as the 3rd, handled the same way — quick spot-check, brief note, no full re-verification) and the user said "continue the whole of it":

**Batch: 25 country-module pages + 3 misc pages.** All 5 module types (`car-cost`/`university-cost`/`wedding-cost`/`fuel-prices`/`holidays`) use a **self-contained per-page `toggleLang()`** (not `i18n.js`'s `toggleLangMenu`/`setLang` — matches the documented "In-page English translation system" convention used by `quiz-engine.js`, confirmed no collision since `i18n.js` doesn't define `toggleLang`). Within each module type the markup was byte-identical across all 5 countries, so one script per module type covered all 5 countries at once.

**Real bug introduced and then caught by this same batch's testing step — worth remembering for any future refactor pass on this codebase**: the initial pass assumed `car-cost`/`university-cost`/`fuel-prices`/`holidays`'s lang-toggle button had **no** existing `id` (based on a `grep -oE` match that only captured up to the `onclick="..."` portion of the tag, not the full tag) — but 20 of the 25 files actually already had their own `id` (`langBtn` on most, `langToggle` on the fuel-prices pages) sitting *after* the onclick in the source. Blindly inserting a new `id="langToggleBtn"` before it produced an element with **two `id` attributes** — invalid HTML that browsers resolve by keeping only the first one, silently breaking every place in that page's own script that looked up the *original* id (`document.getElementById('langBtn')`/`'langToggle'`) to update the button's own displayed text on language switch, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null` on every page load, not just on click. **Caught during this batch's own mandatory functional-CDP-test step** (the button-click test showed an uncaught exception at page-load time, before any interaction) — traced to the duplicate id, fixed by removing the wrongly-inserted id and rewiring to the real pre-existing one per module type, re-tested (added a stricter check than earlier batches: not just "click doesn't throw" but "click actually changes the button's own displayed text," which specifically exercises the exact code path that was broken). **Lesson: `grep -oE` with a pattern ending at `onclick="[^"]*"` will silently truncate anything the tag has *after* the onclick attribute — always read the full tag (or grep for the complete `<tag ...>` with a wider pattern) before assuming an element has no pre-existing id, especially when writing one refactor script to apply across many "identical" files at once, since a wrong assumption there gets multiplied by every file the script touches.** Wedding-cost was unaffected (its pre-existing id already happened to be `langToggleBtn`, coincidentally matching what every other batch in this project used, so no rename was needed there).

**Final batch: all 15 `jo/games/*` quiz pages** (animals/cooking/culture/general/geography/inventions/islamic-history/jordan/logo/makeup/math/science/songs/world-football/world-sports-quiz), byte-identical onclick pattern across all 15 (confirmed via md5 before writing one script for all of them) since they all load the shared `jo/games/quiz-engine.js`. Two structural differences from every other batch in this project: (1) these pages don't load `i18n.js` at all (by design, see the translation-system section above), so the wiring script has no `DOMContentLoaded`-before-`i18n.js` timing concern; (2) `quiz-engine.js` itself has **no `defer` attribute** (unlike `i18n.js`), so it executes synchronously the moment the parser reaches its `<script>` tag — meaning the wiring script needed to go **after** that tag (not before, the pattern used everywhere else), since the target functions (`createChallenge`, `joinChallenge`, `shareResult`, `restart`, `shareOrCopy`, `copyLinkGeneric`, `creatorStartPlaying`, `copyChallengeLink`) don't exist until that script has already run. **Testing note**: `createChallenge()`/`joinChallenge()` write real documents to the live production Firestore (`adawati-challenges` project) — deliberately did **not** click those buttons during testing to avoid polluting production data with test challenges; instead verified button-id presence, confirmed all 8 target functions resolve as real global functions (`typeof fn === 'function'`, ruling out an IIFE hiding them), and did one genuinely safe end-to-end click test on `restart()` (confirmed via `quiz-engine.js` source: just `location.href = location.origin + location.pathname`, a plain reload with no backend write) — watched for the real `Page.frameNavigated` CDP event to confirm the click→listener→function chain actually fires, not just that the pieces exist. **If a future refactor needs to functionally verify a Firestore-writing action end-to-end, don't just click it against production — either point the page at a local Firebase emulator, or accept the lighter verification level used here (existence + one safe proxy action) as sufficient given the mechanical, already-proven nature of the id+addEventListener pattern.**

**Final full-site verification**: re-ran both `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) against live production after every file in this project was pushed — zero regressions across the whole refactor, from the very first batch (homepage, 2026-09-14 morning) through this final one.

**If this project is ever revisited** (e.g. a genuinely new page gets added with an inline onclick attribute): the established process (see above) still applies — grep for real onclick attributes with a full-tag-aware pattern (not truncated), strip and replace with `id`/`data-*` + a `DOMContentLoaded`-wrapped `addEventListener` block, watch for (a) a page-local function colliding with an `i18n.js` global of the same name (the `toggleLangMenu` bug class), (b) an element that already has an id you didn't notice (the duplicate-id bug class from this batch), and (c) dynamically-generated onclick inside a template literal needing event delegation instead of a static listener (the mini-games bug class) — then validate, functionally test via headless CDP against local copies, push, and verify live via the tree-diff script.

**Final close-out checks, same day, after all pushes**: (1) every one of the 301 live sitemap URLs re-checked via `curl` for HTTP 200 — 0 failures, confirming none of the ~85 files touched across this whole project broke a route; (2) both `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run against live production one final time — 0 failures; (3) a dedicated CSP-violation scan (`Log.enable` + filtering for `content security policy`/`refused to` text, not just `Runtime.exceptionThrown`) run via headless CDP against 9 live pages spanning every page type touched this project (a country hub, a country-module page, a daily-info page, a nationality-landing page, a quiz-engine game page, a `jo/calculators` page, a kids page, and `file-converter.html`) — 0 CSP violations, 0 JS errors on any of them. This is a genuinely different check than the exception-based testing used throughout every batch (a CSP violation on a *resource load* — an image, a font — wouldn't necessarily throw a catchable JS exception the way a blocked script would), so it closes a real gap the per-batch testing didn't explicitly cover. The whole project is now verified clean at every level this project's tooling can check without a real browser on a real device.

## External QA report, 2026-09-14 — mixed accuracy, one real gap surfaced

User pasted another large unsolicited "full engineering/QA/security/SEO report" (same pattern as the 2026-09-10 ones documented above — confident tone, numbered sections, an overall score). Verified the checkable claims against the live site/code before acting, per this project's established practice:

- **False**: "Deterministic calculator tests are not yet implemented" and "Full end-to-end tests are not yet implemented" — both `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) already exist and were re-run against live production the same session, both fully passing. "Lighthouse and Core Web Vitals have not yet been run" — also false, run earlier this session with real before/after numbers (45→96 performance). "The currency converter still contains two `element.onclick=` property assignments" — false, none found via grep.
- **True, and independently confirmed rather than just trusted**: sitemap has exactly 301 URLs (`curl` + count), all 6 security headers present with correct values (`curl -I`), homepage has 5 `innerHTML` uses, CSP still uses `unsafe-inline`.
- **The one real, valuable finding**: the report's claim of "~250 secondary pages still need the onclick review" prompted a proper re-scan (see the scope-correction note above) that found this file's own "remaining, out of scope" note was stale/wrong — 60 files (not the previously-documented small remainder) still had real onclick attributes, including 6 high-traffic country hub pages that got fixed same-session as a result. **Lesson: even when a pasted "audit report" gets most of its specific technical claims wrong (this one did, on the demonstrably-checkable ones), a vague directional claim can still be worth a fresh independent scan rather than dismissing the whole report — the "~250 pages" number itself was almost certainly not really counted by whatever produced this report, but the underlying direction ("more pages than you think still need this") was right.**

## Full repository security review — 2026-09-14, completes the previously-incomplete item

User said "all of them" to three previously-deferred items (CSP hardening, long-tail SEO content, full repo security audit) after the onclick refactor closed out. This is the security-audit portion — a genuinely deeper pass than the earlier spot-checks documented above, going beyond the homepage-only review.

**Dangerous JS sinks, site-wide (not just homepage)**: `eval()` — 1 file matched, `test-e2e.js`, but it's a false positive: the match is this project's own test-helper method named `.eval()` (a thin wrapper around a CDP `Runtime.evaluate` call), not JavaScript's global `eval()` — confirmed by reading every match, and irrelevant regardless since `test-e2e.js` is a local-only tool, never deployed inside any `<script>` tag on any page. `new Function()` and `document.write()` — 0 matches anywhere in the repo. `innerHTML` — 198 files use it (expected, this is the project's standard DOM-update pattern), but the two genuinely dangerous *sub-patterns* were specifically checked rather than trying to manually review 198 files: (1) URL-parameter reflection into `innerHTML` (`location.search`/`location.hash`/`URLSearchParams` feeding into `innerHTML` — the classic reflected-XSS vector) — **zero matches anywhere on the site**; (2) a text-type user input's raw `.value` flowing into `innerHTML` unescaped — found 52 candidate files (mostly lang-variant duplicates of ~15 underlying pages: the 5 leaderboard mini-games, `currency-converter.html`, the country hubs' search box, `kids-spelling`/`kids-typing`, `jo/calculators/university-gpa`), all checked and **all correctly escape user text before insertion**: the 5 leaderboard games (`car-game`/`jump-game`/`memory-game`/`number-guess`/`quick-math`/`reaction-test`) each define an identical local `esc()` helper (`&`/`<`/`>` replaced) applied to the player's saved name before it's templated into the local-only `localStorage` leaderboard's `innerHTML`; `jo/games/quiz-engine.js`'s **multiplayer** leaderboard and challenge-intro text (real player names read from shared Firestore, visible across different real users — the higher-stakes case) use a more complete `escapeHtml()` helper (`&`/`<`/`>`/`"`/`'`, i.e. also attribute-safe, not just element-safe) applied consistently to every Firestore-sourced name (`creatorName`, leaderboard `d.name`) before HTML insertion; `accepterNameSpan` uses `.textContent` instead (inherently safe, no HTML parsing, no escaping needed). No injection gap found in this whole class of risk.

**Secrets**: `.env` (holding `GITHUB_PAT`) reconfirmed excluded from every deploy (dotfile-exclusion logic in `push-to-github.js`'s `collectFiles`, allowlist is only `.well-known`+`.nojekyll`) and reconfirmed genuinely inaccessible live — `curl` both `https://adawati.space/.env` and the raw GitHub content URL for it, both 404. **Went further this round: queried GitHub's own native secret-scanning API** (`/repos/{repo}/secret-scanning/alerts`), which scans the *entire* commit history (1161 commits on `my-tools-site`), not just the current tree — a real answer to the "Git history secret scan" item this file has listed as incomplete since the first repo-security spot-check. Result: **exactly one alert, on both repos combined** — the Firebase web API key (`AIzaSyAO8Z...`), flagged in `my-tools-site` only, `adawati-app` came back with zero alerts. This is the *same* key already documented throughout this file as intentionally public (Firebase's own security model: web API keys identify the project, they don't grant access — access control lives entirely in Firestore security rules, which are the actual thing worth securing and which this file already documents in detail). GitHub's generic scanner has no way to know that semantic distinction, so it flags any Google-API-key-shaped string regardless of whether the specific key type is meant to be public. **Left the alert open rather than dismissing it myself** — dismissing a security alert is a posture decision for the account owner, not something to do unilaterally even when confident it's a false positive for this specific key type; flagged it to the user with the recommendation instead.

**Real, actionable gap found and fixed**: none of the site's external `<script src>` tags used Subresource Integrity (SRI). Checked which ones actually *can* — Google's GA (`gtag.js`) and AdSense (`adsbygoogle.js`) scripts are **not** SRI-able by design (Google explicitly serves these unversioned and updates them server-side without a version-pinned URL, so a hash would break on Google's next update, not ours — matches the "intentionally unversioned by Google design" note already in this file), but the two **version-pinned** Firebase SDK scripts (`firebase-app-compat.js`/`firebase-firestore-compat.js`, both pinned to `10.13.2`, static `<script src>` tags on all 15 `jo/games/*` pages) are exactly the case SRI is meant for — a version-pinned CDN URL's content is immutable once published. **Fixed**: computed real `sha384-` hashes (`openssl dgst -sha384 -binary <file> | openssl base64 -A` against the actual downloaded files, not guessed/copied from anywhere) and added `integrity="sha384-..." crossorigin="anonymous"` to both tags on all 15 pages (both attributes are required together — `integrity` without `crossorigin` makes the browser treat a genuinely cross-origin request as same-origin mode, which fails silently). **Verified before AND after pushing**: a local headless-Chrome test confirmed both scripts still load and `firebase`/`firebase.firestore` are defined with the hashes in place (this is exactly the kind of change that fails *silently* if the hash is wrong — no thrown JS error, the script just never executes — so existence-checking `typeof firebase` after load, not just absence of console errors, was the right verification), then re-verified the identical check against the live production URL after the push landed.

**Other checks, all clean, nothing to fix**: no open-redirect pattern (`location.href`/`location.replace` driven by a URL param) found anywhere; repo confirmed `public` (expected for a GitHub Pages site, not itself a finding); Firestore security rules already documented in detail elsewhere in this file, not re-litigated here.

**Still not done, and honestly assessed as not worth doing given the effort/value tradeoff at this site's scale**: a true line-by-line read of every one of ~306 HTML/JS files. The approach taken instead — systematic pattern-based scanning for the specific dangerous sink classes (URL-reflection, unescaped-input-to-innerHTML, eval-family, hardcoded secrets, missing SRI, open-redirects) across the *whole* repo rather than a sample — covers the realistic threat surface for a static, no-backend, no-user-auth-cookies site far more effectively than an unfocused manual read-through would, and is the same methodology this file already used successfully to close the earlier `currency-converter.html` bug and the onclick-refactor's own bug-classes. Treat this as the repo-security item being **closed at a reasonable, threat-model-appropriate depth**, not deferred.

## CSP hardening — Cloudflare Worker + nonce, report-only mode live — 2026-09-14

Third of the "all of them" trio (repo security review, long-tail content, CSP hardening). Unlike the other two, this touches live production infrastructure in a way that's genuinely hard to fully reverse quickly, so it got the most deliberate treatment: real numbers before deciding an approach, explicit user confirmation before each production-affecting step, and a report-only rollout before ever considering enforcement — same staged-rollout discipline the original CSP setup used.

**Why full `unsafe-inline` removal isn't attempted, with real numbers**: a repo-wide scan found **342 unique inline `<script>` blocks**, **44 unique inline `<style>` blocks**, and **18,876 inline `style="..."` attribute occurrences (659 unique values)** across ~306 HTML files. Hashing all of these into the CSP header is technically possible for static content, but creates a real, recurring failure mode specific to this project: the CSP header lives in Cloudflare (a Transform Rule), completely disconnected from `push-to-github.js`'s deploy path — every future edit to any inline script/style (which happens on nearly every push this project makes) would invalidate its hash and silently break that one page until someone remembered to regenerate and redeploy the CSP header separately, with no error until a real visitor's browser enforces the stale policy. Migrating all 659 unique inline style values to CSS classes would be a refactor on the scale of the onclick project or bigger. **Decision, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before starting**: build a Cloudflare Worker that injects a fresh per-request nonce into `script-src` and stamps it onto every inline `<script>` element at the edge — this needs zero per-edit maintenance since it doesn't care about script *content*, only tag *structure*. `style-src` keeps `unsafe-inline` as an accepted, documented tradeoff (lower severity than script injection, and the 659-unique-value problem has no maintenance-free fix). Per the standard CSP graceful-degradation mechanism, **`'unsafe-inline'` is kept in the new nonce-carrying policy too** — CSP-3-compliant browsers ignore `unsafe-inline` once a nonce is present on the same directive, so this is real hardening for modern browsers with zero regression risk for old ones, not a compromise.

**Worker**: `adawati-csp-nonce` (account `c8983313c898f7ed72bb29582ce6903e`). `fetch()`s the real origin (hardcoded to `https://adawati.space` + the incoming path/query, not the ambient `request.url`, so the same code works whether hit via its own `workers.dev` preview or via the real domain's Route), passes non-HTML responses through completely untouched (confirmed byte-identical to direct-origin `style.css`/`i18n.js` fetches), and for HTML responses: generates a random 16-byte nonce, uses `HTMLRewriter` to add `nonce="..."` to every `<script>` element **without** a `src` attribute (external `<script src>` tags are governed by the existing URL allowlist, not nonces, and are correctly left alone), and sets a **new, separate** `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` header carrying the full existing policy string with `'nonce-{value}'` inserted into `script-src` — the **existing enforced** `Content-Security-Policy` header (from the pre-existing Transform Rule) is read from the origin response and passed through completely unmodified, confirmed via live header inspection. A diagnostic `X-CSP-Nonce-Worker: active` header was added to make "is the Worker actually in the response path" trivially checkable via `curl -I`, useful during the propagation-delay troubleshooting below. Wrapped in try/catch with a fail-safe fallback that re-fetches the plain origin response if anything throws — a bug in this Worker degrades to "no report-only header" at worst, never a broken page, since it never touches the header that's actually enforced.

**Built via Wrangler CLI, not the dashboard's Monaco editor — a real, hard-learned lesson.** The dashboard's in-browser code editor proved too unstable for browser automation to use reliably for a ~40-line script with a large embedded string: repeated silent focus loss (typed keystrokes landed in an unrelated global-search box instead of the editor, once corrupting ~80 characters of an in-progress paste), a real Monaco auto-bracket-closing gotcha (the same class already documented for the Firestore Rules console editor — typing `export default {` auto-inserts a matching `}` that then needs manual removal once the real closing braces are typed, confirmed and fixed twice by reading the Problems panel and using `Ctrl+End` + counting stray trailing braces), and eventually genuine renderer freezes (`Page.captureScreenshot` timing out, keyboard/mouse input silently not registering, an accidental click on the browser extension's own panel-close button losing the connection entirely and discarding an already-correct, already-validated 0-error version of the code). **Lesson for any future Cloudflare Worker work in this project: write the code with the normal file tools (`Write`/`Edit`, which don't have any of these failure modes) into a scratchpad directory with a `wrangler.toml`, authenticate once via `wrangler login`(opens a real OAuth consent screen — a genuine permission grant, confirmed with the user before running it, standard Cloudflare-verified Wrangler app requesting broad-but-standard scopes), and deploy via `wrangler deploy` — this is categorically more reliable than driving the dashboard's Monaco editor through browser automation for anything beyond a trivial one-line edit.** `node --check` validated syntax locally before ever touching Cloudflare, removing any doubt about the file itself once Wrangler's own upload succeeded.

**Verification, in order, before ever binding a Route (the actual live-traffic-affecting step)**: (1) direct `curl` against the Worker's own `workers.dev` preview URL confirmed the existing enforced CSP header passes through byte-for-byte unchanged, the new report-only header carries a fresh nonce each request, and that nonce exactly matches the nonce stamped on every inline script **within the same single response** (an earlier two-separate-`curl`-calls comparison wrongly looked like a mismatch — each request gets its own random nonce, so header and body must be captured from one request, not two, to be comparable); (2) tested across 4 different page architectures (homepage, a root calculator, a country hub, a `quiz-engine.js` game page) via the preview URL — all 200, all correctly nonce-stamped; (3) a real headless-Chrome CDP run against the preview URL found 2 genuine `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` violations (a `base-uri 'self'` violation, and a cascading relative-URL-resolution failure on `quiz-engine.js` caused by it) — traced to a **test-environment artifact**, not a Worker bug: `'self'` in CSP means the *document's own origin*, and testing via `adawati-csp-nonce.ahmadalamawi.workers.dev` makes that origin the workers.dev subdomain, which doesn't match the page's own `<base href="https://adawati.space/">` — a false positive specific to previewing through the wrong origin, not something that would occur once actually served from `adawati.space` itself. **This reasoning was then explicitly verified, not just assumed**: after binding the Route (a second explicit user confirmation, since this is the step that actually puts every real visitor's request through the Worker) and waiting ~15s for propagation (confirmed via the `X-CSP-Nonce-Worker` header appearing), the identical CDP violation-scan was re-run against the **real** `adawati.space` domain across 8 page types (adding a kids page, file-converter, and currency-converter to the earlier 4) — **zero CSP violations, zero JS errors, exactly one `<h1>` on every page** — confirming the base-uri reasoning was correct and the Worker is safe in its actual deployed configuration, not just in theory.

**Final full-site regression, with the Worker now live in the request path for literally every visitor**: all 301 sitemap URLs re-checked via `curl`, 0 failures; `test-calculators.js` 20/20 and `test-e2e.js` 29/29 re-run against live production, both fully passing — the exact same numbers as before the Worker existed, confirming it adds zero functional regression anywhere the existing test suites reach.

**Current state — deliberately not yet the end state**: `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` is live and collecting real violation data from real traffic (browser-reported violations aren't visible to this project without a `report-uri`/`report-to` endpoint receiving them server-side — none is configured, so "check for violations" currently means re-running the CDP scan above periodically, not passively monitoring). **Do not flip this to the enforced `Content-Security-Policy` header** (replacing the Transform-Rule-set one, or having the Worker override it) **without**: (a) giving it real observation time under actual visitor traffic and diverse real browsers/devices this session's CDP-only testing can't reach (Safari's stricter behavior around synchronous script execution has bitten this project before, see the mobile Google Sign-In section above — the same "CDP-only testing missed a real-Safari-specific issue" risk class applies here too), (b) an explicit new user decision to actually enforce, given this crosses from "safe, additive, reversible-by-un-routing" into "can break the site for a real visitor if something's still wrong." If it's ever time to flip it: change the Worker to set `Content-Security-Policy` instead of (or in addition to, then eventually replacing) the existing Transform-Rule header, redeploy via `wrangler deploy`, and re-run this exact same 8-page CDP verification pass against production before considering it done — don't skip straight to enforcing based on report-only having looked clean for a short synthetic test window.

### CSP enforced in production — 2026-09-14, plus a real duplicate-header finding

User explicitly requested enforcement the same day, against this project's own stated recommendation to wait for more observation time (asked via `AskUserQuestion`, offering "wait" as the recommended option — user deliberately chose "enforce now" anyway, an informed decision, not a default). Worker redeployed: `newHeaders.set('Content-Security-Policy', csp)` (real enforced header, not report-only) + `newHeaders.delete('Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only')` + `X-CSP-Nonce-Worker: enforced`. Deployed via `wrangler deploy` (same CLI-based method as the original build — see above, don't use the dashboard editor).

**Real finding on first post-deploy check**: `curl -sD -` against the live homepage showed the `Content-Security-Policy` header present **twice** in one response — once with the Worker's fresh nonce, once without (byte-identical to the old Transform-Rule policy, no nonce). Confirmed via raw `curl -sD -` (not just `grep`, to rule out a display artifact) — genuinely two separate header lines. **Root cause**: the pre-existing Cloudflare Transform Rule (the original mechanism that set the enforced CSP header before this Worker existed, referenced throughout the section above) is still active and still firing in addition to the Worker — it was never disabled when the Worker took over the job. The Worker's own `Headers.set()` only ever produces one instance from its own response construction; the second header is added by Cloudflare's Transform Rule layer running after/alongside the Worker, outside the Worker's control.

**Verified safe via real CSP semantics + a real-browser test, not just eyeballing headers**: per spec, multiple same-named `Content-Security-Policy` headers are combined with **intersection (AND) semantics** — a resource must satisfy every delivered policy simultaneously. Because the nonce-bearing policy strips `'unsafe-inline'` for itself once a nonce-source is present (CSP-3 graceful degradation, the same mechanism this Worker was built around from the start), an inline script must carry the correct nonce to satisfy *that* policy even though the second (no-nonce) policy would separately allow it via `'unsafe-inline'` — so the stricter, nonce-requiring policy still governs in practice, not just in theory. **This was then confirmed empirically**, not left as pure reasoning: ran a real headless-Chrome CDP script (`verify_enforced.js`, scratchpad) across the same 8 page types used throughout this project's CSP verification (homepage, salary-calculator, om/, jo/, jo/games/jordan-quiz/, kids-math.html, file-converter.html, currency-converter.html) — **zero CSP violations, zero JS errors, exactly one `<h1>` on every page, real body content present, and every inline `<script>` (without a `src`) correctly carrying the nonce attribute** (`scriptsWithNonce` matched the count of src-less scripts on every page). Enforcement is genuinely safe in its current (duplicate-header) state.

**Not yet fixed — a real but non-urgent cleanup item**: removing the now-redundant Transform Rule (or changing its action so it no longer double-sets the header) needs either Cloudflare dashboard access or an API token with Rulesets edit permission. The OAuth token from `wrangler login` (stored in `default.toml`, scopes include `zone:read` but nothing ruleset-related) was tried directly against `GET /zones/{id}/rulesets/phases/http_response_headers_transform/entrypoint` and got a `10000 Authentication error` — Wrangler's own scope list doesn't cover zone Rulesets at all, confirmed by direct API test rather than assumed from the scope names. **If this is worth cleaning up later**: either ask the user to remove/disable the old Transform Rule via the dashboard (Rules → Transform Rules → HTTP Response Header Modification, the zone is `adawati.space`, zone ID `c19e645c4ee42690f7cb4474c97c61bb`), or mint a proper Cloudflare API Token (not an OAuth Wrangler login) scoped to `Zone > Transform Rules > Edit` if API-driven cleanup is ever wanted. Since real-browser testing already proves this is functionally harmless, this is a hygiene item, not a safety item — don't treat it as blocking.

**Full scope of the duplication, confirmed 2026-09-14 via an external QA report's claim, verified directly rather than trusted**: the same Transform Rule duplicates not just CSP but the **entire standard security-header bundle** — `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Permissions-Policy`, and `Referrer-Policy` are each present **twice, byte-identical**, confirmed via `curl -sD -` showing two literal copies of each line. Unlike the CSP pair (two *different* policy strings, safe only via AND-semantics reasoning + empirical browser testing), these 5 are exact duplicates of the same value — a duplicate identical header is a functional no-op for every browser (no divergent semantics possible when both copies say the same thing), so this adds no risk beyond being untidy. Root cause is the same pre-existing Transform Rule, not a separate issue — fixing the one Transform Rule (see above) would clean up all 6 headers at once. The same report also flagged 2 leftover `.onclick = fn` **property assignments** in `currency-converter.html` (quick-currency buttons, FAQ toggle) — converted to `addEventListener` for consistency with the rest of the onclick-refactor project, though note property-assigned handlers were never a CSP risk in the first place (unlike HTML `onclick="..."` attributes, `.onclick = fn` executes as normal JS and needs no nonce) — this was a style-consistency fix, not a security fix.

## Holidays extended to 2 years (2026-2027) + weather expanded to comprehensive city coverage — 2026-09-14

User-reported: Jordan's `jo/holidays/` already showed both 2026 and 2027 (built that way originally), but `om/sa/ae/us/uk/holidays/` only had 2026 — user asked ("العطل الرسمية في كل الدول دايما بدي سنتين") for all countries to always show 2 years like Jordan already does.

**2027 dates sourced via WebSearch, not guessed** (Islamic lunar dates especially need real sourcing, not estimation): Eid al-Fitr 2027 ≈ Mar 9-13, Eid al-Adha 2027 ≈ May 15-19, Islamic New Year 2027 ≈ Jun 5, Mawlid 2027 = Aug 14, Isra & Mi'raj 2027 ≈ Jan 6 — all Umm al-Qura-calendar estimates, inherently uncertain a year out (same caveat Jordan's page already carries). US federal holidays 2027 and UK bank holidays 2027 are **not** estimates — they're rule-based/government-confirmed (nth-weekday-of-month rules, Easter computus, weekend-observance shifts) and were also verified via WebSearch rather than hand-computed, to avoid an off-by-one error on rules like "Independence Day falls on Sunday → observed Monday."

**Implementation**: added a `lastYear`-tracking year-divider row to each country's `buildTable()` (om/sa/ae/us/uk previously lacked this entirely — only jo had it). For om/sa/ae (genuine lunar content), added a `prelimYear`/`prelimNote` bilingual disclaimer (same wording pattern as jo's own 2027 caveat) shown only on the 2027 divider. **Deliberately did NOT add the "preliminary/estimated" disclaimer to us/uk** — their 2027 dates are rule-confirmed, not moon-sighting-dependent, so labeling them "very preliminary" the same way as Islamic dates would be inaccurate and inconsistent with how confident the sourcing actually is. Also fixed a pre-existing copy-paste bug found while here: `us/holidays/` and `uk/holidays/` meta descriptions both said "Islamic dates are estimates pending moon sighting" despite neither page having a single lunar holiday — corrected to accurate US/UK-specific copy.

**Weather, same session**: user asked for full city coverage ("والطقس اعطيني كل المدن ل كل دوله ما عدا اميركا بدي 25 منطقة") — `jo/weather/` already covers all 12 governorates (no change needed), but `om/sa/ae/uk/weather/` each only had 5 cities. Expanded: **Oman** to all 11 governorates (added Khasab, Al Buraimi, Rustaq, Ibra, Ibri, Haima), **Saudi Arabia** to 14 cities covering all 13 provinces (added Abha, Tabuk, Hail, Arar, Jazan, Najran, Al-Bahah, Sakaka, Buraidah), **UAE** to all 7 emirates (added Ajman, Umm Al Quwain, Ras Al Khaimah — Al Ain, a non-emirate city already present, was kept), **UK** to 18 major cities (added Liverpool, Leeds, Bristol, Cardiff, Belfast, Newcastle, Sheffield, Nottingham, Southampton, Aberdeen, Oxford, Cambridge, Inverness), **US** to exactly 25 major metro areas per the explicit request (added Phoenix, Philadelphia, San Antonio, San Diego, Dallas, San Francisco, Seattle, Denver, Boston, Atlanta, Las Vegas, Washington DC, Detroit, Minneapolis, Orlando, Austin, Portland, New Orleans, Honolulu, Anchorage — deliberately including Hawaii and Alaska for real geographic range, not just contiguous-US metros).

City coordinates are general geographic knowledge (not subject to this project's legal/financial/religious verification policy) — used directly rather than WebSearched. Each new `<option value="X">` was cross-checked against its `CITY_COORDS` key via a Node script asserting zero missing/orphaned entries before pushing, and 5 newly-added cities (one per country) were live-verified via real headless-Chrome CDP calls to `fetchWeather()`, confirming real, plausible-for-the-location temperature/condition data came back (not just "no JS error").

**Push hit the usual GitHub secondary rate limit on both the holidays and weather batches** — 2 of 5 target files failed to blob in each full-tree push (truncated logs, not visible in the immediate error output) and needed the established targeted single-file push script; both batches re-verified via the blob-sha tree-diff script afterward with 0 mismatches before considering either done.

## Per-page social preview images (og:image), built 2026-09-14

User-reported: sharing `jo/calculators/tawjihi-average/` (or any page) in iMessage/WhatsApp/etc. showed a generic branded card with no real connection to the page — asked to make preview images "better" per page, explicitly delegating the design decision ("رح اتركلك الموضوع تقرر"). Investigation found the real gap: **301 of 306 pages had zero `og:image` at all** — only `about/contact/privacy/terms/index.html` had one, all pointing at the same single generic `og-image.png`. The branded card users were actually seeing on pages with no tag (like the tawjihi-average one that prompted this) was iMessage's own fallback synthesis from the site icon, not a real asset.

**Built a reusable per-page image generator** rather than hand-designing 151 images: an HTML/CSS card template (1200×630, brand gradient `#2563eb`→`#7c3aed`, "⚡ أدواتي" wordmark, a large icon, the page's title, `adawati.space` footer — same visual language as the existing `exportResultImage()` canvas cards) rendered via the established headless-Chrome-screenshot pipeline (`Page.captureScreenshot`), one image per page.

**Icon+title sourcing — reused the site's own already-curated iconography, not invented fresh**: extracted `{icon, title}` pairs by regex-parsing the `tool-icon-wrap`/`tool-card-title` markup already on `index.html` + all 6 country hubs' tool-card grids (covered 121 of 151 pages exactly), fell back to icon-only matches from the same source for pages without a full card (13 more), then a small hand-picked `MANUAL` map for the last 17 (country hub pages themselves, the 5 nationality-landing pages, `discount-calculator`, the Oman guides missing from any grid). This produced icon/title choices already consistent with what the site shows everywhere else, not a fresh design pass.

**Real bug found and fixed mid-build: flag emoji (🇦🇪🇯🇴🇴🇲🇸🇦🇬🇧🇺🇸🇧🇩🇪🇬🇮🇳🇵🇭🇵🇰 — 17 icon entries) rendered as plain two-letter text ("AE", "US", etc.) instead of actual flags** when screenshotted via this machine's headless Chrome — not a CSS font-stack issue (tried adding `Segoe UI Emoji`/`Noto Color Emoji` to the `.icon` font-family, no effect), a genuine OS-level missing color-emoji-font-for-flag-ligatures limitation of this specific headless Chrome install. **Fixed by replacing all 17 flag icons with non-flag alternatives that render correctly and stay thematically fitting**: 🗽 (US), 🏛️ (Jordan hub), 🕋 (Saudi — the standard, respectful representation), 🏰 (UK), ⛰️ (Oman), 🏙️ (UAE), 🏺 (jordan-quiz game, distinct from the hub's 🏛️), 🧳 (all 5 nationality/expat-landing pages — reuse across same-type pages already an established pattern elsewhere in this metadata set, e.g. 🚗 repeated for every country's car-cost page), 💱 (all 5 `omr-to-*` currency pages). **If this pipeline is reused and a new page's icon happens to be a flag emoji, expect the same failure and substitute a non-flag icon** — don't assume a CSS font-family fix will work, it was tried and confirmed ineffective on this machine.

**Format: JPEG, not PNG** — first pass rendered PNG screenshots directly (`Page.captureScreenshot` default), averaging ~312KB each (47MB for all 151, no compression on a gradient+text+emoji image). Switched to `format:'jpeg', quality:88` (Chrome's screenshot API supports this natively, no extra tool/dependency) — ~30KB each (4.3MB total), visually indistinguishable at normal viewing size. Doesn't affect any real page-load metric (OG images are fetched server-side by the sharing platform generating a preview card, never by a visitor's own browser rendering the page), but no reason to carry 10x the file size for zero benefit.

**Wiring**: `og:image`/`og:image:width`/`og:image:height` + a full `twitter:card`/`twitter:title`/`twitter:description`/`twitter:image` block inserted into each of the 151 files, anchored right after each page's own `og:url` tag (matching the existing 5-page convention) with fallback anchors (`og:description` → `<meta name="description">` → `</head>`) for the 17 pages that were missing `og:url`/`og:title` entirely (mostly root game/currency-landing pages that predate the site's full meta-tag convention). For the 30 pages in `build-lang-pages.js`'s `TOOLS` list, only the root file needed editing — `node build-lang-pages.js` propagates the same absolute image URL into all 5 lang variants automatically (OG images don't need per-language versions).

**If a new page is added without an `og:image`**: the same pattern applies — pull a fitting icon from an existing hub card if the page has one, otherwise pick something thematically apt (avoiding flag emoji per the above), render via the card template (scratchpad-only this session, rewrite fresh if picked up again, same as other scratchpad-only scripts noted elsewhere in this file) at 1200×630 JPEG quality 88, save to `og/<slug>.jpg` (slug = path with `/`→`_`, `.html`/`/index.html` stripped), and wire the same 7-tag block after `og:url`.

**Push verification**: this was the single largest batch this project's rate-limiting has hit — 452 total files touched (151 HTML + 151 new images + 150 lang variants), 136 individual files failed to blob across the initial full-tree push and two subsequent partial pushes (GitHub's secondary throttle, not the real hourly quota — confirmed via a direct test call mid-session showing 4518/5000 remaining). Fixed via a generalized batched targeted-push script (blob every mismatched file with 500ms pacing + 3-attempt retry-with-backoff per file, one combined tree+commit at the end) rather than one-file-at-a-time — reusable for any future batch this size. Re-ran the full blob-sha tree-diff verification after each round; final state: **0 mismatches across all 452 files**, confirmed live via direct `curl` (both the image URLs themselves — real `200 image/jpeg` responses — and the exact page from the user's original report, `jo/calculators/tawjihi-average/`, now serving a real `og:image` pointing at a real generated file). `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) both re-run clean afterward — zero regression from any part of this feature.

**Also found and cleaned up while investigating this**: two unrelated stray scratchpad-artifact files had leaked into the live repo from earlier work in this same session/project — a metadata JSON from this OG-image build that landed in a mangled-path directory literally named after an escaped temp path (a backslash-escaping bug in an early draft script, root-caused and avoided in the final version), and a much older leftover from the 2026-09-14 logo-quiz football-club fork (`jo/games/logo-quiz/Users96896...json`, a candidate brand-name list, harmless content but never meant to be live). Both deleted from the live tree via explicit `sha: null` tree entries (git's delete-a-path mechanism) — **worth periodically grepping the live tree for `Users96896`/`AppData`/`Temp` path fragments if this project keeps having sessions/forks write scratch files into the working directory instead of the scratchpad**, since `push-to-github.js`'s blind "push everything in the tree" approach has no way to distinguish an accidental stray file from real content.

## xlsx (SheetJS) CVE fix — self-hosted instead of a CDN-allowlist change, 2026-09-14

Closed the `xlsx` gap left open in the 2026-09-14 dependency-scan section above (CVE-2023-30533 prototype pollution + CVE-2024-22363 ReDoS, both in the pinned 0.18.5 build `file-converter.html` loaded from jsDelivr). That section's note assumed the fix just needed adding `cdn.sheetjs.com` to the CSP `script-src` allowlist — **re-verified before acting and found two things wrong with that plan**: (1) the actual fixed versions are 0.19.3 (prototype pollution) and **0.20.2** (ReDoS) — 0.19.3 alone, as the old note said, still leaves the ReDoS bug open; confirmed via WebSearch against the CVE advisories directly, not trusted from memory. (2) **Adding a new domain to the Worker's CSP wouldn't actually have worked**, given the still-open duplicate-CSP-header situation documented in the CSP-hardening section above: the old Cloudflare Transform Rule fires *in addition to* the Worker and uses an "Add" action (confirmed by the earlier finding that both a nonce'd and non-nonce'd `Content-Security-Policy` header arrive in the same response) — since CSP combines multiple headers with AND-semantics, a resource needs the domain allowed in *both* policies, and the Transform Rule's copy isn't editable (no Cloudflare Rulesets write access, same blocker documented in that section). Editing only the Worker's policy would have silently failed to unblock the new CDN.

**The actual fix sidesteps this entirely**: downloaded the real, verified 0.20.3 build (latest stable, past both CVE fixes) directly from `cdn.sheetjs.com` (sanity-checked: real SheetJS file header comment, correct embedded `version="0.20.3"` string, 952KB — not an error page), and **self-hosted it** at `vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.min.js` instead of loading from any external CDN. Since the site's own origin (`'self'`) is already allowed by both CSP policies, this needed **zero CSP changes at all** — a strictly simpler fix than the CDN-allowlist plan, and one that isn't blocked by the Transform Rule access gap. `vendor/` is a new top-level directory (this project's first self-hosted third-party JS library — `fonts/` was the only prior precedent for a self-hosted external asset). Only `loadXlsx()` in `file-converter.html` changed, from the jsDelivr URL to the relative `vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.min.js` path (resolves correctly via the page's `<base href>` regardless of nesting depth, same as every other relative link on the site).

**Verified real API compatibility, not just "it loads"**: this page uses exactly 4 SheetJS calls (`XLSX.read`, `XLSX.write`, `XLSX.utils.sheet_to_csv`, `XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json`) — all long-stable Community Edition APIs unchanged across the 0.18→0.20 range. Confirmed via a live CDP test against production: built a real workbook in-memory (`book_new`/`aoa_to_sheet`/`book_append_sheet`/`write`), read it back (`XLSX.read`), and converted to both CSV and JSON — data round-tripped exactly (`{Name:'Ahmed',Score:95}` survived the full write→read→sheet_to_json cycle unchanged), confirming `XLSX.version === '0.20.3'` was actually the code running, not a cached/stale reference. `test-calculators.js` re-run clean afterward as a broader regression check.

**Push note**: the new `vendor/` directory hit the same "GitHub Pages build lag for brand-new paths" pattern already documented elsewhere in this file (git tree confirmed correct via blob-sha diff immediately, but the live URL 404'd for ~20-30s before the Pages build caught up) — don't assume a failed push if a *new path* 404s right after pushing, same as the daily-info-modules and gold-price sections above already found.

**mammoth (Word conversion)** and **pdf.js** remain as documented in the earlier dependency-scan section (mammoth's CVE not applicable in a browser context; pdf.js already patched via the `isEvalSupported:false` workaround) — this fix only touched xlsx. If jsPDF/html2canvas or any other pinned CDN library is ever found to have a real CVE with no fixed build on its existing CDN, check for a maintainer-run CDN with the patched version first (as here) before assuming a CSP-allowlist change is required — self-hosting under `vendor/` avoids the CSP/Transform-Rule complication entirely for any future case like this.

## Mobile nav menu horizontal overflow — fixed 2026-09-14

User-reported via screenshot (site owner hit this themselves): on mobile, opening the country or language dropdown inside the collapsed hamburger menu required swiping right to actually see it — the menu content was rendering off-screen.

**Root cause, found via CDP mobile-emulation diagnostics (not guessed from CSS reading alone)**: `.nav-links` (the collapsed mobile nav panel) sets `flex-direction: column` in its mobile media query, but **inherits `flex-wrap: wrap` from the desktop base `.nav-links` rule** (`flex-wrap` was never explicitly reset in the mobile override). `flex-wrap:wrap` + `flex-direction:column` on a height-constrained flex container (`max-height: calc(100vh - 68px)`) makes overflowing items wrap into a **second column** instead of just growing/scrolling vertically — so once About/Contact/Sign-Up/dark-toggle filled the first "column" height, both `.lang-switcher` instances (country picker + language picker) got pushed into a second column positioned off to the right of the viewport, requiring horizontal scroll to reach. Confirmed precisely via a live diagnostic script reading each nav child's actual `getBoundingClientRect()` — the switcher elements sat at `left:378` inside a 390px-wide viewport, i.e. almost entirely off-screen.

**Fixed**: added `flex-wrap: nowrap;` (the actual fix) and `overflow-x: hidden;` (defense-in-depth) to `.nav-links`'s mobile media-query rule in `style.css`. Verified via a fresh cache-busted fetch of the live file that the change is correctly deployed — **full CDP behavioral re-verification is still pending** a Cloudflare cache purge (see the "Cloudflare edge-cache staleness" section immediately below — `style.css` got cached with the pre-fix content in the propagation window right after this push, same as documented incidents elsewhere in this file, and this session doesn't have cache-purge API access).

## Cloudflare edge-cache staleness — hit again 2026-09-14, still no purge access
