Reconfirms the exact pattern already documented in the Lighthouse section above ("Cloudflare caches per-URL lazily on first request... applies to any file now that Cloudflare proxies the site") — hit twice more this session, on `style.css` (the mobile-nav fix above) and `jo/games/quiz-engine.js` (the share-image feature below). Both times: the live git tree + a direct cache-busted `curl` confirmed the origin had the correct new content within seconds of pushing, but the bare (no query string) URL kept serving pre-fix content, `cf-cache-status: HIT`, for several minutes at minimum (`max-age=14400` = 4 hours is the theoretical worst case without a purge).

**New finding this round: tried a direct Cloudflare API cache-purge call** (`POST /zones/{id}/purge_cache`) using the same `wrangler login` OAuth token already on file — **also rejected with the same `10000 Authentication error` as the Rulesets-edit attempt documented in the CSP section**, confirming Wrangler's OAuth scope grant covers Workers/Pages/D1/etc. but not `Zone.Cache Purge` either. **This session has no way to force-refresh Cloudflare's edge cache after a push** — the same access gap as the Transform Rule cleanup, just for a different Cloudflare permission. Asked the user directly to purge (Dashboard → Caching → Configuration → Purge Everything, or a custom purge of the specific changed file) — this takes them seconds and is the only way to close the propagation gap immediately; otherwise it self-resolves within the `max-age` window.

**If this keeps recurring and is worth solving properly**: either (a) the user grants a Cloudflare API Token scoped to `Zone > Cache Purge > Purge` (much narrower and lower-risk than the Rulesets-edit access that CSP cleanup would need), or (b) a Cache Rule change lowering `style.css`/`quiz-engine.js`/other actively-edited files' edge TTL specifically (the existing "Respect origin TTL" Cache Rule mentioned in the Lighthouse section only bounds it by the origin's own `Cache-Control: max-age=14400`, which is itself the long window causing this). Until either happens, **always verify a CSS/JS fix with a cache-busted fetch (`?bust=<random>`) rather than a bare URL**, and expect that bare-URL behavioral testing (via CDP navigation, which can't easily add a cache-buster to sub-resource requests) may show stale results for a while after any push touching a frequently-fetched shared asset — this is a known, recurring, currently-unfixable-by-this-session propagation lag, not a sign the push itself failed.

## Quiz-result sharing — branded image with a rank-based witty caption, built 2026-09-14

User asked (بالتحديات بدي صورة مع كتابة حلوة وفيها مزح — "for challenges I want an image with nice/funny text") for the "شارك نتيجتك" (share your result) button on `jo/games/*` result screens to produce a fun branded image instead of the previous plain-text `navigator.share()` call.

**Two share mechanisms on the result screen, clarified for the user mid-build (he was asking why a second "مشاركة" showed up) — not a duplicate, two different jobs**: "شارك نتيجتك" (`shareResultBtn`→`shareResult()`) shares *your own score*; the separate "شارك التحدي مع أصحابك" box (`challengeShareArea`, shown once a leaderboard exists) is for inviting *more people* into the *same still-open* challenge — different link, different purpose, both legitimately needed. Only `shareResult()` was changed here.

**Captions are rank/margin-based, not the existing absolute-score `QUIZ_CONFIG.resultMessages` tiers** — those two systems answer different questions ("was 9/12 a good score" vs. "how did I do against the specific people in this challenge") and both now coexist: `resultMessages` still drives the on-screen result text, this new `RESULT_CAPTIONS` set (in `quiz-engine.js`, ar+en, matching the established English-optional-scope convention for these pages) is only for the shared image. `getResultCaption()` computes the caller's rank and score-gap-to-nearest-rival from `latestLeaderboard` (already populated by the existing `renderLeaderboard()` onSnapshot listener) and picks one of 8 tiers: solo (no rivals yet), tied-for-first, big win / comfortable win / narrow win, big last-place gap / soft last-place, or a generic middle-of-the-pack line — directly echoing the user's own example phrases ("لقد انتصرت أيها الوحش", "الفرق كبير", "لازمك تدريب").

**Visual card** (`buildResultShareCanvas()`): same brand gradient + bidi-aware-per-string-direction technique as `exportResultImage()` in `i18n.js`, reimplemented self-contained in `quiz-engine.js` since `jo/games/*` pages don't load `i18n.js`. Shows the brand mark, game title, the big witty caption, the player's score/percentage, and up to 6 leaderboard rows (medal emoji for top 3, the viewer's own row highlighted in yellow).

**Real bug caught and fixed before finalizing — a fixed/minimum canvas height left large empty gradient space below short results** (a solo player with no rivals yet, or a short 1-line caption, still forced at least 1200px tall) — the exact same mistake `exportResultImage()`'s own CLAUDE.md note already warns against ("A first version used a fixed 1350px height regardless of content... don't reintroduce a fixed height"). **Fixed with a proper two-pass measurement**: a throwaway `<canvas>` context measures the caption's actual wrapped line count *before* the real canvas is created, then height is summed purely from real content blocks (title/caption-lines/score/leaderboard-rows/footer), no artificial floor or ceiling. Verified the fix's arithmetic by hand against the pre-fix render (a 2-line caption + 2-row leaderboard case dropped from a forced 1200px down to a computed ~788px) — full CDP visual re-confirmation blocked on the same Cloudflare cache-purge gap documented above (re-evaluating the whole fresh script source into an already-loaded page's global scope throws on the repeated top-level `const` declarations, a CDP/page-injection limitation rather than a code defect — confirmed the deployed *source* itself is correct via a direct cache-busted fetch instead).

**Share call**: builds the canvas, converts to a PNG blob/File, and — when `navigator.canShare({files:[...]})` is supported — shares the image together with the existing leaderboard text and challenge URL in one `navigator.share()` call; falls back to the pre-existing text-only share (or clipboard-copy) path if file-sharing isn't supported, so no regression on browsers/platforms that can't take an image share.

## Export-image original-input gap — fixed across 9 calculators, 2026-09-14

User-reported (screenshot of `bmi-calculator.html`'s shared image): the exported result image showed the BMI value and category but not the height/weight the visitor actually entered — a share is meaningless to the recipient without knowing what was calculated. Audited every calculator using `exportResultImage()` and found the same gap on **9 of 11**: `bmi-calculator`, `loan-calculator`, `age-calculator`, `end-of-service`, `vat-calculator`, `tip-calculator`, `date-diff`, and one of `percentage-calculator`'s 3 tabs + one of `discount-calculator`'s 2 tabs. `currency-converter` (fixed earlier the same session) and `compound-interest` already included their original inputs — no change needed there.

**Fixed by prepending the real original input values** (read live from each page's own input elements, not recomputed) as additional `rows` entries before the existing result rows, reusing each page's own already-translated label (`t.lbl_weight`, `t.eos_start`, `t.tip_bill`, etc.) — matching the site-wide convention of reusing existing i18n keys rather than inventing new ones (same approach used for the currency-converter fix earlier this session).

**Two calculators needed active-tab/mode-aware logic, not a flat row list**:
- `percentage-calculator.html` has 3 independent tabs (X% of Y / X is what% of Y / % change), each with its own input pair — built an `inputRows` map keyed by the active tab index, concatenated with the existing result row.
- `discount-calculator.html` has 2 tabs (discount / plain percentage) — the "discount" branch already included its inputs; only the "percentage" branch needed rows added. **First pass reused the same `percent_label1`+`percent_label2` phrase for both the new input-summary row and the existing result row, producing a visually duplicated label** ("What is % of the number" appearing twice) — caught by actually reading the test output, not just confirming no JS error. Fixed by using the distinct `pct_pct`/`pct_num` keys (already shared in `i18n.js`, originally written for `percentage-calculator.html`) for the two input rows instead.

**`vat-calculator.html`** needed care since its amount-input label text changes meaning depending on add-tax vs. extract-tax mode ("Amount before tax" vs. the VAT-inclusive total) — used the label element's own *live* `textContent` (which the page's existing mode-toggle logic already keeps correct) rather than a static translation key, so the exported row is never wrong regardless of mode.

**Verified via live CDP tests against production** (not just local validation) — for each fixed calculator, filled real input values, called the real `calculate()`/`calc1()`/etc. function, temporarily wrapped `exportResultImage` to capture its `rows` argument without actually triggering a canvas render/share, and confirmed the captured rows contain the real entered values. Caught and fixed the discount-calculator label duplication this way. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean afterward.

## Gold-price API outage + server-side fallback via the Worker, 2026-09-14

User-reported (screenshot): `om/gold-price/` showing "تعذّر تحميل الأسعار الآن" (failed to load prices). Investigated rather than assuming a code bug — confirmed `xaus.com` (the API all 6 countries' gold-price pages depend on) was returning `503 usage_exceeded` **site-wide** (not just the API subpath), a genuine outage on their end. This affected all 6 gold-price pages simultaneously, not just Oman's — the existing error-handling was already working correctly (showing the graceful fallback message, not crashing), this was a real external dependency failure, not a bug.

**User explicitly chose to build a real fallback** (via `AskUserQuestion`) rather than just wait it out. Real constraint hit immediately: `open.er-api.com`/`api.frankfurter.app`/`api.exchangerate-api.com` (all already in the CSP `connect-src` allowlist) don't carry XAU (gold) as a currency — none of the site's existing whitelisted APIs provide gold spot prices. `goldprice.dev`'s anonymous no-key tier does, but adding its domain to the browser-side CSP `connect-src` would hit the **exact same duplicate-Transform-Rule blocker** documented in the CSP-hardening section above (both the Worker's policy and the un-editable old Transform Rule's policy would need the new domain, and this session still can't edit the latter).

**Fix: route the fallback through the existing `adawati-csp-nonce` Cloudflare Worker instead of calling third-party APIs directly from the browser.** Added a new `/api/gold-fallback?currency=X` path handled specially at the top of the Worker's `fetch()` — it fetches `api.goldprice.dev` (XAU/USD spot, troy ounce) + `open.er-api.com` (USD→target-currency rate) **server-side** (Worker-to-API fetches aren't CSP-governed at all, only browser-initiated ones are) and returns a computed 24k gram price as same-origin JSON. Since the browser only ever calls `adawati.space/api/gold-fallback` (already covered by `'self'`), **this needed zero CSP changes** — a cleaner fix than the allowlist route, and one this session could actually deploy without needing Cloudflare dashboard/Rulesets access.

**Real problem hit deploying this: `goldprice.dev`'s anonymous tier is rate-limited via Cloudflare's *shared* Worker egress IP pool** — other, unrelated Cloudflare Workers hitting the same free tier exhausted it, so calls from *this* Worker got `429` even though this project's own traffic was nowhere near any documented limit. Confirmed this wasn't a fluke with 3 consecutive retries, all `429`. **User signed up for a free goldprice.dev API key** (email only, no credit card, isolated per-key quota — this is a real credential, not a public value, so it went into a Cloudflare Worker secret via `wrangler secret put GOLDPRICE_API_KEY`, never hardcoded in `worker.js` or committed to this repo) — wired in as `Authorization: Bearer <key>` per their docs (confirmed the exact header format via their API reference before guessing). Fixed the shared-IP throttling immediately — verified all 6 currencies (JOD/OMR/SAR/AED/USD/GBP) return real `200` prices via the authenticated key.

**Wired into all 6 `{jo,om,sa,ae,us,uk}/gold-price/index.html` pages**: on `xaus.com` fetch failure (network error, non-2xx status, or malformed response — not just a `.catch()` on the raw promise, which wouldn't have caught a 503-with-valid-JSON-body response), falls through to `/api/gold-fallback?currency=<code>`, computes the same 24k/22k/21k/18k karat breakdown from the returned `price24`, and renders normally with a small "(fallback source)"/"(مصدر احتياطي)" note appended to the updated-time label so it's transparent to visitors which source they're seeing — never silently presents estimated/fallback data as if it were the primary live feed. `om`'s page (which already had the `rawPrices`/`renderPrices()`/local-market-toggle refactor from the earlier gold-price-accuracy investigation) got an `applyGoldPrice(price24, isFallback)` wrapper around that existing flow; the other 5 (simpler `karatPrices`-direct pages) got an equivalent `applyGoldPrice` extracted from their own previously-inline fetch handler, applied via one parameterized script covering all 5 at once (byte-for-byte identical structure across them, differing only in currency code/symbol/decimals/locale).

**Verified end-to-end via live CDP against production, not just the Worker endpoint in isolation**: blocked `*xaus.com*` at the network level (`Network.setBlockedURLs`, the same technique used to isolate a fallback path throughout this project) and confirmed `om`/`jo`/`us` gold-price pages all correctly render real fallback prices with the source note, `errorMsg` staying hidden — not just that the Worker endpoint itself returns valid JSON. Also confirmed the real (non-blocked) live behavior during the actual ongoing `xaus.com` outage: the page still resolves correctly, just takes longer (~8-9s total) since `xaus.com`'s own response is slow-to-fail rather than failing instantly — acceptable degraded behavior, meaningfully better than the pre-fix broken-error state.

**If `xaus.com` recovers and this is revisited**: the primary path is untouched (same `fetch('https://xaus.com/...')` call as before), so no further action needed — the fallback only ever activates on a real primary-source failure. If `goldprice.dev`'s key ever needs rotating, `wrangler secret put GOLDPRICE_API_KEY` again (from the `csp-worker` scratchpad directory) and redeploy; the key itself lives only in Cloudflare's secret store, never in this repo.

## General site audit — FAQ schema/visible sync, 2026-09-15

User asked for a general "enhance and fixes" audit pass. Ran a full site scan (broken internal links, JSON-LD validity, missing i18n.js, surviving onclick attributes, missing title/description/canonical, inline `<script>` syntax errors, and FAQ-schema-vs-visible-content sync) across all 306 HTML files.

**Clean on first pass**: 0 broken internal links (4,920 checked), 0 JSON-LD parse errors, 0 pages missing i18n.js, 0 real onclick attributes, 0 missing meta description/canonical, 0 inline script syntax errors. The "5 pages missing `<title>`" finding was a false positive — the 5 holiday pages use `<title id="pageTitleTag">` for their countdown feature (same pattern already noted elsewhere in this file), not a missing tag.

**Real, large finding: 37+ unique pages had FAQ JSON-LD schema with zero visible FAQ content**, plus ~17 more with schema/visible count mismatches in both directions — the same "structured data richer than visible content" SEO risk already fixed on other pages earlier this project, just never applied to newer additions: all 3 daily-info modules (gold-price/fuel-prices/holidays) across om/sa/ae/us/uk (jo's own gold-price/fuel-prices/holidays needed the same fix), 4 misc duplicate-tool pages (om/salary-calculator, sa/vat-calculator, ae/vat-calculator, uk/percentage-calculator), all 6 root mini-games (car-game/jump-game/memory-game/number-guess/quick-math/reaction-test, each ×5 lang variants), and all 9 kids-* pages. **Fixed by rendering each page's own already-existing JSON-LD Question/Answer text as visible `<details>` cards** — not new content, just making already-reviewed schema text visible, matching Google's guidance that structured data should reflect real on-page content. Games are in `build-lang-pages.js`'s `TOOLS` list, so `node build-lang-pages.js` was re-run to propagate into all lang variants.

**Real pre-existing bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to the sync task itself**: `compound-interest.html` and `image-compressor.html` (both English root pages, `lang="en"`) had their **entire FAQ JSON-LD schema written in Arabic** while the visible FAQ content was in English with different (equivalent-concept) wording — a genuine content-language mismatch that predates this session, not something introduced by any recent edit. A first mechanical pass (reusing the "render missing schema questions as visible details" script) wrongly appended Arabic `<details>` onto these English pages before this was caught — reverted immediately. **Actual fix**: translated each page's JSON-LD schema into English, matching the wording of its own already-existing visible `<details>` content, and added one new visible English FAQ item for the one schema question ("What is compound interest?" / WebP compression) that had no English visible counterpart. Confirmed via `build-lang-pages.js` that lang variants (ar/fr/es/de/ru) copy the ROOT file's entire `<head>` JSON-LD verbatim (not a separately-authored per-language schema as first assumed) — so this fix is now consistent (schema=visible, same language) on every variant, though the underlying "secondary FAQ content stays English-only, not translated" limitation is pre-existing and unchanged, matching the same minimum-viable-scope pattern already established elsewhere in this file (e.g. `jo_hero_*` keys, nationality-page FAQ content).

**Second real bug, a script-tooling mistake caught before pushing, not a site bug**: `jo/calculators/university-gpa/index.html` has a non-FAQ collapsible `<details id="gradeTableDetails">` (a grade-conversion table, unrelated to the FAQ card) elsewhere on the page. A batch script's regex for extracting `<details><summary>...</summary><p>...</p></details>` blocks non-greedily matched from that unrelated `<summary>` all the way to the first `<p>` anywhere later in the file, producing one giant garbage "FAQ question" combining unrelated page content. **Caught immediately by spot-checking the diff before pushing** (a "changed on disk" notice surfaced the garbled content) — removed the single bad entry, leaving the genuine 3 additional FAQ questions the same batch had correctly extracted from real `<details>` blocks elsewhere on the page. Verified via a full re-scan of all 19 files in that batch for suspiciously long entries (>150 chars name / >600 chars answer) — 0 other files affected, this was a one-off caused specifically by that one page's non-FAQ `<details>` element. **Lesson: any HTML-regex-based batch extraction of `<details>`/`<summary>`/`<p>` triples must account for a page having a non-FAQ `<details>` element elsewhere — always spot-check for anomalously long extracted entries before trusting a batch script's output on unfamiliar pages, not just files matching the exact template already tested.**

**Third real (smaller) bug, caught during verification of `jo/students/scholarships-loans/index.html`**: after the mechanical "add missing schema entries for un-matched visible questions" pass, the file's schema (4) and visible (3) counts still didn't match — investigation found a **pre-existing, separate orphan**: the schema already had a question ("شو صندوق دعم الطالب الجامعي؟") with **no visible FAQ entry at all**, predating this whole audit, just coincidentally masked by the count math (a different visible-only gap of the same size happened to net the totals to a misleading near-match). Fixed by adding the missing visible `<details>` using the schema's own already-existing answer text. **Lesson: a count-based mismatch check (schema count vs visible count) can hide two opposite-direction gaps that partially cancel out — when fixing one direction, re ­verify by checking each individual question is actually present in the other representation, not just that the totals now agree.**

**Verification**: all touched files validated (`JSON.parse` + `new Function()` + div-balance) before every push. Two pushes needed the established targeted-push-for-stale-files fix (25 files total across both batches got dropped by GitHub's secondary rate limit and needed re-pushing) — re-verified via blob-sha diff against the final commit each time, 0 mismatches before considering done. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run clean after each batch. A final full re-scan confirmed the FAQ sync issue is resolved site-wide except one correctly-benign case (university-gpa's `<details>` count includes its 1 legitimate non-FAQ collapsible table, which is expected, not a bug).

## Performance/accessibility re-check, 2026-09-15

Continuing the "enhance and fixes" general audit into performance/accessibility, per explicit user request.

**Apparent performance regression investigated and ruled out as a false alarm**: a fresh Lighthouse run on the homepage showed Performance 68-69/100 and LCP 5.8-7.1s — a big drop from the 96/2.0s recorded 2026-09-14. Confirmed reproducible across 2 consecutive runs (not random noise), but neither `index.html`, `style.css`, nor `i18n.js` had been touched since 9/13-9/14 (file mtimes checked directly) — ruling out any of this session's edits as the cause before investigating further. **Root-caused via a controlled comparison**: re-ran Lighthouse with `--blocked-url-patterns` excluding `*googlesyndication*`/`*googletagmanager*`/`*doubleclick*` — score jumped back to 94, LCP dropped to 2.9s, TBT from 90-140ms to 10ms. **Conclusion: 100% Google AdSense/ad-network real-time-loading variance** (which ad creative loads, real-time bidding latency — inherently different every page load) — not a site regression, not something to "fix" (removing ads isn't on the table, and this variance is a known, accepted characteristic of running AdSense, already noted elsewhere in this file re: "third-party cookies" being unavoidable while ads are running). **Lesson for future performance re-checks on this site: always run one comparison with ad/analytics domains blocked before concluding a score change is a real regression** — a live ad-supported page's Lighthouse score is expected to fluctuate significantly run-to-run for reasons entirely outside this codebase's control.

**Real accessibility bug found and fixed**: Lighthouse flagged `color-contrast` again (score 0) despite the `--text-muted` variable already being darkened to `#5c6b83` on 2026-09-14 specifically to fix this class of issue. Investigation found the **footer** (`.footer`, permanently dark background `#0f172a` in light mode / `#010409` in dark mode, independent of the site's light/dark toggle) used **6 hardcoded literal colors** (`#64748b` ×4, `#475569` ×2 — in `.footer-brand-block p`, `.footer-col a`, `.footer-copy`, `.footer-langs span`, `.footer-copy-simple`) that were never touched by the earlier fix since they don't reference the `--text-muted` variable at all. Contrast ratios: `#64748b` vs `#0f172a` = 3.75:1, `#475569` vs `#0f172a` = 2.36:1 — both fail the 4.5:1 WCAG AA minimum for normal text. **This affects the footer on every single page site-wide** (`.footer-copy-simple` is the shared simple-footer class used on essentially all ~300 tool pages, not just the homepage), so this was a much bigger-reach bug than a homepage-only issue. **Fixed**: replaced all 6 occurrences with `#8492a6` (computed via the WCAG relative-luminance formula: 5.65:1 against the light-mode footer background, 6.50:1 against the dark-mode one — comfortable margin above 4.5:1 in both cases, verified by calculation before applying, not guessed). Verified via a second Lighthouse run: accessibility 92 → 98, `color-contrast` fully resolved. **Lesson: a CSS-variable-based contrast fix only reaches the elements that actually reference that variable — always grep for hardcoded literal hex colors matching the old failing value site-wide before considering a contrast fix complete, since a component styled with a literal color (like this project's footer) silently bypasses any variable-based fix entirely.**

**Remaining accessibility finding, pre-existing and already documented, not re-investigated**: `heading-order` (a footer `<h4>` with no preceding `<h2>`/`<h3>` on the page) — same minor, low-priority item noted in the original 2026-09-14 Lighthouse section of this file.

**Verification**: CSS brace-balance checked before pushing (242/242). `style.css` itself failed to blob on the first full-tree push attempt (usual secondary rate-limit pattern) and needed the targeted single-file push script — confirmed via blob-sha diff before considering it live. Re-ran `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) after the fix — one `test-e2e.js` run hit a single flaky failure (`no console/runtime errors on /jo/games/logo-quiz/`), reproduced-and-cleared on an immediate re-run with a fresh direct CDP check finding zero actual console errors — confirmed as transient (likely a momentary network blip loading one of the many logo-quiz images), not a real regression from the CSS change, before moving on.

## Security deep-dive, 2026-09-15

Continuing the "enhance and fixes" audit into security, per explicit user request, after the performance/accessibility pass above. Re-ran the established dangerous-sink scan (`eval`, `document.write`, `new Function`) fresh across the whole repo — same clean result as the 2026-09-14 pass (only the known `test-e2e.js` `.eval()` method-name false positive). Checked for mixed content (hardcoded `http://` resource references) — 2 hits, both false positives (XML namespace URI strings inside `build-sitemap.js`'s sitemap-schema declaration and `vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.min.js`'s internal Office-format namespace constants, neither is a fetched network resource). Reconfirmed `.env` still returns 404 live and the goldprice.dev API key (`ga_live_...`) does not appear anywhere in the repo — it only exists as a Cloudflare Worker secret, as originally set up.

**Real, if modest, finding: the `/api/gold-fallback` Worker endpoint had zero caching or rate-limiting and was publicly reachable without auth**, calling a paid, API-key-authenticated third-party service (`api.goldprice.dev`) on every single hit — `Cache-Control: no-store` on its own response meant nothing prevented a bot or deliberate prober from spamming this endpoint and exhausting the goldprice.dev API quota, an availability/cost risk (not a data-exposure risk — the currency query parameter was already confirmed safe against injection, since it's only ever used as a plain object-key lookup into the exchange-rate response, never interpolated into a URL or command). **Fixed**: added Cloudflare's `caches.default` Worker-level cache (keyed on origin+pathname+normalized currency) with a 60-second TTL, only caching genuine 200 responses (never the 502 error path, so a transient goldprice.dev outage doesn't get needlessly cached) — this both protects the API quota and naturally rate-limits repeated hits for the same currency without needing real authentication infrastructure, which wasn't worth building for a low-traffic fallback-of-a-fallback endpoint. Deployed via `wrangler deploy` (established CLI method for this Worker, not the dashboard editor). **Verified live**: two consecutive requests for the same currency returned byte-identical prices (confirming the second was genuinely served from cache, not a fresh API call) with `CF-Cache-Status: HIT` on the second; Cloudflare's own edge cache picked up the `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60` header too and applied its own (longer, Cache-Rule-governed) TTL on top — a harmless, even more protective side effect, not a bug, since gold-price data freshness at the minute-to-minute level was never a real requirement for this already-approximate fallback source. Confirmed the Worker's primary CSP-nonce functionality is completely unaffected post-redeploy (checked fresh unique nonces + correct CSP on 4 different page types), and re-ran `test-calculators.js` (20/20) + `test-e2e.js` (29/29) clean.

**Other checks, all clean, nothing to fix**: Firestore security rules (already documented in detail elsewhere in this file, not re-litigated), SRI on the Firebase SDK scripts (already in place from 2026-09-14), the single known/accepted GitHub secret-scanning alert (the intentionally-public Firebase web API key) unchanged. This closes the security-deep-dive portion of this session's general audit at a reasonable, threat-model-appropriate depth for a static site with one small serverless endpoint — a full line-by-line Worker/Firestore-rules re-audit wasn't repeated since nothing has changed there since the 2026-09-14 review beyond this one endpoint.

## External "deep scan" QA report, 2026-09-15 — mostly a scanner artifact, two real minor gaps fixed

User pasted another large unsolicited "deep verified live scan report" (same pattern as the several before it) claiming a headline "internal path and asset-integrity problem": 2,929 of 3,482 internal targets failing, with specific examples like `/ae/style.css` → 404, `/om/about.html` → 404, `/om/salary-calculator/i18n.js?v=42` → 404, `/ae/ae/vat-calculator/` → 404 (locale prefix duplicated). Also claimed 701 onclick attributes across 220 pages (directly contradicting the 2026-09-14 onclick-refactor project documented at length above) and 2 leftover `.onclick=` assignments in `currency-converter.html` (also already fixed 2026-09-14).

**Verified rather than trusted, per this project's established practice — and this time the verification actually mattered, since the specific 404 URLs the report cited are individually real** (`curl` confirmed all 4 examples genuinely return 404). But the report's own methodology was flawed: **it evidently ignored the `<base href="https://adawati.space/">` tag every page carries** and resolved relative paths (`style.css`, `i18n.js?v=42`, `about.html`, `ae/vat-calculator/`) naively against each page's own directory instead of against the base — exactly the failure mode `<base href>` exists to prevent. **Confirmed via a real headless-Chrome CDP test** (not just reasoning about URL-resolution spec behavior): loaded `/ae/` and `/om/salary-calculator/` live, read back the actual resolved `href`/`src` the browser computed for the stylesheet and i18n script — both correctly resolved to `https://adawati.space/style.css` and `https://adawati.space/i18n.js?v=42` (root-level, correct), with **zero failed network requests** on either page. The malformed URLs the report flagged (`/ae/style.css` etc.) are real 404s in isolation, but no real browser ever actually requests them — they're an artifact of a link-checker that doesn't fully parse HTML semantics (a common category of bug in generic crawlers). **This was the report's single largest, most alarming finding (driving its "6.5/10 navigation integrity" sub-score) and it does not describe anything a real visitor or search engine crawler using a real browser would ever hit.**

**The onclick claims were also independently re-verified and found false**: a fresh full-repo regex scan (the same `<tag ...onclick="` pattern established during the original refactor) found **0 onclick attributes across all 306 HTML files** — matching the already-documented "PROJECT COMPLETE" state, not the report's claimed 701/220. `currency-converter.html` also has zero `.onclick=` property assignments, confirming that fix (also already documented) is still in place.

**Two claims in the report were real and worth fixing, both minor and already resolved**:
- **`favicon.ico` returned 404** — real, since some legacy tools/bots/RSS readers still probe this well-known path directly regardless of the site's `<link rel="icon">` SVG tag (which is what modern browsers actually use, already in place site-wide since the 2026-09-07 PWA audit). Fixed by generating a proper multi-size (16/32/48px) ICO from the existing `icon-1024-master.png` master via .NET `System.Drawing` in PowerShell (same established icon-generation technique as the original PWA icon work), placed at the repo root — no HTML changes needed since browsers/tools request this well-known path directly.
- **`.well-known/security.txt` was missing** — a real, legitimate, low-effort best practice (RFC 9116) for a site with real financial calculators and a genuine user base. Added a minimal valid one (Contact, Expires, Preferred-Languages, Canonical fields) using the same contact email already public on `contact.html` — no new information exposure, just making an already-public contact method discoverable via the standard convention. Confirmed `.well-known/` is already allowlisted in `push-to-github.js`'s dotfile-exclusion logic (`.well-known`, `.nojekyll`), so no script change was needed.

**Other claims in the report were either already-known/documented (duplicate security headers — real, but the same pre-existing Cloudflare Transform Rule gap already documented and blocked on dashboard access, not new) or generic recommendations already satisfied by existing work** (Lighthouse, `test-calculators.js`, `test-e2e.js` all already exist and were re-run clean multiple times this session, despite the report's checklist implying they don't exist). **`site.webmanifest`/`sitemap_index.xml` 404s were correctly self-assessed by the report itself as non-issues** (manifest.json and sitemap.xml already cover those needs) — confirmed manifest.json is dynamically linked via `i18n.js` (`injectManifestLink`-style code, not a static `<link>` tag), which is sufficient for real browsers' PWA install-prompt behavior even though a static-HTML scanner wouldn't see it.

**Lesson reinforced**: this is now several external "QA report" pastes in a row where the report's own specific technical claims — even ones with concrete-looking numbers and exact URLs — turned out to be wrong or scanner-artifact-driven on verification, while a couple of genuinely real, minor, previously-unflagged gaps (favicon.ico, security.txt this time) were mixed in. **Keep verifying every specific, checkable claim before acting** — including ones that "feel" alarming or come with precise numbers, since precision doesn't imply accuracy — but don't dismiss a whole report just because its majority is wrong; a quick scan for the few genuinely actionable items is worth the time.

## External "in-depth probe" report, 2026-09-15 — mostly false, one real fix applied

User pasted another external report, more measured than the previous ones, claiming: 8 URLs with 1-word placeholder meta descriptions ("Today"/"Adawati"), 11 sensitive paths tested clean, 3 calculator logic findings (BMI good, compound-interest silent failure, age-calculator no feedback on future dates), and 128 internal links with 0 broken.

**Verified each claim before acting**:
- **Meta description claim: FALSE.** Fetched all 8 URLs live — every one has a full, proper 60-150+ character description (e.g. "Today's gold price per gram in AED for 18K, 21K, 22K and 24K, updated live from the global gold spot price..."). The scanner almost certainly mis-parsed the possessive apostrophe (`Today's` → `Today`, `Adawati's` → `Adawati`), truncating its own extraction to the first word and misreporting it as the entire description.
- **Sensitive path exposure: TRUE, confirmed clean** (`.env`, `.git/HEAD`, `package.json`, `admin/`, `.DS_Store` all 404 live) — a real, accurate finding, just confirming existing good posture, not a gap.
- **BMI / compound-interest calculator finding: partially true, but the suggested fix was wrong.** Confirmed BMI does show a visible red error box (`bmi_invalid`) on invalid input, and compound-interest does silently hide the result on `P<=0||r<=0||t<=0`. But BMI is wired to a **Calculate button click** while compound-interest uses **live `oninput`** (fires every keystroke) — showing an error message on every keystroke while a user is still typing a number (e.g., typed "1" of "15") would be a genuine UX regression, and is inconsistent with the same silent-hide-while-typing pattern used deliberately across most other live-`oninput` calculators sitewide (percentage-calculator, vat-calculator, etc.). **Not changed** — the report's observation was accurate but its recommended fix would have made the UX worse for this specific interaction model.
- **Age-calculator future-date finding: TRUE, and genuinely worth fixing** — unlike compound-interest, the birthdate field uses `onchange` (fires only after a complete, deliberate date-picker selection, never mid-input), so silently returning with zero feedback really is a UX gap here. **Fixed**: added a `future_date_error` translation key (ar/en/fr/es/de/ru, Arabic written in MSA since this is a root generic tool, not a `/jo/` page) and a dedicated `#ageError` box (reusing the existing `.result-box.danger` style already used by BMI) that shows "Birth date must be in the past" when triggered.
- **128 internal links, 0 broken: plausible and not re-verified at length** — this scanner's own reported number (0 broken) is consistent with the real, correct behavior already confirmed via the base-href investigation in the previous report's debunking, so no reason to doubt it.

**Real implementation bug caught and fixed before pushing, worth remembering**: the first attempt at the age-calculator fix wrote the error message directly into `#result`'s `innerHTML`, which **destroyed the pre-existing child elements** (`#ageMain`, `#ageSub`, `#stat-years`, etc.) that the success path's code expects to already exist in the DOM — this would have permanently broken the calculator after the *first* time a user hit a future date, since every subsequent valid calculation would throw `Cannot read properties of null` trying to update elements that no longer existed. **Fixed by adding a separate sibling `#ageError` element instead of ever touching `#result`'s internal structure.** Caught via a real functional CDP test (not just "no syntax error") that specifically exercised the sequence: trigger the error, then enter a valid date afterward, and confirmed the calculator still works — this is exactly the kind of two-step regression a syntax-only check would have missed.

**Second real bug caught in the test harness itself, not the site**: the first local-server test setup (a minimal `http.createServer` with no `Content-Type` header) made Chrome render the page source as plain text instead of parsing it as HTML — `getElementById()` returned `null` for *every* element, including long-pre-existing ones like `#result`, which looked exactly like a broken page until traced to the missing `Content-Type: text/html` header on the test server. **Lesson: any future ad-hoc local static server used for testing must explicitly set `Content-Type` per file extension** — Chrome does not sniff HTML from an untyped response the way it might from a real web server's defaults.

Validated (`JSON.parse`+`new Function()`+div-balance) on all 6 lang variants, `node build-lang-pages.js` re-run, pushed (first attempt failed entirely under GitHub's secondary rate limit, second succeeded), verified live via blob-sha diff + `curl`, and both `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run clean afterward.

## om/index.html mixed-language bug — fixed 2026-09-15

User-reported via two real mobile screenshots (not an external scanner report): with the site set to English and country set to Oman, `om/index.html`'s hero privacy badge, its OMR-to-INR tool card, and the entire "📅 يوميات" (Daily Info) section (holidays/fuel-prices/gold-price/weather/prayer-times/emergency-numbers cards) still rendered in Arabic. Investigation found the same root cause already documented elsewhere in this file for `jo/index.html`'s mixed-language bug: `i18n.js`'s `applyTranslations()` only touches elements carrying a `data-i18n` attribute — these specific elements were built (by the earlier "5-module country expansion" and "daily-info modules" forks) with hardcoded Arabic text and **no `data-i18n` attribute at all**, so the language switch never reached them.

**Scope, once actually read in full**: beyond the two elements the screenshots showed, the same bare-Arabic pattern also covered the `#decisionsSection` (car-cost/wedding-cost/university-cost cards + its own header) and — found while fixing the featured OMR card — **4 more currency-conversion cards** (`omr-to-pkr`/`bdt`/`egp`/`php`) in the page's separate, larger searchable `#toolsGrid` grid, which have their own independent (undocumented-in-the-screenshots) instances of the identical bug.

**Fixed**: added 30 new i18n keys (`om_omr_title`/`om_omr_desc`, `om_pkr_title`/`om_pkr_desc`, `om_bdt_title`/`om_bdt_desc`, `om_egp_title`/`om_egp_desc`, `om_php_title`/`om_php_desc`, `om_daily_label`, `om_holidays_title`/`desc`, `om_fuel_title`/`desc`, `om_gold_title`/`desc`, `om_weather_title`/`desc`, `om_prayer_title`/`desc`, `om_emergency_title`/`desc`, `om_decisions_label`, `om_carcost_title`/`desc`, `om_wedding_title`/`desc`, `om_unicost_title`/`desc`) across all 6 languages (ar/en/fr/es/de/ru) in `i18n.js`, matching the exact wording already used on `ae/index.html`'s equivalent English cards where a direct AE↔OM equivalent existed (adapted for Oman's own already-verified single-unified-9999 emergency framing and "Omani" degree wording, not copied verbatim — AE uses a 999/998/997 three-number scheme, a different fact). Wired `data-i18n` onto every corresponding `om/index.html` element; arrow elements reuse the existing shared `start` key like every other card on the page.

**Real mistake made and self-caught mid-edit**: a Node script inserting the initial privacy-badge key across all 6 language blocks in one pass, driven by pre-computed (and therefore stale-after-each-insertion) line numbers, wrote Arabic text into the Russian block instead of a Russian translation — caught via `grep -n "om_badge_privacy" i18n.js` showing the same Arabic string appearing twice, fixed via a targeted `Edit`. The subsequent, larger batch of 22+8 new keys used a safer `indexOf`-with-advancing-cursor insertion script instead of precomputed line numbers, specifically to avoid repeating this class of bug — verified afterward via `grep` that each language block shows only its own language's text, not a duplicate.

**Real testing gotcha hit, worth remembering for any future local-server test of a page carrying `<base href="https://adawati.space/">`**: an ad-hoc local static server (`http://localhost:8900`) initially made all i18n testing look broken — `T.en.om_omr_title` (and every other new key) came back `undefined` in a CDP check, even though the file on disk was correct. Root cause: the page's own `<base href="https://adawati.space/">` makes the `<script src="../i18n.js?v=42">` tag resolve against the *live production domain*, not the local server, so the browser was silently loading the **old, unedited, live** `i18n.js` instead of the local one being tested — the exact same class of gotcha already documented in this file for the mobile-nav-collapse and quiz-result-sharing features, just newly hit here too. **Fixed by testing via CDP's `Fetch` domain instead of a bare local server**: enabled `Fetch.enable` with `urlPattern: "https://adawati.space/*"`, navigated directly to the real `https://adawati.space/om/index.html` URL, and fulfilled every intercepted request from local disk instead of the network — this is a more general, reusable version of the "isolated preview page" workaround used in past sessions, since it works for a full un-modified page (not just an isolated snippet) and needs no page rewriting. **Worth adopting as the standard technique for any future local test of a page using `<base href>`** — simpler and more faithful than manually building an isolated preview page each time.

Validated (`JSON.parse` — n/a, `i18n.js` isn't JSON but `node --check` passed; `new Function()`/div-balance on `om/index.html`, 0 errors), functionally tested via the Fetch-interception CDP technique above confirming every previously-Arabic element now correctly shows English in EN mode and confirming Arabic mode is unaffected (regression-free) with zero JS errors. Pushed via a targeted 2-file push (i18n.js + om/index.html) after a full-tree push attempt failed outright under GitHub's secondary rate limit; verified live via blob-sha diff (both files byte-identical to local) and `curl` (the only diff between local and live `om/index.html` is the CSP nonce the Cloudflare Worker injects into inline `<script>` tags — expected, not a discrepancy). `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run clean afterward, including a zero-console-errors check specifically on `/om/index.html`.

**The 5 `oman-*-guide.html` link cards further down `om/index.html`'s searchable grid** (`data-cat="oman"`, linking to `oman-tax-guide.html` etc.) have the same bare-Arabic-hardcoded-text pattern but were **deliberately left unfixed this round** — they're a different card family (guide-page links, not calculator tools) and lower-signal than what was actually reported; flagged here in case a future pass wants to close this out too.

## Cross-hub mixed-language bug, widened scope — fixed 2026-09-15

After the om/index.html fix above, the user kept finding the same bug class live in the browser on other hubs ("لسا هون في اشي" — "there's still something here"), first on `sa/index.html`'s popularSection VAT card, then on `jo/index.html`'s hero stats row. Rather than keep whack-a-moling from screenshots, ran a full audit of all 6 country hubs' `.tool-card-title`/`.tool-card-desc`/`.stat-item-lbl` elements for missing `data-i18n`, in both directions (Arabic hardcoded on ar-default hubs, English hardcoded on en-default hubs).

**Real, separate, more serious root cause found on `jo/index.html` and `sa/index.html`**: both had an **unconditional** `localStorage.setItem('lang', 'ar');` in their own inline `<script>` (running synchronously, before the deferred `i18n.js` tag) — this silently overwrote any language the visitor had already chosen (on this page or any other) back to Arabic **on every single page load**, before `i18n.js`'s own `detectDefaultLang()` even ran. `om`/`ae`/`us`/`uk`/the global `index.html` all correctly use the guarded form (`if (!localStorage.getItem('lang')) localStorage.setItem('lang', 'ar');`/`'en'`) — only default if nothing was chosen yet. This fully explains the user's repeated reports: navigating to jo or sa (even mid-session, even right after explicitly picking English elsewhere) silently reset the page to Arabic, and any of the missing-`data-i18n` elements below would then never have gotten a chance to show English at all regardless of the other fixes. **Fixed** by changing both to the guarded form, matching the already-correct pattern on the other 4 hubs.

**Missing `data-i18n` found and fixed, by hub**:
- **`jo`**: hero stats-row "أداة ودليل"/"🔒 خصوصية تامة" (wired to `stat_tools` / new `stat_privacy_short`), plus all 14 `jo/games/*` challenge cards in **both** of jo's card sections (the legacy un-tabbed list and the tabbed `data-cat="challenges"` grid, 28 elements total) — reused the new shared `g_*` keys (see below). **Jo's own ~35 Jordan-specific tool cards** (tawjihi-average, university-gpa, income-tax, package-customs, decisions bundle, daily-info cards, etc.) were **deliberately left untouched** — this is the established site convention that Jordan-vertical content is Jordanian-dialect Arabic by design, not a bug of the same class; translating ~35 cards to English is a real, separate, much larger scope decision that needs an explicit ask, not a mechanical fix.
- **`om`**: one remaining stats-row item ("🔒 معظم الأدوات محلية" → `stat_privacy_short`, cosmetic text now reads "خصوصية تامة" to match the shared key) and all 13 `jo/games/*` challenge cards (om doesn't have logo-quiz).
- **`sa`**: stats-row ("أداة ودليل" → `stat_tools`; "VAT السعودية" → new `sa_stat_vat_lbl`), the popularSection VAT card (title+desc → new `sa_vat_card_title`/`sa_vat_card_desc`), **7 more popularSection card descs** that had a translated title but a hardcoded Arabic desc (currency/loan/hijri/compound-interest/bmi/percentage/date-diff — new `sa_*_card_desc` keys preserving the exact existing sa-specific wording), one more stray `data-cat="oman"`-tagged card pair (Gulf VAT + Hijri, unreachable via any of sa's real tabs but still shown under the `activeCat==='all'` default — same latent-card pattern documented elsewhere in this file; only the VAT one's desc needed a data-i18n fix here since Hijri's was already wired) all 6 dailySection/decisionsSection cards (reusing `om_*` keys, since the Arabic text was byte-identical to om's), and all 13 `jo/games/*` challenge cards.
- **`ae`/`us`/`uk`** (English-default — same bug, mirrored: hardcoded English with no Arabic translation, so switching to Arabic left these stuck in English): stats-row country-specific labels (`ae_stat_vat_lbl`, `us_stat_tip_lbl`/`us_stat_units_lbl`, `uk_stat_vat_lbl`/`uk_stat_rates_lbl`), the ae VAT popularSection card (`ae_vat_card_title`/`desc`), us's Mortgage Calculator + Unit Converter popularSection cards (`us_mortgage_card_title`/`desc`, reused `unit_card_title` + new `us_unit_card_desc`), uk's Percentage/Mortgage/Unit Converter popularSection cards (reused `pct_card_title`/`unit_card_title` + new `uk_percentage_card_desc`/`uk_mortgage_card_title`/`desc`/`uk_unit_card_desc`), all 6 dailySection/decisionsSection cards each (reusing `om_*` keys for the identical parts — holidays/fuel/gold/weather/prayer/car-cost/wedding-cost text was byte-identical to om's own English translations — plus new per-country keys only where the underlying fact differs: `{ae,us,uk}_emergency_desc`, `{ae,sa}_unicost_desc`, `us_unicost_title`/`desc` since US's card is literally named "College Cost Calculator" not "University Cost Calculator", `uk_unicost_desc`), and all 14 `jo/games/*` challenge cards each (13 shared + logo-quiz, new `g_logoquiz_title`/`desc`).

**Reuse strategy, to avoid key sprawl**: rather than give every hub its own prefixed key for genuinely identical content, reused the existing `om_*` daily/decisions keys (created for the original om fix) as the de-facto shared keys wherever the Arabic text (om/sa) or English text (ae/us/uk) was byte-identical — confirmed this by direct diff before reusing, not assumed. Only added new per-country keys where the underlying fact/wording genuinely differs (emergency numbers, degree-cost descriptions, VAT-specific popularSection cards). Also reused already-existing generic keys (`stat_tools`, `unit_card_title`, `pct_card_title`) where a hub's wording was an exact or near-exact match, accepting a cosmetic text change on the 1-2 instances where wording differed slightly (e.g. uk's "Percentage Calculator" now reads "Percentage & Discount" to match the sitewide canonical name already established in the 2026-09-10 naming-consistency fix — this was already the intended canonical name, this hub instance had just been missed). Game-card keys (`g_*`) are **ar+en only**, matching the established "English only for trivia content" scope convention — `g_logoquiz_desc`'s Arabic value was set to match jo's own pre-existing wording exactly (not a fresh translation) specifically so wiring `data-i18n` wouldn't change jo's default Arabic display text.

**Real script bug hit and caught mid-session, worth remembering**: an early version of the game-card-wiring script built its replacement string as a template literal containing literal `$1`/`$3`/`$5`/`$7` placeholders concatenated with the real captured group text, then called `.replace(literalSubstring, ...)` on that string (looking for the wrapper divs, which weren't there) before ever passing it to `src.replace(re, replacementString)` — but passing a string containing `$1` etc. as the *replacement* argument to `String.prototype.replace(regex, string)` makes JS treat those as **backreferences to the original regex's capture groups**, which silently reconstructed the exact original matched text with **zero net change** — the script printed "wired 13 of 13" while actually being a complete no-op. Caught immediately by grepping the output file for the expected `data-i18n` attribute and finding it absent. Fixed by building the replacement via plain string concatenation (`m[1] + newAttr + m[2] + ...`) using `slice`+insert instead of `.replace(regex, string)` at all. **Lesson: never build a `.replace(regex, replacementString)` replacement string that happens to contain literal `$N` sequences — JS will reinterpret them as backreferences regardless of your intent; always verify a batch-replace script's actual file output, not just its own success log, especially when the "count changed" logic can't distinuish a real edit from a no-op reconstruction.**

**Verification**: all 6 hub files + `i18n.js` validated (`new Function()` on every inline script, 0 errors). Functionally tested via the CDP `Fetch`-domain interception technique (documented in the earlier om fix section) against `https://adawati.space/*` URLs pointing at local files — confirmed, for every hub, that switching to English translates every previously-broken element with zero remaining untranslated Arabic-with-no-`data-i18n` elements except the two deliberately-scoped exceptions (jo's own ~35 tool cards, om's 5 `oman-*-guide.html` cards, both already documented). Also specifically re-tested the `localStorage.lang` persistence fix (set 'en' on a previous page, reload jo/sa, confirm `document.documentElement.lang` stays 'en' instead of reverting to 'ar') and a genuine fresh-visit-with-no-stored-language regression check (confirming jo/om/sa still correctly default to Arabic and ae/us/uk to English for new visitors, unaffected by the guard-condition fix). Pushed via a targeted 7-file push (i18n.js + all 6 hub `index.html`), verified live via blob-sha diff (all 7 byte-identical to local) and a final CDP run against the **real live** `https://adawati.space` domain (not local files) reproducing the identical zero-untranslated-elements result. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) both re-run clean.

**Deliberately left for a future, separate pass** (flagged here, not silently dropped): jo's own ~35 Jordan-specific tool cards untranslated to English (a real scope decision, not a quick fix); om's 5 `oman-*-guide.html` cards (same as before); sa's stray `data-cat="oman"` VAT/Hijri card pair being unreachable via any real sa tab (the same latent-card display bug documented earlier in this file for other hubs — worth a proper audit of whether other hubs have similar stray `data-cat` values from copy-pasted templates, not just the already-fixed Oman-only-tool-card instance).

User said "Fix all" to the three items explicitly deferred at the end of the previous section. All three closed out in this pass:

**1. jo/index.html's own ~22 unique Jordan-specific tool cards translated to English** (43 total elements wired, since each card appears in both jo's legacy un-tabbed section and its tabbed `data-cat` grid, plus one card — "Best Tools for Students" — that only appears once). New `jo_*`-prefixed keys (ar+en only, matching this hub's existing English-optional scope convention already established for `jo_hero_*`): car-cost, income-tax, package-customs, rent-vs-buy, social-security, tawjihi-average, university-cost, university-gpa, wedding-cost, zakat, emergency-numbers, fuel-prices, jordan-quiz, wordle, gold-price, holidays, prayer-times, best-tools, scholarships-loans, government-vs-private, jordan-vs-gulf, weather.
