Stray unreachable `data-cat="oman"` cards fixed on jo, sa, AND ae** (not just sa — a fresh audit per-hub of `cat-tab` values vs. actual card `data-cat` values found the identical bug on jo and ae too, both with a VAT-Gulf-calculator card + a Hijri-converter card carrying `data-cat="oman"` despite neither hub having a real "oman" tab — `us`/`uk` were confirmed clean, `om` is correctly `data-cat="oman"` since it legitimately has its own "oman" tab). **Fixed by recategorizing, not deleting** — unlike the original 2026-09-14 "Oman-only tool cards" bug fix (which deleted cards that could never legitimately belong on that hub), a Gulf-wide VAT calculator and a Hijri date converter are genuinely useful, on-topic tools for any of these hubs; the fix was simply giving them the *correct* category (`data-cat="money"` for VAT, `data-cat="quick"` for Hijri) so they become reachable via a real tab click instead of only via search. Verified via a live CDP test that clicking each hub's "money"/"quick" tab now actually surfaces these cards.

**Verification**: all 5 touched files (`i18n.js`, `jo/om/sa/ae/index.html`) validated (`new Function()` on every inline script, 0 errors). Re-ran the same untranslated-element sweep used in the prior section across all 6 hubs — **result: 0 untranslated elements on every single hub**, closing out this bug class completely (previously om/sa were 0 already, jo dropped from 94 → 8 → 0 across this pass). Fresh-visit language-default regression re-confirmed unaffected (jo/om/sa still Arabic, ae/us/uk still English, 0 JS errors across all 6 navigations). Pushed via a targeted 5-file push, verified live via blob-sha diff (all 5 byte-identical) and a final CDP run against **live production** reproducing the identical 0-untranslated-elements result on all 6 hubs with 0 JS errors. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) both re-run clean.

**This closes the entire cross-hub mixed-language bug thread** — no further deferred items remain from this investigation.

## CRITICAL, ACTIVE BUG — enforced CSP blocks ALL inline event-handler attributes site-wide, not just onclick — found 2026-09-16

**This is a live production regression, likely affecting a large number of calculators right now.** The 2026-09-14 onclick→addEventListener refactor project (documented at length above) was scoped to `onclick="..."` attributes only. But the CSP enforced the same day (see "CSP enforced in production" above) blocks **every** inline event-handler attribute — `oninput`, `onchange`, `onkeydown`, `onkeyup`, etc. — not just `onclick`. Per spec, `script-src` (with no separate `script-src-attr` directive) governs inline event-handler attributes too, and once a nonce is present the same graceful-degradation rule that lets nonce'd `<script>` tags ignore `'unsafe-inline'` also applies here — except **there is no way to put a nonce on an inline attribute**, so any page relying on `oninput`/`onchange`/etc. for its interactivity silently stopped working the moment CSP enforcement went live, with **zero console-visible error** in a normal check (confirmed only via a `document.addEventListener('securitypolicyviolation', ...)` listener, which reports `script-src-attr:inline` — this does not surface as a `Runtime.exceptionThrown` event, so none of this project's usual "0 JS errors" CDP checks would have caught it).

**Confirmed broken, live, via direct reproduction**: `jo/calculators/social-security/` (the existing, previously-shipped contribution calculator — `oninput="calc()"` on all 4 wage fields) and `jo/calculators/tawjihi-average/` were both directly confirmed non-functional in production via this exact test. Given `oninput`/`onchange` is a extremely common pattern across this codebase's calculators (used long before the onclick-specific refactor project existed), **this almost certainly affects many more pages than these two** — every calculator built with live-`oninput` recalculation (the pattern explicitly described as "most other live-`oninput` calculators sitewide" in the compound-interest/BMI discussion above) is a candidate. This was not caught by the original CSP-enforcement verification pass because that pass checked for `Runtime.exceptionThrown` and console-log CSP text, not `securitypolicyviolation` events specifically for `script-src-attr`.

**How to check a given page**: navigate to it, install `document.addEventListener('securitypolicyviolation', e => console.log(e.violatedDirective))`, then dispatch a real `input`/`change` event on one of its fields — `script-src-attr:inline` in the output means it's broken. A `new Function()`/console-error check alone will NOT catch this.

**Fixed so far** (2026-09-16): `jo/calculators/social-security-pension/` (built same session, caught before considering it shipped), `jo/calculators/social-security/` (user-reported live via a screenshot — see the dedicated section below), and `jo/calculators/tawjihi-average/` (fixed as an explicit follow-up request right after) — all converted from `oninput="calc()"` attributes to `element.addEventListener('input', calc)` wired in a `DOMContentLoaded` block. **The two specific pages this section originally named as confirmed-broken are now both fixed and live-verified** (`social-security/`: real screenshot values 800+100+50+100 JOD reproduced live, correct 971.250 JOD net; `tawjihi-average/`: the page's own worked example reproduced live, 85.07% — see that page's own note on a pre-existing, unrelated floating-point rounding quirk). **This does NOT mean the site-wide bug is closed** — only these 3 specific pages have been checked and fixed; the likely 50-150+ file scope described below has not been swept. Any other page using `oninput`/`onchange` for live recalculation should still be assumed broken until individually checked with the `securitypolicyviolation` method below.

**If this is picked up**: reuse the exact process already established for the onclick refactor (grep for `oninput=`/`onchange=`/`onkeydown=`/`onkeyup=` across all HTML, strip the attribute, add `element.addEventListener(eventType, handler)` in a plain script block positioned after the element in source order — no `DOMContentLoaded` wrapper needed if the script tag is already below the elements in the HTML, matching this project's own established convention, e.g. this file's own gender-toggle buttons and the fix above), then verify each fixed page with the `securitypolicyviolation`-listener method above (not just "no exception thrown"), functionally test via CDP that the calculator's result actually updates on input, push, and verify live via blob-sha diff. Given the likely scope, treat this as its own dedicated project, not something to fold into an unrelated task — flag it to the user before starting a full sweep given the size.

## Jordan Social Security pension calculator — built 2026-09-16

`jo/calculators/social-security-pension/index.html` — old-age (تقاعد الشيخوخة) and early-retirement (التقاعد المبكر) pension estimator, complementing the existing `jo/calculators/social-security/` (which computes ongoing *contributions*, not the eventual *pension payout* — these are two different calculators, cross-linked both ways).

**Formula, verified directly against the primary source** (Social Security Law No. 1 of 2014 as amended, full text fetched from `ssc.gov.jo`'s own published PDF — not a secondary source): Articles 62 (old-age) and 64 (early retirement), plus the attached Table 5 (early-retirement age-based reduction percentages) and Table 4 (dependents' increment, cross-referenced against Article 62(c)/64(d)(3) directly since Table 4 itself covers survivor-benefit shares, a different topic — the dependents percentages used here come from the article text, not Table 4).

- **Base pension**: 2.5% × years of contribution on the first 1,500 JOD of average monthly wage, + 2% × years of contribution on the excess above 1,500 JOD.
- **Old-age eligibility**: age 60 (male) / 55 (female) + at least 180 contributions. Full base pension, no reduction.
- **Early-retirement eligibility** (the *general* path, Article 64(a) — see scope note below): age ≥50 with ≥252 actual contributions (male) or ≥228 (female), OR age ≥45 with ≥300 actual contributions regardless of gender.
- **Early-retirement reduction**: base pension reduced by an age-bucketed percentage per Table 5 — 20% at age 45 down to 2% at age 59 for men (15 one-year buckets), 14% at 45 down to 2% at 54 for women (10 buckets, since women's normal retirement age is 55).
- **Dependents increment**: +12% of pension for the 1st dependent (min 10, max 100 JOD) + 6% each for a 2nd and 3rd dependent (min 10, max 25 JOD each) — capped at 3 dependents, matches the exact wording in both Article 62(c) and 64(d)(3).

**Deliberately out of scope, flagged transparently in the page's own disclaimer** (this law has extensive grandfather/transitional provisions that would multiply the complexity far beyond what a simple estimator can responsibly cover):
- The *newest* early-retirement path (Article 64(h), for insured persons whose *first-ever* SSC enrollment happened after the current amendment) has stricter conditions (age 55/252 male, 52/228 female) and its *own separate* reduction table embedded in 64(h)(3) — not Table 5. Since most of the current workforce was insured before this amendment, Article 64(a)'s general path (used by this calculator) is the broadly-applicable one, but very recently-first-insured workers should verify their own case.
- Hazardous-profession early retirement (Article 64(c) — different age/contribution thresholds).
- The pre-2014 transitional early-retirement paths (Article 64(b) — depends on the person's exact age *before* the law took effect, now decreasingly relevant with time).
- The anti-wage-spike averaging cap (Articles 62(d)/64(d)(4) — the law caps how much the average-wage calculation can be inflated by a large raise shortly before retirement; this calculator has no way to know the wage 4-5 years prior, so it isn't applied).
- Disability/incapacity pensions (راتب اعتلال) and survivor/death pensions (راتب تقاعد الوفاة) — entirely separate benefit categories under this same law, not covered here.
- Military/police insured persons (Article 71 excludes them — separate system).

**Verification method**: formula and all percentages checked directly against the official law PDF (not a news article or secondary summary) — fetched `ssc.gov.jo`'s own consolidated law text and read Articles 62/64 and Table 5 in full before writing any number. 4 hand-calculated test cases (old-age with 2 dependents, early retirement via the age-50 path, non-eligible at age 40, early retirement via the age-45/300-contribution path for a woman with 1 dependent) all matched the calculator's live output exactly, confirmed via CDP against both a local Fetch-intercepted copy and the final live production page.

**Found and fixed during this build — see the CSP section immediately above**: the calculator initially used `oninput="calc()"` (matching this file family's existing established pattern, e.g. `jo/calculators/social-security/index.html`), which turned out to be silently broken by the site's own enforced CSP. Caught via live CDP testing before considering the page shipped (the live production test showed empty result fields despite 0 thrown JS errors) — converted to `addEventListener('input', calc)` before finalizing.

## Saudi GOSI retirement pension calculator — built 2026-09-16

`sa/calculators/gosi-pension/index.html` — a GOSI (General Organization for Social Insurance) old-age/early-retirement pension estimator, the Saudi analog of the Jordan pension calculator above, built as idea #2 from a background research fork investigating real-search-demand tool ideas for Jordan/Gulf markets (idea #1, the Jordan pension calculator, was built first the same day; a fresh research fork was launched immediately after this one to regenerate a full ranked idea list, per explicit user instruction — see whatever section documents that fork's outcome for what came of it).

**Formula, verified directly against the primary source**: fetched the actual **Social Insurance Law** (Royal Decree No. M/273, dated 26/12/1445H, effective 3 July 2024) — the official English translation published by the Bureau of Experts at the Council of Ministers (`misa.gov.sa`), not a secondary HR-blog summary (several were checked first via WebSearch and found to disagree with each other on the exact contribution-rate/retirement-age numbers, which is exactly the kind of conflict this project's verification policy exists to catch). Read Part 2 ("Annuities Branch", Articles 14-27) in full.

- **Base pension** (Article 17.1): 2.25% × average contributory wage (highest 180 contribution months, Article 26.1) × months of contribution ÷ 12, capped at 100% of that average wage.
- **Normal retirement** (Article 16.1-2): legal age 65, minimum 180 months (15 years) of contribution — full base pension, subject to the minimum-pension floor below.
- **Minimum pension floor** (Article 17.2): SAR 4,000/month at 480 months (40 years) contribution; SAR 2,000/month at the 180-month minimum; linear interpolation between. Applied only to normal (age-65) retirement in this calculator — see the ambiguity note below.
- **Early retirement** (Article 16.3): available up to 120 months (10 years) before legal age — i.e. as early as 55 — if the contributor has stopped GOSI-covered employment and has at least 360 months (30 years) of contribution.
- **Early-retirement reduction** (Article 17.3): 3% per 12 months, applied not to the full gap-to-65 but to `min(months before legal age, months short of 480)` — i.e., a longer contribution history softens the early-retirement penalty. Confirmed via hand-worked examples before coding (e.g., exactly 30 years contributed retiring at exactly 55 → both gaps are 120 months → full 30% reduction; 35 years retiring at 58 → 84 months early but only 60 months short of 480 → reduction capped at 15%, not 21%).
- **No gender distinction and no dependents increment** — unlike Jordan's law, this Saudi law's retirement-age/reduction rules are gender-neutral and the retiree's own pension amount carries no per-dependent boost while alive (family-member survivor benefits are a separate, later article not covered by this calculator) — so the calculator has no gender toggle and no dependents field, a genuine (not accidental) difference from the Jordan page's inputs.
- **Contributory wage cap**: SAR 45,000/month (Article 8.2) — user-entered wages above this are silently capped in the calculation, with an on-page note explaining why.

**Deliberately out of scope, flagged transparently in the page's own disclaimer** — this law has an even larger transitional-provisions problem than Jordan's, found while reading the Royal Decree's own transitional clauses (not the Law's numbered articles, which only describe the *steady-state* rules used above):
- **The transitional retirement-age schedule** (Royal Decree Clause Fifth): anyone who was already contributing to GOSI before the law's 3 July 2024 effective date, was under 50 Hijri years old then, and had less than 240 months (20 years) of pre-2024 contribution, follows a **sliding retirement age from 58 years 4 months up to 65** depending on their exact age in 2024 (a full lookup table in the decree, not a formula) — this is not a rare edge case, it's the rule that actually governs most of the current workforce for years to come, but computing it needs the person's exact age *in 2024*, not just their current age, so it isn't modeled here.
- **Full carve-out to the old law**: anyone with 240+ months (20 years) of pre-2024 contribution, or already entitled to a pension before the effective date, is excluded from this new law entirely (Royal Decree Clause Second) and stays under the old Social Insurance Law (M/33) / Civil Pension Law — completely different rules this calculator doesn't attempt.
- **Minimum-floor vs. early-retirement-reduction interaction**: Article 17(3) says the reduction applies to "the pension amount provided for in paragraph (1)" (the raw formula), not explicitly to the floored amount from paragraph (2) — the public law text doesn't fully resolve the order of operations, and the answer likely lives in unpublished implementing regulations. This calculator applies the reduction to the raw formula and applies the minimum floor only to normal (non-early) retirement, and says so explicitly rather than guessing which order GOSI's own systems actually use.
- Disability, survivor/death pensions, the gradual 18%→22% contribution-rate phase-in (2024-2028 — doesn't affect the pension payout formula itself, only the paycheck deduction), and the late-retirement +3%/year bonus for working past 65 (Article 17.4) — all separate topics, not modeled.

**Built CSP-safe from the start** (the project has an active, unfixed site-wide bug where enforced CSP silently blocks `oninput`/`onchange` attributes — see the CRITICAL BUG section above): every input on this page uses `element.addEventListener('input', calc)` wired in a `DOMContentLoaded` block, no inline event-handler attributes anywhere — confirmed 0 via a `<tag ...on(click|input|change)=` regex sweep of the finished file, and confirmed live via a `document.addEventListener('securitypolicyviolation', ...)` listener returning an empty array after real `input` events were dispatched on the live production page.

**Verification**: 8 hand-calculated test cases (normal retirement at various contribution lengths, early retirement at the max-reduction boundary and a softened-reduction case, sub-180-month lump-sum-only case, not-yet-eligible cases for both the "wait to 65" and "need 360 months + stop working" branches, floor-applies and floor-doesn't-apply cases) run against a standalone Node reproduction of the exact formula before ever touching the HTML — all 8 matched hand derivation exactly. Then re-verified live via CDP against the pushed production page (not just local files): filled real inputs, dispatched real `input` events, read the rendered result back out of the DOM for the normal-retirement, early-retirement, and lump-sum-ineligible cases, plus the language toggle — all matched the standalone reproduction's numbers exactly, 0 CSP violations, 0 JS errors. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run clean afterward as a broader regression check, since this build also touched the shared `i18n.js` (2 new additive keys) and `sa/index.html`.

**Integration**: added to `sa/index.html`'s `decisionsSection` grid (alongside car-cost/wedding-cost/university-cost), cross-linked to `sa/calculators/car-cost/`, `sa/vat-calculator/`, `sa/gold-price/`; added to `build-sitemap.js`/`sitemap.xml` (now 303 URLs) and `build-search-index.js`/`search-index.json` (137 entries). Self-contained per-page AR/EN toggle (`toggleLang()`/`LANG_AR`/`LANG_EN`), matching the established pattern for `sa/calculators/car-cost/` and the rest of the country-module family — not wired into `i18n.js`'s global translation system, except for the two new hub-card keys (`sa_gosi_title`/`sa_gosi_desc`, ar+en only) needed so the hub card itself doesn't fall into the mixed-language bug class already fixed extensively elsewhere in this file.

## UAE and Saudi end-of-service gratuity calculators — built 2026-09-16

`ae/calculators/end-of-service/index.html` and `sa/calculators/end-of-service/index.html` — items #1 and #2 from the fresh research fork's ranked idea list (both flagged there as the best-ROI candidates: strong demand evidence, small-medium effort, minimal regulatory ambiguity). Built together per explicit user instruction ("Both").

**UAE formula, verified via WebSearch cross-referencing multiple independent sources** (a UAE government platform page 404'd when fetched directly, so this relied on corroboration across several detailed, article-citing secondary sources — Khaleej Times, Bayzat, BCL Globiz, Access Financial, edarabia, gulfnews — all of which agreed exactly on every number, a strong signal for a decades-simpler law than Saudi's GOSI reform): Federal Decree-Law No. 33 of 2021, Article 51.
- Daily wage = basic monthly salary ÷ 30.
- Gratuity = 21 days × daily wage × min(years, 5) + 30 days × daily wage × max(years − 5, 0). Fractions of a year pro-rated.
- **Hard cutoff, not pro-rated**: under 1 year of continuous service = zero gratuity (unlike Oman's Article 61, which pro-rates even sub-1-year service — a genuine difference between the two laws' 2021-era reforms, worth remembering if these get confused).
- **The 2021 law abolished the old resignation penalty** — resignation and termination now get the identical amount (multiple sources independently confirmed this), so unlike the Saudi calculator below, this page has no reason/resignation toggle at all.
- Capped at 24× basic monthly salary (2 years' basic wage) — rarely binds in practice (needs ~29+ years of continuous service under the standard formula before it does), but implemented and hand-verified anyway (a 40-year/SAR-5000 test case confirmed the cap correctly engages).
- Excluded, disclosed transparently: free-zone-specific rules (which can differ from mainland MOHRE rules), civil-service/government employment (not covered by this law at all), and gross-misconduct dismissal under Article 44 (can forfeit gratuity entirely).

**Saudi formula, verified via WebSearch** (multiple independent, article-citing sources agreed exactly — this is a stable, decades-old law unlike the new GOSI reform, so cross-referenced secondary sources were treated as sufficiently reliable without chasing the primary Arabic legal text): Labor Law (Royal Decree M/51), Articles 84 and 85.
- Base benefit (Article 84): half a month's wage × min(years, 5) + a full month's wage × max(years − 5, 0), based on the last wage.
- **Termination/contract-end**: full Article 84 amount, no reduction.
- **Resignation** (Article 85) applies a service-length-tiered multiplier instead: 0% under 2 years, 1/3 from 2–5 years, 2/3 from 5–10 years, 100% at 10+ years — this is why, unlike the UAE page, this calculator has a reason toggle (termination vs. resignation) that materially changes the result.
- Excluded, disclosed transparently: disciplinary dismissal (Article 80, can forfeit the benefit) and the Article 81 exception where certain resignation reasons grant the full amount despite short tenure.

**Built CSP-safe from the start**, same discipline as the GOSI pension calculator above — `addEventListener` only, 0 inline event-handler attributes on either page, confirmed via regex sweep before pushing.

**Verification**: 4 UAE + 4 Saudi hand-calculated test cases run against a standalone Node reproduction of each formula before touching the HTML (UAE: a sub-5-year case, an above-5-year case matching a cited worked example exactly, a cap-triggering extreme-tenure case, and a sub-1-year ineligibility case; Saudi: a resignation case in the 5-10-year two-thirds tier, a resignation case past the 10-year full-entitlement threshold, a sub-2-year resignation ineligibility case, and a termination case showing no reduction applies) — all 8 matched exactly. Re-verified live via CDP against the pushed production pages: real `input` events dispatched, results read back from the DOM, matched the standalone reproduction exactly on all 6 live-tested cases, 0 CSP violations, 0 JS errors on either page.

**Integration**: added to `ae/index.html`'s and `sa/index.html`'s `decisionsSection` grids, cross-linked to each country's car-cost/VAT/gold-price tools (and to `sa/calculators/gosi-pension/` from the Saudi gratuity page, and vice versa), new `ae_gratuity_title`/`desc` and `sa_gratuity_title`/`desc` i18n keys (ar+en only, matching the established scope for this hub-card family). `build-sitemap.js`/`sitemap.xml` now 305 URLs, `search-index.json` 139 entries.

Verified live via the established technique (temporarily wrapping `exportResultImage` to capture its arguments without actually rendering/triggering a share) — confirmed the exact 8 rows and correct values before considering this done. This still leaves the generic `injectShareBtn()` link-share button appended below it, same as every other calculator on the site that has its own bespoke export button (e.g. `bmi-calculator.html`) — an existing, accepted site-wide pattern, not something this fix changed.

## Jordan tawjihi-average calculator — CSP-oninput bug fixed, 2026-09-16

Same bug class, same fix pattern as `jo/calculators/social-security/` immediately above — the other page the CRITICAL BUG section had named as confirmed-broken. Removed all 8 `oninput="calc()"` attributes (the 4 grade-11 shared-subject fields + 4 grade-12 specialization fields), wired `addEventListener('input', calc)` for all 8 in a `DOMContentLoaded` block.

**Verified live via CDP using the page's own published worked example** (Arabic 88, English 92, Islamic education 90, Jordan history 85, specialization marks 80/85/78/90 — expected per the page's own info card: grade-11 contribution 26.80%, grade-12 contribution 58.28%, final 85.08%): live result came back **85.07%** (grade-11 26.80% matched exactly, grade-12 came back 58.27% instead of 58.28%). **Root-caused as a pre-existing floating-point display quirk, not a bug introduced by this fix**: `(80+85+78+90) * 0.175` evaluates to exactly `58.275` as a real number, but JavaScript's `Number.prototype.toFixed(2)` rounds this down to `58.27` because `58.275` isn't exactly representable in IEEE 754 binary floating point (confirmed via a standalone `node -e` check reproducing the identical value) — this is unrelated to the `oninput`→`addEventListener` change (the `calc()` function's math was not touched) and was already present in the original, pre-fix `calc()` logic; the page's static example text (typed by hand when the page was built) just never matched what the live JS actually outputs. **Left as-is, not fixed** — a ±0.01-percentage-point cosmetic display rounding difference, flagged to the user rather than silently patched, since fixing it (e.g. adding a small epsilon before rounding) wasn't part of the requested CSP fix and wasn't asked for.

0 CSP violations, 0 JS errors confirmed live post-fix. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean afterward.

## Jordan pension calculator redesign — project early vs. normal retirement, 2026-09-16

User feedback (Jordanian dialect, casual): suggested adding "your expected pension" by asking current salary + expected salary at end of service for early or normal retirement. Investigated first rather than building the literal ask: the law's "average wage" base is specifically the **last 36 contributions (3 years) for normal retirement, or last 60 (5 years) for early** — not a lifetime average — so averaging "current salary" (which could be decades away from retirement) with "expected salary at retirement" would often produce a misleading number for anyone not already close to retiring. Proposed instead: a single field asking for expected wage specifically **near** retirement (the number that actually matters legally), and — since the user's own phrasing explicitly named both paths ("تقاعد مبكر او طبيعي") — projecting **both** early and normal retirement forward from the person's *current* age/contributions, rather than requiring them to already know a hypothetical target retirement age. Confirmed with the user before building ("طيب تمام" / "ابني ب فكرتك").

**Redesigned `jo/calculators/social-security-pension/index.html` inputs**: `عمرك عند التقاعد` (age at retirement) → `عمرك الحالي` (current age); `متوسط أجرك الشهري` (average wage) → `راتبك المتوقع بالسنين الأخيرة قبل التقاعد` (expected wage near retirement) — with the note explaining why (matches the 3-year/5-year legal averaging window, not a lifetime or current-salary average). Gender toggle and dependents field unchanged.

**New projection logic** (`calc()` rewritten): given current age + current contribution-months, computes the **earliest possible early-retirement age** by projecting continuous future contribution forward against both Article 64(a) paths (age≥50 + 252/228 contributions, or age≥45 + 300 contributions) — takes the earlier-qualifying of the two paths — then applies the existing age-bucketed reduction table at that projected age. Separately computes the **normal retirement** projection at the legal age (60/55), with contributions projected forward the same way. Both scenarios render as separate cards when they apply; if projected contributions at normal retirement age still fall short of the 180-contribution minimum, shows a single not-eligible message with the actual projected shortfall instead of either card. **A newly-disclosed assumption, added to the page's own "what this doesn't cover" list**: the projection assumes continuous, uninterrupted future contribution from the entered current age, at a wage matching the entered near-retirement figure — a real gap in someone's contribution history or a materially different final wage will change the real outcome.

**Verification**: 4 hand-calculated scenarios (a 35-year-old male with a 10-year head start, a 40-year-old female with dependents, a 52-year-old male already past the early-eligibility contribution threshold, and a 58-year-old male too late-starting to ever qualify under either path) run against a standalone Node reproduction of the exact new projection formula before touching the HTML — all 4 matched by hand. Re-verified live via CDP against the pushed production page with the identical 4 scenarios (including a live gender-toggle click for the female case) — all matched exactly, 0 CSP violations, 0 JS errors.

**Also added, matching the pattern just shipped for `jo/calculators/social-security/`**: an `exportPensionResult()` function + `#pExportBtn` using the shared `exportResultImage()` canvas utility — rows show the entered near-retirement wage plus whichever of the early/normal results actually rendered (the normal-retirement row is the highlighted one when both are shown, since it's the guaranteed unreduced figure; the early row is highlighted only when it's the sole scenario shown).

## Loan calculator — compound/fixed interest mode toggle, built 2026-09-20

Per explicit user request ("حاسبة القسط الشهري لازم في اوبشن فائدة مركبة وثابتة"), `loan-calculator.html` (and all 5 lang variants via `build-lang-pages.js`) gained an `.option-toggle`/`.option-btn` pair (`data-mode="compound"`/`"flat"`, matching the site's established binary-choice UI pattern e.g. diet-plan.html's gender toggle) — **compound** is the pre-existing amortization/reducing-balance formula (unchanged, still the default), **flat** is new: `interest = principal × annualRate/100 × years` computed once, `monthly = (principal + interest) / months`. `buildAmortTable()` now takes a `mode` param — compound mode is unchanged (reducing-balance loop), flat mode builds a simplified equal-split table (constant principal = amount/months, constant interest = totalInterest/months per row) with a dedicated `#flat-table-note` disclosure explaining it's simplified vs. real bank schedules. `exportLoanResult()` includes the selected mode as a row. All new `LOAN_T` keys (`lbl_mode`/`mode_compound`/`mode_flat`/`mode_note`/`flat_table_note`) added ar/en/fr/es/de/ru. Verified via CDP against a local Fetch-served copy: compound (5000 @ 5%/3y → 149.85/mo, 394.76 total interest) and flat (159.72/mo, 750.00 total interest, first-row 138.89 principal/20.83 interest, last-row 0.00 balance) both matched independent hand-calculation exactly; switching modes back and forth correctly rebuilds the table/chart and toggles the flat-note visibility; Arabic translations verified. Pushed via a targeted 6-file push (root + 5 lang variants), verified live via blob-sha diff, `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean afterward (confirms the pre-existing default-compound behavior is unaffected).

## GSC "Duplicate, Google chose different canonical than user" — investigated 2026-09-20, real cause identified, not a code bug

User received a GSC validation-failed notification for this issue (validation started 9/8/26, failed 9/19/26). Investigated live via Search Console rather than assuming either "already fixed" or "something's broken": **Page indexing → Pages** report showed 2 affected URLs (`https://adawati.space/` and `https://adawati.space/percentage-calculator.html`), both failing validation. URL Inspection on both showed the same root cause: **Google-selected canonical is the raw GitHub Pages URL** (`https://adawati2027.github.io/my-tools-site/` and `.../percentage-calculator.html` respectively) instead of the user-declared `adawati.space` canonical — a classic GitHub Pages + custom-domain duplicate-content issue, where Google indexed the raw `github.io` origin (likely before the CNAME/redirect was fully in place or respected) and hasn't yet reconciled its canonical choice to the custom domain.

**Confirmed the underlying fix is already genuinely in place, live, right now** — this is NOT a currently-broken redirect: `curl -I` against both raw github.io URLs returns a real `301 Moved Permanently` to the correct `adawati.space` URL, and the `CNAME` file at the repo root is clean (`adawati.space`, no extra whitespace/lines). This directly reconfirms the existing CLAUDE.md note ("CNAME on GitHub Pages auto-redirects... with a real 301 — already verified working, don't re-verify") — still true. **So this is a Google-side signal-reconciliation lag, not a live technical bug to fix in code.** Google's automated validation pass (which ran 9/8→9/19) evidently still found/preferred the old github.io canonical at that time; a fresh **Live Test** in URL Inspection on both URLs came back clean ("URL is available to Google", "Page can be indexed", 0 issues) — the duplicate-canonical determination only happens post-indexing (Google's own UI says so: "not available in the live test... Google selects a canonical URL only after a page is indexed"), so a clean live test doesn't contradict the report.

**Action taken**: clicked "Request Indexing" on both affected URLs (added to Google's priority crawl queue) — this is the standard lever for nudging Google to re-crawl and re-evaluate canonical choice sooner than its normal cycle, rather than waiting for the next automated validation pass. **Not a guaranteed fix** — Google can still take one or more cycles to fully drop the old github.io canonical preference. **If this resurfaces or validation fails again**: don't re-investigate the redirect/CNAME from scratch (both reconfirmed solid 2026-09-20) — instead (a) re-run Live Test + Request Indexing on whichever URLs are flagged, (b) consider whether new pages are affected (check GSC's "Duplicate, Google chose different canonical than user" row under Page Indexing → Pages for the current affected-page list, it may grow beyond these 2), and (c) if it persists across several cycles, this may warrant asking the user whether `robots.txt` should explicitly disallow crawling `adawati2027.github.io` (not currently done — GitHub Pages doesn't let you customize robots.txt for the raw `github.io` subdomain per-repo the way you can for the custom domain, so this may not even be actionable; not investigated further this session since the redirect-based fix is the standard, correct approach and just needs time).

## AdSense "replicated content" fix — original commentary added to all 30 daily-info pages, 2026-09-20

User was about to click "Request review" on the AdSense rejection notice (see the earlier "Google AdSense rejection" investigation) without having actually fixed the two flagged issues — asked for a gut-check first rather than just clicking. Confirmed via a fresh `<h2>`-count-style check: **all 30 daily-info pages** (`{gold-price,weather,prayer-times,fuel-prices,holidays}` × `{jo,om,sa,ae,us,uk}`) had **zero** genuine long-form content sections — exactly the thin API-data-wrapper pattern AdSense's "Google-served ads on screens with replicated content" flag describes. Recommended NOT submitting for review until this was addressed, then built the fix per the user's "Yes after finish indexing" follow-up.

**Fix**: added one `💡`-titled original-commentary card (2 real paragraphs, general practical/educational content — not a new regulatory or numeric claim, so no fresh WebSearch verification needed, matching the same low-risk-content bar already established for the "💡 نصائح" cards on Jordan calculators) to every one of the 30 pages:
- **Gold price**: what moves gold prices globally (USD strength, inflation/rate expectations, central-bank buying, geopolitics), what karat actually means, and practical buying tips (compare making charges, ask for a receipt with karat/weight, 24k bars/coins for investment vs. jewelry for wear).
- **Weather**: forecast accuracy degrading further out, what "feels like" temperature actually measures, and a country-adapted planning tip (desert day/night swing for jo/om/sa/ae, regional diversity for us, changeability for uk).
- **Prayer times**: how the astronomical calculation works and why the specific calculation method differs by country (correctly named per what the site's own API calls already use — Egyptian General Authority for jo, Gulf Region for om/ae, Umm al-Qura for sa, ISNA for us, Muslim World League for uk — describing an already-implemented choice, not asserting a new fact), plus why times shift daily and why a local mosque's announced time might differ by a couple of minutes.
- **Fuel prices**: pump price generally reflects global crude + exchange rate + local tax/subsidy mechanisms (deliberately generic — no specific country's review schedule asserted, since that would need real verification), plus concrete driving/maintenance tips to cut consumption.
- **Holidays**: fixed-Gregorian vs. lunar-Hijri holiday mechanics (why Islamic dates shift ~11 days earlier each year, and why they're estimates until an official moon-sighting confirms them — consensus-safe framing, no fiqh position asserted), plus practical planning tips (banks/government offices closed, book ahead of Eid periods).

**Insertion mechanics**: gold-price/prayer-times/fuel-prices/holidays got the card inserted right before their existing FAQ card (verified as a byte-for-byte-identical, unique anchor across all 24 of those files first); weather (which has no FAQ card) got it inserted right before the "other tools" links block, after the existing "Data Source" card (also verified unique). `om/sa/ae/us/uk`'s `fuel-prices`/`holidays` pages have their own in-page `toggleLang()` EN/AR system — the new card was added as a **static block in the page's default language only** (same non-toggled pattern already used, unremarked, by that same page's own FAQ section — confirmed by reading `applyLang()`, which never touches the FAQ card's text), not wired into the toggle, to avoid the extra engineering complexity for a feature-completeness gap that doesn't block the actual AdSense fix.

**Verification**: `new Function()` + div-balance + `JSON.parse` on JSON-LD across all 30 files (0 errors), a live CDP functional test against local copies confirming the card renders correctly with the right text on 5 sample pages spanning every module type and both languages, pushed as a single 30-file batch (no rate-limit issues this time), verified live via blob-sha tree-diff (0 mismatches) and direct `curl` spot-checks on 6 more pages. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean afterward.

**Still not done — the "low value content" flag** is a separate issue (site traffic/maturity, not a content-depth problem) and wasn't addressed by this fix; don't assume clicking "Request review" is now safe just because this specific flag is resolved — recommend checking recent Search Console Performance data for real organic-traffic growth before resubmitting, and let the user make that call explicitly (this session flagged the risk but did not tell them to submit).

## Age calculator — Hijri date + top-3 historical events added to export image, 2026-09-21

Per the original user request ("حاسبة العمر بدي نضيف التاريخ الهجري بمشارك الصورة وتعطي اهم ٣ احداث صارت في مثل هاذ اليوم وكمان تضيفه ب مشاركة الصورة"), `age-calculator.html` gained two things:

1. **Hijri birthdate + Hijri age added to `exportAgeResult()`'s rows** — these were already computed and shown on-page (`#hijriBox`) but were never included in the shareable export image; a real, previously-undiscovered gap. Fixed by storing `hBirth`/`hijriAgeStr` as module-level vars (`lastHijriBirthStr`/`lastHijriAgeStr`) inside `calcAge()` and pushing them as rows.

2. **"Top 3 historical events on your birthday" (new feature, didn't exist before)** — interpreted "مثل هاذ اليوم" as the person's birth month/day (not today's date), since that's the framing that actually fits an age calculator and pairs naturally with the Hijri-birthdate feature in the same request. Sourced live from **Wikipedia's own "on this day" REST API**, specifically the `/onthisday/selected/{MM}/{DD}` endpoint — Wikipedia's own editorially-curated "selected anniversaries" feed (the same data used on Wikipedia's own Main Page), not the noisier chronological `/onthisday/events/` endpoint, which returns much lower-signal entries. Confirmed via direct testing that `selected` is meaningfully more notable/curated than `events`. Takes the first 3 entries per language. Language follows the page's current UI language (ar/en/fr/es/de/ru all map directly to real Wikipedia language editions — confirmed all 6 have live `onthisday` data with real CORS support), falling back to `en` for any unsupported case.

**Real architectural finding while building this: the site's CSP `connect-src` does not allow `*.wikipedia.org`, and — per the already-documented duplicate-Transform-Rule situation (see CSP-hardening section above) — adding it directly to the Worker's policy would NOT have been enough, since the old un-editable Transform Rule's own connect-src would also need it and CSP directives combine with AND semantics.** Sidestepped this exactly the way the gold-price fallback did: added a new `/api/onthisday?lang=X&month=M&day=D` route to the **existing `adawati-csp-nonce` Cloudflare Worker** (server-side Worker→Wikipedia fetches aren't CSP-governed at all), which fetches the real Wikipedia endpoint, strips MediaWiki template-leak artifacts from the text (a real bug found in raw API output — some entries contain literal leaked CSS like `.mw-parser-output .m{...}` from broken wikitext-to-plaintext conversion; a `/\{[^{}]*\}/g` strip handles it), caches the result for 24h via `caches.default` (much longer than the gold-fallback's 60s, since "on this day" content for a given calendar date is stable all day), and returns clean same-origin JSON. The page's `fetchBirthdayEvents()` calls this same-origin endpoint — zero CSP changes needed on the site side.

**Recovering the live Worker source before editing it**: this session had no local copy of the currently-deployed `worker.js` (each prior Worker change in this project's history was written fresh in a since-discarded scratchpad, per the established pattern). Rather than reconstruct it from memory of CLAUDE.md's own description (risky — could silently drop the gold-fallback handler or a CSP directive), pulled the **actual live source directly from Cloudflare's API** (`GET /accounts/{id}/workers/scripts/adawati-csp-nonce` using the existing `wrangler login` OAuth token read straight from `%APPDATA%\xdg.config\.wrangler\config\default.toml`) — confirmed byte-for-byte what's actually running before adding the new route. **If this Worker needs editing again in a future session with no local source file, pull the live script this same way first** rather than rewriting from documentation.

**Deployed via `wrangler deploy`** (CLI, not the dashboard editor — same established reason as every other Worker change in this project), using a `wrangler.toml` matching the live script's own `compatibility_date` (2026-09-14) read from `GET .../workers/scripts/adawati-csp-nonce/settings` first, so the redeploy wouldn't accidentally shift it. Verified immediately after deploy that the production Route was unaffected (`X-CSP-Nonce-Worker: enforced` still present on the homepage) before testing the new endpoint — a Worker redeploy via `wrangler deploy` with no `routes` in `wrangler.toml` does NOT touch the zone Route binding, confirmed empirically, not just assumed.

**Testing**: used the established Fetch-domain CDP interception technique (serve the page from local disk while letting the browser's *own* relative-URL requests — `/api/onthisday` — hit the real live Worker) since this feature is meaningless to test purely locally. Confirmed end-to-end on the real production endpoint: English mode showed 3 real, correctly-cleaned Wikipedia events; Arabic mode (after a live language switch) re-fetched and showed different, real Arabic-Wikipedia events; the export-image capture showed all of Hijri birthdate, Hijri age, and all 3 events present as rows. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) both re-run clean afterward — confirms the Worker change didn't regress anything else routed through it (which is everything on the site, given it's the CSP-nonce Worker in front of every page).

**Gotcha hit while re-running the regression suite this session, worth remembering**: `test-e2e.js` needs headless Chrome on **port 9750**, not the 9700 used by `test-calculators.js` (and by the earlier ad-hoc CDP scripts throughout this session) — confirmed via `grep CDP_PORT test-e2e.js`. Launching Chrome on the wrong port produces a generic, unhelpful `TEST RUNNER ERROR:` with no message (an `AggregateError [ECONNREFUSED]` whose `.message` happens to be empty) — don't assume this means a real regression; check `CDP_PORT` in the specific test file before concluding anything is broken, and don't reuse one test's Chrome instance/port for the other.

## QR generator — real bug found and fixed, 2026-09-21 (user-reported: failed even on a single character)

User reported via screenshot: generating a QR code for `https://adawati.space` (and separately confirmed even a single character) failed with "❌ تعذّر توليد الرمز — النص طويل جداً، جرّب نصاً أقصر" (text too long). **This message was always a lie** — investigation found the `catch` block in `generateQR()` unconditionally showed the "too long" message for *any* thrown error, regardless of actual cause, ever since the page was built.

**Root cause investigation**: could not reproduce via headless Chrome CDP (character-by-character typing simulation and direct `new QRCode(...)` calls both succeeded cleanly against the live production page) — this is the same "reproduces fine in automation, fails on a real device" pattern already documented elsewhere in this file for the mobile Safari popup-timing bug, and the lesson from that section applies again: don't conclude the code must be fine just because CDP-driven Chrome can't trigger it. Downloaded and inspected the actual `qrcodejs@1.0.0` library source (from cdnjs, confirmed reachable and CSP-allowed) — it uses `typeNumber:4` and, critically, its `Canvas`-based drawer calls `canvas.getContext('2d')` **synchronously during construction**, with no null-check on the returned context. **This is the classic failure signature of a privacy/fingerprinting-protection browser feature** (common in privacy-focused mobile browsers and some ad-blocker "Canvas Blocker" features) that poisons or nulls `getContext('2d')` to prevent canvas-fingerprinting — `new QRCode(...)` would then throw a `TypeError` reading a property off `null` immediately, regardless of input text length, exactly matching "fails even on a single character" and being invisible to any headless-Chrome-based test (which has no such privacy extension).

**Fixed**: (1) added an explicit Canvas-availability pre-check (`document.createElement('canvas').getContext('2d')`, verifying it returns a real object with a `fillRect` method) *before* attempting `new QRCode(...)` — if it fails, shows an honest, actionable message ("your browser is blocking QR image generation, likely due to a privacy/fingerprint-protection setting — try disabling it for this site or use a different browser") instead of the misleading length claim; (2) the remaining generic `catch` block now only shows the "too long" message when `text.length > 300` (a real, generous threshold) and otherwise shows a neutral retry message — no longer lies about the cause; (3) added a 180ms debounce (`generateQRDebounced`) on the input-typing listener (previously fired `generateQR()` on *every* keystroke, tearing down and rebuilding the QRCode instance in rapid succession) as a secondary defensive measure, since rapid re-invocation of a Canvas-based drawer is also a plausible (if unconfirmed) trigger for a decade-old library not designed for that usage pattern — the explicit click button and paste handler still fire promptly.

**Verified via CDP**: normal generation still works correctly (confirmed a real `<canvas>` element renders); a simulated Canvas-blocked browser (`HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.getContext` overridden to return `null`, replicating the hypothesized real cause) now correctly shows the new accurate message instead of silently failing or lying about text length. **Could not verify the fix resolves the user's exact real-device failure** (same limitation as always with this class of bug) — ask the user to retest on the same device/browser that showed the original error.

## i18n.js split into per-language packs, lazy-loaded — built 2026-09-21

Deferred idea from the 2026-09-16 audit (`i18n.js` was ~372KB/3,148 lines, loaded in full on every page regardless of which of the 6 languages a visitor actually needed) — picked up per explicit user request. Split the giant `const T = {ar:{...}, en:{...}, ...}` object (lines 12-1365, the bulk of the file's byte weight) into 6 standalone files — `i18n-lang-ar.js`/`en`/`fr`/`es`/`de`/`ru.js`, each doing `window.T = window.T || {}; window.T.<code> = {...};`. Core `i18n.js` dropped from ~375KB to ~131KB (all the actual logic — auth, favorites, dark mode, export-image, PWA, etc. — which every page needs regardless of language, so it stays in the shared core).

**Lazy-load mechanism, zero page-template changes required for correctness** (a key design win — this did NOT need touching all 300+ pages to work correctly, only `i18n.js` itself): `loadLangPack(lang)` dynamically injects `<script src="{I18N_BASE}i18n-lang-{lang}.js">` and returns a Promise; `I18N_BASE` is derived once at parse time from `document.currentScript.src` (stripping the trailing `i18n.js` filename) — this correctly resolves relative to however that specific page loaded `i18n.js` (bare `i18n.js`, `../i18n.js`, or an absolute base-href-resolved URL), no per-page-type special-casing needed. `setLang(lang)` — called both by the main init sequence and by every page's own dropdown-click wiring (`setLang(a.dataset.lang)`, present verbatim in ~300 pages' own inline scripts) — now self-defers: `if (!T[lang]) { loadLangPack(lang).then(() => setLang(lang)); return; }`, then re-invokes itself once loaded. Since the function name/signature is unchanged, none of those ~300 pages' own wiring code needed editing.

**A whole-init-sequence correctness fix was needed, not just `setLang()` itself**: several other `DOMContentLoaded`-time functions (`renderFAQ`, `updateAuthBtn`, `injectStarBtns`) also read `T[lang]` directly, not just `setLang()`'s own body — if only `setLang()` were made lazy-aware, these would run against an empty `T` on first page load (since no page ships a pack synchronously) and either throw or silently render nothing. Fixed by wrapping the **entire** original `DOMContentLoaded` init sequence into `_initAfterLangReady()`, and only calling it once `T[detectDefaultLang()]` is confirmed loaded (loading it first if not) — this protects every T-dependent init function uniformly rather than patching each one individually.

**Real bug found and fixed during the split, would have broken production if shipped as-is**: a *separate*, scattered block of 6 lines (`T.en.pct_seo_html = '...'; T.ar.pct_seo_html = '...'; ...`) existed **after** the main `const T = {...}` object closed — added by an earlier session for percentage-calculator's SEO content, relying on `T.en`/`T.ar`/etc. already being fully populated synchronously (true in the original file, since the whole `const T` literal ran as one atomic block). After the split, `T.en` doesn't exist yet at that point in the file — `T.en.pct_seo_html = ...` threw `TypeError: Cannot set properties of undefined`. **Caught via local CDP testing before pushing** (an `exceptionThrown` listener during a scripted page load, not a syntax check — `node --check` alone would never catch this since it's valid JS, just a runtime-order assumption). Fixed by moving each of those 6 lines into its matching `i18n-lang-<code>.js` file (appended after that file's own `window.T.<code> = {...}` assignment, so the target object already exists by the time the line runs). **Lesson if this pattern is ever extended: grep the whole file for `T\.\w+\.\w+\s*=` (bare `T.<lang>.<key> = ...` assignments, not `T[lang]` reads) before trusting a single-boundary extraction — any such assignment sitting outside the main object literal needs to move into its matching lang-pack file too.** Confirmed via the same grep that no other such assignment exists anywhere else in the file, and confirmed (via a separate grep across all ~300 HTML files' own inline scripts) that none of them use bare `T.ar.`/`T.en.` etc. either — the established, safe, already-documented pattern used by every page (`tip-calculator.html`, `currency-converter.html`, etc.) is exclusively the defensive `(typeof T!=='undefined' && T[lang]) ? T[lang] : {}` runtime read, which is safe under this lazy-load model since it only ever executes on user interaction (e.g. an export-image button click), by which point the active language's pack is guaranteed loaded by the init sequence above.

**`build-lang-pages.js` compatibility verified, not assumed**: this script evaluates the whole of `i18n.js` in a Node `vm` sandbox (with mocked `document`/`window`) to extract `PAGE_META` at build time (see the i18n Auth section elsewhere in this file for why `loadFirebaseAuth()` must stay function-scoped for the same reason). The sandbox's `document` stub has no `currentScript` property at all — confirmed this resolves to `undefined` safely (my own `I18N_BASE` IIFE already wraps the read in try/catch and falls back to `''`), so the vm-eval doesn't throw and `PAGE_META` extraction is unaffected — re-ran `build-lang-pages.js` after the split and confirmed identical output (same 30 tools list) before and after.

**`sw.js` updated**: added `/i18n-lang-(ar|en|fr|es|de|ru)\.js$/` to the same network-first `isCore` bucket as `i18n.js`/`style.css`/`quiz-engine.js`/`search-index.json` (bumped `CACHE` to `adawati-v63`) — these are exactly the "actively edited shared JS" class of file the cache-staleness lesson elsewhere in this file already warns about; cache-first would risk shipping a stale lang pack indefinitely to a returning visitor. Also added `i18n-lang-en.js` and `i18n-lang-ar.js` to the `STATIC` precache list (the homepage's default + the majority-default for Arabic-first country hubs) — a deliberate, reasonable baseline for the offline-PWA scenario, not exhaustive: an offline-first-ever-visit in a language other than en/ar (e.g. a French visitor who's never been online on this site before) won't have a pack cached yet, same class of limitation any non-precached asset already has.

**Verified live against production** (not just locally) — 4 page types (global homepage, salary-calculator, om hub, jo hub) all correctly loaded only their own needed language pack (`loadedLangs` in each case was a single-element array matching the page's actual language), zero JS exceptions. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29, after one test fix — see below) both re-run clean against live production.

**One real, expected test-suite update needed, not a site bug**: `test-e2e.js`'s "switching to Arabic" check clicked the language-menu Arabic option and read `document.documentElement.lang` in the *same synchronous eval*, with zero wait — valid when `setLang()` was always fully synchronous, but a switch to a non-default language now genuinely triggers a network fetch (`loadLangPack`) before applying. Fixed by splitting that test into two evals with a `s.wait(1500)` between the click and the read, matching how a real user's browser actually behaves (sees the correct language within one fetch round-trip, not instantly) — this is the test catching up to an intentional behavior change, not evidence of a regression.

**Current state — a real, live, verified win already, but not yet the fully-optimized end state**: every page now downloads only ONE language pack instead of all 6 (e.g., an English visitor gets ~131KB core + ~52KB en pack ≈ 183KB vs the old 375KB single file; a French/German/Russian/Spanish visitor gets an even bigger cut, ~131KB + ~25-35KB ≈ 156-166KB). **But since no page yet ships its own default-language pack as a static `<script>` tag, every single page load currently takes the "cold" lazy-fetch path** (`i18n.js` loads → `detectDefaultLang()` runs → `T[lang]` missing → dynamically fetch the pack → apply) — this adds one extra *sequential* network round-trip to the translation-rendering path on every page, which a same-directory static `<script src="i18n-lang-{default}.js" defer>` tag (loaded in parallel with `i18n.js` itself, before it in document order) would eliminate for the common case (visitor viewing a page in its own natural default language, which is the large majority of traffic). **This is the natural next phase, deliberately not attempted in this same session**: it requires editing all ~300 HTML files (root tool pages + `build-lang-pages.js`-generated lang variants + country hubs + country-module pages + nationality-landing pages — each needs the correct per-page-type default language determined and a correctly-relative-pathed tag inserted before its existing `i18n.js` tag), which is exactly the kind of large, many-file, rate-limit-prone batch rollout this project always stages carefully (matching the onclick-refactor and OG-image projects' own multi-batch discipline) rather than attempting in one shot. **If picked up**: default language per page-type is already fully known (lang-variant folders `/ar//fr//es//de//ru/` = that language; `jo/om/sa` + the 5 nationality-landing pages = ar; `ae/us/uk` = en; bare root pages = en — matches the already-established `localStorage.setItem` default-language convention documented in the cross-hub mixed-language-bug sections above), so the only real engineering work is a batch script inserting the correctly-relative-pathed static tag per file family, then the usual validate → functionally test → push-in-batches → blob-sha-verify cycle.

## i18n.js split, Phase 2 — static per-page pack tags rolled out site-wide, 2026-09-21

Completed the same day as the Phase 1 core split above, per explicit user follow-up ("كمل"). This closes the gap Phase 1 deliberately left open: every page now ships its own default-language pack as a static `<script defer>` tag placed immediately before `i18n.js`, so the common case (a visitor viewing a page in its own natural default language — the large majority of traffic) loads translations **synchronously in parallel** with `i18n.js` itself, with **zero dynamic fetch** — the lazy-load mechanism built in Phase 1 now only actually engages when a visitor's stored language choice differs from a given page's own default (e.g. an Arabic-preferring visitor on an English-default root tool page).

**30 `build-lang-pages.js` `TOOLS` root files**: all confirmed `<html lang="en">` (verified via a direct grep sweep, not assumed), so all 30 got a hardcoded `<script src="i18n-lang-en.js?v=1" defer></script>` inserted before their existing `i18n.js` tag via a small batch script.

**`build-lang-pages.js` itself updated** with a new substitution step (`g.`) that rewrites `i18n-lang-en.js` → `i18n-lang-{code}.js` per generated variant — since the root file's body (now carrying the hardcoded 'en' pack tag) gets copied verbatim into each of the 5 lang-variant files, without this substitution every `/ar//fr//es//de//ru/` variant would have silently shipped the wrong (English) static pack. Re-ran `build-lang-pages.js` and confirmed via spot-check that `ar/age-calculator/index.html` correctly got `i18n-lang-ar.js`, `fr/age-calculator/index.html` got `i18n-lang-fr.js`, etc. — all 150 variants regenerated correctly in one pass.

**114 hand-maintained pages** (country hubs, country-module pages under `{om,sa,ae,us,uk,jo}/calculators/`+daily-info folders, the 5 nationality-landing pages, 9 `kids-*.html` pages, `oman-*-guide.html`, `omr-to-*.html`, `about/contact/privacy/terms.html`, `index.html`) — a generic batch script determined each file's target language by reading its own `<html lang="XX">` attribute directly (ground truth, not inferred from path — this correctly handled a case that would have been wrong by path-guessing alone: `eg/index.html` turned out to be `lang="en"` by default, not `ar` as a naive "nationality pages are Arabic" assumption would have guessed) and inserted the pack tag using the **exact same relative path prefix** as that file's own existing `i18n.js` reference (bare `i18n.js`, `../i18n.js`, etc., regardless of nesting depth or `<base href>` presence) — this correctly handles every path convention already in use across the site without needing per-directory-depth special-casing, since `I18N_BASE` (added in Phase 1) is itself already derived from wherever `i18n.js` actually loaded from.

**Explicitly excluded, unchanged**: the 15 `jo/games/*` + `wordle/` pages, which don't load `i18n.js` at all (by design, see the "In-page English translation system" section above) — confirmed 16 files (not 15) correctly fell into the "no i18n.js" bucket during the initial site-wide scan (the 16th being `wordle/index.html`).

**Verification, in order**: (1) syntax/div-balance/JSON-LD validation across all 114 hand-maintained files (0 errors) plus the earlier 30+150 TOOLS-family files (0 errors); (2) local CDP test confirming the fast path actually engages — network-request logging showed exactly 2 i18n-related requests per page load (the static pack + `i18n.js` itself, both declared statically, zero runtime-injected `<script>` elements) on both a root tool page and a `kids-*` page; (3) pushed all 295 files (30 modified TOOLS root files + 150 regenerated lang variants + 114 hand-maintained files + `build-lang-pages.js`) in a single batched push — the largest this project has done (previous largest was 452 files for the OG-image project) — using the established batched-blob-with-retry pattern (500ms pacing, up to 5 retries per blob, one combined tree+commit at the end); **0 blob failures across all 295 files**, no GitHub secondary-rate-limit issues hit this time; (4) verified live via the full blob-sha tree-diff script — **0 mismatches across all 295 files**; (5) re-ran the exact same network-request-logging CDP test against **live production** on 9 diverse page types (global homepage, a root tool, `om/index.html`, `jo/index.html`, a Saudi country-module page, a currency-landing page, a kids page, an Oman guide page, and `eg/index.html`) — every single one showed exactly 2 static i18n-related requests, zero dynamic fetches, zero JS exceptions, correctly matching each page's own actual default language (including confirming `eg/index.html` really is `lang="en"` by default, validating the "read `<html lang>` directly rather than guess from path" design choice); (6) all 305 live sitemap URLs re-checked via `curl`, 0 failures; (7) `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) both re-run clean against live production.

**This closes the i18n.js split project end-to-end** — both phases (core split + lazy-load mechanism, then site-wide static pack tags for the zero-extra-round-trip fast path) are now live, verified, and documented. Real numbers: an English visitor on a typical page now downloads ~131KB (core) + ~52KB (en pack) ≈ 183KB instead of the original single 375KB file; a French/German/Russian/Spanish visitor gets an even bigger cut, ~131KB + ~25-35KB ≈ 156-166KB — all with zero added network round-trips versus the pre-split single-file behavior, since both the core and the matching pack now load in parallel via `defer`, in document order.

**If a new page is added in the future**: give it a `<script src="{same-prefix-as-i18n.js}i18n-lang-{its-default-lang}.js?v=1" defer></script>` tag immediately before its `i18n.js` tag, matching its own `<html lang>` attribute — skip this only for pages that don't load `i18n.js` at all (the `jo/games/*` family). If it's a new `build-lang-pages.js` `TOOLS` entry, the root file's tag should hardcode `i18n-lang-en.js` (matching every other root tool's own default) and the existing substitution step in `build-lang-pages.js` will automatically retarget it correctly for each generated variant — no further changes needed there.

## exportResultImage — long row values now wrap instead of overflowing, 2026-09-21

User-reported via two real screenshots of age-calculator's export image: the historical-events rows (built earlier this session — see "Age calculator — Hijri date + top-3 historical events added to export image") rendered with the event's year and its full sentence text visibly overlapping/running off the card edge. Root cause: `exportResultImage()`'s row renderer (`fillRowText`) does a single unwrapped `ctx.fillText()` call per label/value — fine for every other calculator's genuinely short values ("70kg", "5%", "149.85"), but a full sentence (e.g. "انعقاد مؤتمر الطائف بالسعودية لحل مشكلة الحرب الأهلية اللبنانية.") rendered at the normal value font size is far wider than the card, so it overflowed past its anchor edge far enough to overlap the label positioned at the opposite edge — this was a real, pre-existing gap in the shared function (short label/value pairs were the only case anyone had ever exercised it with before), not a bug specific to the events feature's own code.

**Fixed generically in `exportResultImage()` itself**, not per-calculator, so every current and future user of the function benefits: a two-pass measurement (a throwaway canvas measures each row's value against a width threshold — matching the same "measure before creating the real canvas" pattern already established for the quiz-result-sharing feature's caption wrapping) decides per-row whether the value fits the normal two-column layout or needs to switch to a stacked layout (label as a small heading, value word-wrapped across multiple lines below it, both anchored to the same edge instead of opposing edges) — and the canvas height calculation (already row-count-driven, not fixed) now accounts for wrapped rows needing more vertical space. Short rows render through the exact same code path as before, unaffected.

**Verified**: a local test built the exact scenario from the user's screenshots (Arabic mode, 3 long event rows mixed with short date/age rows) — all 3 events now wrap cleanly with zero overlap. A regression check on `loan-calculator.html` (all short values, plus one moderately-long "Interest Type: Compound (Reducing Balance)" value that also now correctly stacks/wraps instead of whatever overflow risk it may have silently had before) confirmed the normal short-row layout is visually unaffected. Re-verified live against production `age-calculator.html` with a real fetched set of events (different text than the local test, since events are live-fetched from Wikipedia) — same clean result, 0 JS exceptions. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) and `test-e2e.js` (29/29) re-run clean afterward.

## age-calculator export — final summary row label/value swapped, fixed 2026-09-21

Follow-up to the wrap fix above — user pointed at the bottom of the exported card looking "not organized." Root cause was a separate, real bug in `age-calculator.html`'s own `exportAgeResult()`, not the shared function: the final highlighted row was built as `{label: document.getElementById('ageSub').textContent, value: main, highlight:true}` — but `#ageSub` holds the small continuation phrase ("و 0 شهر و 0 يوم") and `main` holds the big number ("14 سنة"), so the export literally put the continuation half first (as the "label") and the number half second (as the "value"), reading backwards instead of as the single coherent on-page phrase "14 سنة و 0 شهر و 0 يوم".

**Fixed**: combined both pieces into one `value` string (`main + ' ' + ageSubText`) and added a proper new short label key `lbl_your_age` ("عمرك"/"Your Age"/etc., all 6 languages) instead of reusing `#ageSub`'s text as the label. Verified live on production (real fetched values, real events) — the row now renders as a clean heading ("عمرك") above the full wrapped phrase ("24 سنة و 0 شهر و 0 يوم"), matching the same stacked layout already used for the Hijri-age row right above it. Pushed as 6 files (root + 5 lang variants), verified live via blob-sha diff, `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean.

## Loan calculator — CSV/PDF export for the amortization schedule, built 2026-09-21

User requested export buttons for the full amortization table (`#amort-body`) on `loan-calculator.html`. Added two buttons (`#amortCsvBtn`/`#amortPdfBtn`) below the table.

**CSV**: pure client-side, no library — reads the already-rendered table rows via `getAmortRows()`, builds a properly-escaped (quoted fields containing commas, e.g. `"4,870.98"`) CSV string with a UTF-8 BOM prefix (so Excel opens Arabic headers/content correctly) and triggers download via `Blob` + `<a download>`.

**PDF — a real, reproducible jsPDF bug found and worked around, not a quick copy of the existing file-converter.html pattern.** The obvious approach (matching `file-converter.html`'s existing `xlsxToPdf()`/`csvToPdf()`, which build an off-screen `<div style="position:fixed;left:-9999px">` table and call jsPDF 2.5.1's own `doc.html(el, {..., autoPaging:'text'})` convenience method) produces a **PDF with real, correctly-encoded text content that renders completely blank** — confirmed by direct inspection of the PDF's raw content stream (not just visual testing, since Chrome's own PDF.js viewer doesn't reliably paint on CDP screenshots in headless automation either, which nearly caused a false read here). **Root cause, found by reading the actual PDF byte content**: `doc.html()` internally re-invokes `html2canvas` once per internal chunk for content taller than a single capture pass (independent of `autoPaging`, and independent of chunking rows into smaller groups — even a 20-row chunk triggered it, while a hand-verified 2-row chunk didn't) — and every chunk after the first ignores the source element's actual on-page position, producing content drawn at wildly negative coordinates (~-7472 PDF points, matching `-9999px × ~0.75pt/px`) that lands thousands of points off the visible page. The `html2canvas:{scrollX:0,scrollY:0}` option (a commonly-cited community fix for a related jsPDF/html2canvas coordinate issue) only fixed the *first* chunk, not subsequent ones — confirmed not a full fix by re-inspecting coordinates after applying it.

**Actual fix**: bypass jsPDF's `.html()` wrapper entirely. Call `html2canvas()` **directly** on the off-screen table div (proven reliable at any content height — visually verified rendering a full 36-row table correctly as a single 718×1040px canvas), then **slice the resulting canvas ourselves** into page-sized chunks (`Math.floor((297-20)mm × (718/190)px-per-mm)` ≈ 1047px per A4 page) via a scratch `<canvas>` + `ctx.drawImage(source, 0, y, w, sliceH, 0, 0, w, sliceH)`, and place each slice with `doc.addImage()` (adding `doc.addPage()` between slices) — a completely different jsPDF code path from `.html()` that has no coordinate-carryover bug, since each `addImage()` call gets an explicit, correct `x`/`y` we control directly.

**Second, real, independently-found problem while testing this: PNG output was enormous** — a 36-row single-page PDF was 2.9MB (all from one losslessly-compressed PNG image of a plain bordered table), and a 360-row/10-page test (30-year loan) produced a 37MB file before this was caught — clearly impractical to generate/download. **Fixed** by switching to JPEG at quality 0.85 (`canvas.toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.85)` + `doc.addImage(..., 'JPEG', ...)`) — cut the 36-row case to 146KB (20x smaller) and the 360-row/10-page case to 1.28MB, with no visible quality loss for this content type (solid-color borders and dark text on a white background, exactly what JPEG handles well).

**Verification, in order**: (1) syntax + div-balance validated; (2) local CDP test confirmed real CSV content (correct headers, correct escaping, correct computed values matching `test-calculators.js`'s own independently-derived 149.85/mo expected value for the same 5000/5%/3y inputs); (3) direct PDF byte-content inspection (not just visual) confirmed the buggy `.html()` coordinates, confirmed the `addImage()`-based rewrite produces sane positive on-page coordinates throughout; (4) visual verification via three extracted canvas slices from a real 30-year/360-row/10-page run (first page, a page-boundary-straddling middle page, and the last page) — page breaks land cleanly with no row split/duplicated across the boundary, Arabic RTL column order and text render correctly, final row 360 correctly shows a 0.00 remaining balance; (5) pushed as 6 files (root + 5 lang variants), verified live via blob-sha diff (0 mismatches); (6) **a real GitHub Pages build-lag + Cloudflare edge-propagation false alarm hit and resolved during production verification** — a fresh-profile CDP navigation to the bare live URL showed the new button missing (`hasBtn:false`) even though the git tree diff was clean; traced via `GET /repos/.../pages/builds/latest` showing `"status":"building"` for the exact pushed commit SHA, waited ~55s polling until `"status":"built"`, then a cache-busted (`?bust=<timestamp>`) navigation confirmed the button present and the full CSV+PDF flow (including a real `.click()` on `#amortPdfBtn`) working end-to-end with 0 JS exceptions — matches the already-documented "verify a fix with a cache-busted fetch, not a bare URL, immediately after a push" lesson elsewhere in this file, this time triggered by Pages build lag rather than Cloudflare's edge cache specifically. `test-calculators.js` (20/20) re-run clean afterward.

**If this canvas-slicing + addImage pattern is useful elsewhere**: it's a more robust general-purpose "long HTML table → paginated PDF" building block than jsPDF's own `.html()`/`autoPaging` for any table that might exceed roughly one page's worth of content — worth reaching for directly next time rather than re-discovering this same `.html()` bug. `file-converter.html`'s own `xlsxToPdf()`/`csvToPdf()` use the exact same `.html()`+`autoPaging:'text'` pattern that was just shown to be broken for tall content — **not fixed as part of this task** (out of scope, a different feature, would need its own dedicated verification pass), but flagged here since it's now a known, real, likely-still-present bug there too if a user ever converts a sufficiently long spreadsheet/CSV to PDF.

## Style/tone conventions

- All Jordan-vertical copy is in Jordanian-dialect Arabic (not MSA), casual and direct — match existing pages' voice, not formal Arabic.
- Every calculator with a legal/financial figure gets a visible "ليست رسمية / أداة تقديرية" disclaimer and a link to the actual official source (istd.gov.jo, customs.gov.jo, ssc.gov.jo, etc.).
- Don't add comments to code explaining what it does; this codebase has none and it should stay that way except where a genuinely non-obvious constraint needs recording (see existing sparse examples in the quiz JS for the bar this should clear).
